Existence and Multiplicity Results for Systems of Singular Boundary Value Problems
Naseer Ahmad Asif, PhD

Abstract




– Monograph –

\frontmatter

Preface

Singular boundary value problems (SBVPs) arise in various fields of Mathematics, Engineering and Physics such as boundary layer theory, gas dynamics, nuclear physics, nonlinear optics, etc. The present monograph is devoted to systems of SBVPs for ordinary differential equations (ODEs). It presents existence theory for a variety of problems having unbounded nonlinearities in regions where their solutions are searched for. The main focus is to establish the existence of positive solutions. The results are based on regularization and sequential procedure.

First chapter of this monograph describe the motivation for the study of SBVPs. It also include some available results from functional analysis and fixed point theory. The following chapters contain results from author’s PhD thesis, National University of Sciences and Technology, Islamabad, Pakistan. These results provide the existence of positive solutions for a variety of systems of SBVPs having singularity with respect to independent and/or dependent variables as well as with respect to the first derivatives of dependent variables.

\mainmatter

Chapter 1
Introduction and Preliminaries

Many problems in applied sciences are modeled by singular boundary value problems (SBVPs). For example, in the study of rotating flow
hallam
, in the theory of viscous fluids
callegarifiedman
, in the study of pseudoplastic fluids
callegari
;
nachman
, in boundary
layer theory
callegarinachman1
;
schlichting
;
vajravelu
;
wang
, the
theory of shallow membrane caps
baxrob
;
dickey1
;
johnson
, in
pre-breakdown of gas discharge
diekmann
, the turbulent flow
of a gas in a porous medium
esteban
, can be represented by
SBVPs. Further, many mathematical models of various applications from nuclear physics, plasma physics, nonlinear optics, fluid mechanics, chemical reactor theory, predator-prey interactions
blossey
;
farkas
;
marletts
;
turchin
;
zws
are systems of time dependent partial differential equations (PDEs) subject to initial and/or boundary conditions. In the investigation of stationary solutions, these models of systems of PDEs can be reduced to systems of SBVPs.

In the scenario of the above mentioned models of various phenomenon,
the theory of SBVPs has become much more important. In this
monograph, we present existence results for positive solutions to
various systems of BVPs for nonlinear ODEs. We provide sufficient
conditions for the existence of at least one and two solutions for the singular systems of nonlinear ODEs subject to various type of boundary conditions (BCs) both on finite and infinite domains. We use the classical tools of functional analysis including the fixed point theory and the theory of the fixed points index. The rest of this chapter is devoted to the basic study of these notions. In the following section, we present
some definitions and notions from functional analysis. Moreover, some famous fixed point results such as Schauder’s fixed point theorem and the
Guo-Krasnosel’skii fixed point theorem are also included,
amr
;
amann
;
Dk
;
Dj
;
fonseca
;
GL
;
HPC
.

1
Some basic definitions and known results

Definition 1.1

A subset
\Omega
of a Banach space
\mathcal{B}
is said to be compact if and only if every sequence
\{x_{n}\}\subset\Omega
has a convergent subsequence with limit in
\Omega
. Moreover,
\Omega
is relatively compact if
\overline{\Omega}
is compact.

Definition 1.2

Let
\Omega
be a subset of a Banach space

\mathcal{B}
. A map
T:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is compact if

T
maps every bounded subset of
\Omega
into a relatively compact
subset of
\mathcal{B}
. Moreover,
T
is completely continuous if

T
is continuous and compact.

Definition 1.3

A nonempty subset
K
of a Banach space

\mathcal{B}
is a retract of
\mathcal{B}
if there exist a
continuous map
r:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow K
, a retraction, such that

\left.r\right|_{K}=I_{K}
, where
I_{K}
is identity map on
K
.

Definition 1.4

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a real Banach space. A
nonempty, closed and convex set
P\subset\mathcal{B}
is said to be
a cone if the following axioms are satisfied:

(\mathbf{P_{1}})

\alpha\,x\in P
for all
x\in P
and
\alpha\geq 0
,

(\mathbf{P_{2}})

x,-x\in P
implies
x=0
.

Theorem 1.5

(Arzelà-Ascoli theorem)
Let
\Omega
be a compact
subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. A set
\mathfrak{M}
of continuous functions on
\Omega
is
relatively compact in
C(\Omega)
if and only if
\mathfrak{M}
is a family of uniformly bounded and equicontinuous functions.

Now we recall the notion of degree for continuous maps. The degree of a map in finite dimensional spaces is known as the Brouwer degree. Let
\Omega
be a bounded and open subset of a finite dimensional Banach space
(\mathbb{B},\|\cdot\|)
. Let
T_{0}:\overline{\Omega}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}
be a
C(\overline{\Omega})\cup C^{1}(\Omega)
map, and
S_{0}=\{x\in\Omega:J_{0}(x)=0\}
be the set of all critical points of the map
T_{0}
, where
J_{0}(x):=\det T_{0}^{\prime}(x)
is the Jacobean of
T_{0}
at
x
. If
y\notin T_{0}(\partial\Omega\cup S_{0})
, then the Brouwer degree is defined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{deg_{{}_{B}}}(T_{0},\Omega,y)=\sum_{x\in T_{0}^{-1}(y)}\operatorname{sgn}J_{0}(x),

which corresponds to the number of solutions of
T_{0}(x)=y
in
\Omega
. However, if
T_{0}\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2}(\Omega)
,
y\notin T_{0}(\partial\Omega)
and
y\in T_{0}(S_{0})
, then
\operatorname{deg_{{}_{B}}}(T_{0},\Omega,y)=\operatorname{deg_{{}_{B}}}(T_{0},\Omega,z)
, where
z\notin T_{0}(S_{0})
such that
\|z-y\|<\operatorname{dist}(y,T_{0}(\partial\Omega))
. Further, if
T_{0}\in C(\overline{\Omega})
and
y\notin T_{0}(\partial\Omega\cup S_{0})
, then
\operatorname{deg_{{}_{B}}}(T_{0},\Omega,y)=\operatorname{deg_{{}_{B}}}(T_{1},\Omega,y),
where
T_{1}\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2}(\Omega)
satisfies
\sup_{x\in\Omega}\|T_{0}(x)-T_{1}(x)\|<\operatorname{dist}(y,T_{0}(\partial\Omega))
.

In 1934, J. Leray and J. Schauder
JLJS
extended the notion of degree to infinite dimensional spaces. They proved that along with continuity some compactness condition for the map is required. However, this is more suitable for a map of the form
I-T
, where
I
is the identity map and
T
is a completely continuous map. For defining the Leray-Schauder degree, the following theorem
fonseca
is helpful to approximate a compact map with a finite-dimensional map.

Theorem 1.6

Assume that
\Omega
is an open and bounded subset of a real Banach space
(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|)
and
T:\overline{\Omega}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a completely continuous map. Then, for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist a
finite-dimensional space
\mathbb{B}
and a continuous map

T_{\varepsilon}:\overline{\Omega}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}
such that

\|T(x)-T_{\varepsilon}(x)\|<\varepsilon
for every

x\in\overline{\Omega}
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded and open subset of a Banach space

\mathcal{B}
. Let
T:\overline{\Omega}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
be a
completely continuous mapping and
y\notin(I-T)(\partial\Omega)
.
The Leray-Schauder degree of
I-T
over
\Omega
at point
y
is
defined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{deg_{{}_{LS}}}(I-T,\Omega,y)=\operatorname{deg_{{}_{B}}}(I-T_{\varepsilon},\Omega,y),

where
T_{\varepsilon}
is an approximation of
T
in a finite dimensional space such that
\|T(x)-T_{\varepsilon}(x)\|<\varepsilon:=\operatorname{dist}(y,(I-T)(\partial\Omega))
.
When
T:\overline{\Omega}\rightarrow K
be a completely continuous map
such that
0\notin(I-T)(\partial\Omega)
, where
K
is a retract of a Banach space
\mathcal{B}
and
\Omega
is an open subset of
K
, then for any retraction
r:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow K
the Leray-Schauder degree
\operatorname{deg_{{}_{LS}}}(I-T\circ r,r^{-1}(\Omega),0)
is known as the fixed point index of the map
T
over
\Omega
with respect to the retract
K
and is denoted by
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega,K)
. The following are the most significant properties of the fixed point index for completely continuous maps.

(\mathbf{F_{1}})

Normalization:
For every constant map
T
mapping
\overline{\Omega}
into
\Omega
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega,K)=1.

(\mathbf{F_{2}})

Additivity:
For any disjoint open subsets
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
of
\Omega
such that
T
has no fixed
point on
\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2})
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega,K)=\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{1},K)+\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{2},K).

(\mathbf{F_{3}})

Homotopy:
For every compact interval
[a,b]\subset\mathbb{R}
and every compact map

h:[a,b]\times\overline{\Omega}\rightarrow K
such that
h(\tau,x)\neq x
for
(\tau,x)\in[a,b]\times\partial\Omega
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(h(\tau,\cdot),\Omega,K)

is well defined and is independent of
\tau\in[a,b]
.

(\mathbf{F_{4}})

Solution:
If
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega,K)\neq 0
, then
T
has at least one fixed point in
\Omega
.

(\mathbf{F_{5}})

Permanence:
If
K_{1}
is a retract of
K
and
T(\overline{\Omega})\subset K_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega,K)=\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap K_{1},K_{1}).

(\mathbf{F_{6}})

Excision:
For every open set
\Omega_{1}\subset\Omega
such that
T
has no fixed point in

\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega,K)=\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{1},K).

Theorem 1.7

(Schauder’s fixed point theorem)
Let
X
be a nonempty,
closed, bounded and convex subset of a Banach space
\mathcal{B}

and
T:X\rightarrow X
be a completely continuous map. Then,
T
has
a fixed point in
X
.

Proof

For some
x_{*}\in X
, consider a map
h:[0,1]\times X\rightarrow X
defined by

\displaystyle h(\tau,x)=(1-\tau)x_{*}+\tau Tx.

Then, the conclusion follows from the homotopy property
(\mathbf{F_{3}})
together with the normalization property
(\mathbf{F_{1}})
.

Lemma 1.8

Let
\Omega
be an open and bounded set in a real Banach space

\mathcal{B}
,
P
be a cone of
\mathcal{B}
,
0\in\Omega
and

T:\overline{\Omega}\cap P\rightarrow P
be a completely continuous
map. Suppose
x\neq\lambda Tx
, for any
x\in\partial\Omega\cap P
,

\lambda\in(0,1]
. Then, the fixed point index
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)=1
.

Lemma 1.9

Let
\Omega
be an open and bounded set in a real Banach space

(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|)
,
P
be a cone of
\mathcal{B}
,

0\in\Omega
and
T:\overline{\Omega}\cap P\rightarrow P
be a
completely continuous map. Suppose there exist a
v\in P\setminus\{0\}
with
x\neq Tx+\delta v
for every
\delta>0
and

x\in\partial\Omega\cap P
. Then, the fixed point index

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)=0
.

Proof

Let
\mu=\sup\{\|Tx\|:x\in\Omega\cap P\}
and

\rho=\sup\{\|x\|:x\in\Omega\}
. Choose

\delta_{1}>(\mu+\rho)/\|v\|
and define a map

h:[0,1]\times(\overline{\Omega}\cap P)\rightarrow P
as

\displaystyle h(\tau,x)=T(x)+\tau\delta_{1}v.

Then, by the homotopy property
(\mathbf{F_{3}})
, we
obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)=\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T+\delta_{1}v,\Omega\cap P,P).

Now, if
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)\neq 0
, then there exist an element

x\in\Omega\cap P
such that
x=Tx+\delta_{1}v
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\|x\|=\|Tx+\delta_{1}v\|\geq\delta_{1}\|v\|-\|Tx\|\geq\delta_{1}\|v\|-\mu>\rho,

a contradiction. Hence,
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)=0
.

Lemma 1.10

Let
\Omega
be a bounded and open set in a real Banach space

(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|)
,
P
be a cone of
\mathcal{B}
,

0\in\Omega
and
T:\overline{\Omega}\cap P\rightarrow P
be a
completely continuous map. Suppose
Tx\npreceq x
, for any

x\in\partial\Omega\cap P
. Then, the fixed point index

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)=0
.

Proof

We claim that, for some
v\in P\setminus\{0\}
,
x\neq Tx+\delta v
for every
\delta>0
and
x\in\partial\Omega\cap P
. Suppose there exist some
\delta_{0}>0
and
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega\cap P
such that

x_{0}=Tx_{0}+\delta_{0}v
. Then,
x_{0}=Tx_{0}+\delta_{0}v\succ Tx_{0}
, a contradiction as
x_{0}
can not be mapped toward the origin under
T
. Hence, by Lemma
1.9
,
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega\cap P,P)=0
.

Theorem 1.11

(Guo-Krasnosel’skii fixed point theorem)
Let
P
be a
cone of a real Banach space
(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|)
. Let
\Omega_{1}
,

\Omega_{2}
be bounded and open neighborhoods of
0\in\mathcal{B}

such that
{\Omega}_{1}\subset\Omega_{2}
. Suppose that

T:(\overline{\Omega}_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1})\cap P\rightarrow P
is
completely continuous such that one of the following conditions
holds:

(i)

\|Tx\|\leq\|x\|
for
x\in\partial\Omega_{1}\cap P
,
\|Tx\|\geq\|x\|
for
x\in\partial\Omega_{2}\cap P
.

(ii)

\|Tx\|\leq\|x\|
for
x\in\partial\Omega_{2}\cap P
,
\|Tx\|\geq\|x\|
for
x\in\partial\Omega_{1}\cap P
.

Then,
T
has a fixed point in

(\overline{\Omega}_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1})\cap P
.

Proof

Assume that
(i)
holds. If
T
has a fixed point on
\partial\Omega_{1}\cup\partial\Omega_{2}
then proof is complete. Suppose
Tx\neq x
for all
x\in\partial\Omega_{1}\cup\partial\Omega_{2}
. First of all we show that
x\neq\lambda Tx

for
\lambda\in(0,1]
and
x\in\partial\Omega_{1}\cap P
. Suppose,

x_{1}=\lambda_{1}Tx_{1}
for some
\lambda_{1}\in(0,1)
and

x_{1}\in\partial\Omega_{1}\cap P
. Then,

\|x_{1}\|=\lambda_{1}\|Tx_{1}\|<\|Tx_{1}\|\leq\|x_{1}\|
, a
contradiction. Hence, by Lemma
1.8
, the fixed point
index
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{1}\cap P,P)=1
.

We claim that there exist a
v\in P\setminus\{0\}
with
x\neq Tx+\delta v
for every
\delta>0
and
x\in\partial\Omega_{2}\cap P
.
Suppose,
x_{2}=Tx_{2}+\delta_{2}v
for some
\delta_{2}>0
and

x_{2}\in\partial\Omega_{2}\cap P
. Then,

\|x_{2}\|=\|Tx_{2}+\delta_{2}v\|>\|Tx_{2}\|\geq\|x_{2}\|
, a
contradiction. Therefore, by Lemma
1.9
, the fixed point
index
\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{2}\cap P,P)=0
.

Thus, by the additivity property of fixed point index

(\mathbf{F_{2}})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,(\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1})\cap P,P)=\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{2}\cap P,P)-\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T,\Omega_{1}\cap P,P)=0-1=-1.

Thus,
T
has a fixed point in
(\overline{\Omega}_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1})\cap P
. The proof for
(ii)
is similar.

For each
x\in C[0,1]\cap C^{1}(0,1]
, we write

\|x\|=\max_{t\in[0,1]}|x(t)|
,
\|x\|_{1}=\sup_{t\in(0,1]}t|x^{\prime}(t)|
and
\|x\|_{2}=\max\{\|x\|,\|x\|_{1}\}
. Further, for each

x\in C^{1}[0,1]
, we write
\|x\|_{3}=\max\{\|x\|,\|x^{\prime}\|\}
. The following results are known
agarwaloregan
;
liuyan1
;
wj
;
xian1
;
YRA
;
YRA1
.

Lemma 1.12

(\mathcal{E},\|\cdot\|_{2})
is a Banach space.

Lemma 1.13

If
x\in\mathcal{E}
, then
|x^{\prime}(t)|\leq\frac{\|x\|_{2}}{t}
for all

t\in(0,1]
.

Lemma 1.14

If
x\in P:=\{x\in\mathcal{E}:x(t)\geq t\|x\|\text{ for all }t\in[0,1],\,x(1)\geq\|x\|_{1}\}
, then

\|x\|_{2}=\|x\|
.

Lemma 1.15

Let
\sigma\in C(0,1)
and
\sigma>0
on
(0,1)
with

\int_{0}^{1}\sigma(t)dt<+\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle t\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G(\tau,s)\sigma(s)ds\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)\sigma(s)ds\text{ for }t\in[0,1],

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau\int_{\tau}^{1}\sigma(s)ds\leq

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)\sigma(s)ds,

where

\displaystyle G(t,s)=\begin{cases}s,\,&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\\
t,\,&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1.\end{cases}

Lemma 1.16

If
x\in P:=\{x\in C^{1}[0,1]:x(t)\geq\gamma\|x\|\text{ for all }t\in[0,1],\,x(0)\geq\frac{b}{a}\|x^{\prime}\|\}
,
then
x(t)\geq\gamma\rho\|x\|_{3}
for all
t\in[0,1]
, where

\gamma=\frac{b}{a+b}
,
\varrho=\frac{1}{\max\{1,\frac{a}{b}\}}
,

a,b>0
.

Lemma 1.17

Let
\sigma\in C(0,1)
and
\sigma>0
on
(0,1)
with

\int_{0}^{1}\sigma(t)dt<+\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle\gamma\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G(\tau,s)\sigma(s)ds\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)\sigma(s)ds\text{ for }t\in[0,1],\,\gamma=\frac{b}{a+b},\,a,b>0,

\displaystyle\frac{b}{a}\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{\tau}^{1}\sigma(s)ds=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}G(0,s)\sigma(s)ds,

where

\displaystyle G(t,s)=\frac{1}{a}\begin{cases}b+as,\,&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\\
b+at,\,&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1.\end{cases}

Lemma 1.18

Let
x\in C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1)
satisfies
x^{\prime\prime}<0
on
(0,1)
,

x(0)=0
,
x^{\prime}(1)=a\geq 0
. Then,
x(t)\geq tx(1)
for
t\in[0,1]
.

Lemma 1.19

The Green’s function

H(t,s)=\begin{cases}\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\eta}-\frac{\alpha t(\eta-s)}{1-\alpha\eta}-(t-s),\,&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\,s\leq\eta,\\
\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\eta}-\frac{\alpha t(\eta-s)}{1-\alpha\eta},\,&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1,\,s\leq\eta,\\
\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\eta},\,&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1,\,s\geq\eta,\\
\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\eta}-(t-s),\,&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\,s\geq\eta.\end{cases}

(1)

satisfies

(i)

H(t,s)\leq\mu s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092pt(t,s)\in[0,1]\times[0,1]
,

(ii)

H(t,s)\geq\nu s(1-s),\hskip 12.80365pt(t,s)\in[\eta,1]\times[0,1]
,

(iii)

H(t,s)\geq\nu t(1-t)s(1-s),\hskip 12.80365pt(t,s)\in[0,1]\times[0,1]
,

where

\displaystyle\mu:=\frac{\max\{1,\alpha\}}{1-\alpha\eta}>0,\,\nu:=\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\}\min\{\eta,1-\eta\}}{1-\alpha\eta}>0.

Chapter 2
Singular Systems of Ordinary Differential Equations with Nonlocal Boundary Conditions

Existence theory for nonlinear three-point boundary value problems (BVPs) was initiated by Gupta
gupta
. Since then the study of nonlinear regular multi-point BVPs has attracted the attention of many researchers; see for example,
bl
;
kp
;
lb
;
llw1
;
mr
;
mm
;
wj
;
liuqiu
;
zhang
for scalar equations, and for systems of BVPs, see
cheungwong
;
dr
;
kw
. Recently, the study of SBVPs has also attracted much attention. An excellent resource with an extensive bibliography was produced by Agarwal and O’Regan
ao
. In Sections
2
and
3
, we study the following systems of SBVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=f(t,y(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=g(t,x(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=0,\,x(1)=\alpha x(\eta),\\
y(0)&=0,\,y(1)=\alpha y(\eta),\end{split}

(2)

and

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=f(t,x(t),y(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=g(t,x(t),y(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=0,\,x(1)=\alpha x(\eta),\\
y(0)&=0,\,y(1)=\alpha y(\eta)\end{split}

(3)

where
\eta\in(0,1)
,
0<\alpha\eta<1
. For the system of SBVPs (
2
), we assume that
f,g:(0,1)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow(0,\infty)
are continuous,
f(t,0)
and
g(t,0)
are not identically
0
. For the system of SBVPs (
3
), we assume that
f,g:(0,1)\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow(0,\infty)
are continuous. Further, both
f
and
g
are allowed to be singular at
t=0
,
t=1
,
x=0
and/or
y=0
. By singularity we mean that the nonlinearities
f
and
g
are allowed to be unbounded at
t=0
,
t=1
,
x=0
and
y=0
.

Further, in Section
4
, we present existence result
for the following coupled system of SBVPs subject to four-point coupled BCs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=f(t,x(t),y(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=g(t,x(t),y(t)),\hskip 12.80365ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=0,\,x(1)=\alpha y(\xi),\\
y(0)&=0,\,y(1)=\beta x(\eta),\end{split}

(4)

where the parameters
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\xi
,
\eta
satisfy
\xi,\eta\in(0,1)
,
0<\alpha\beta\xi\eta<1
. We assume that the nonlinearities
f,g:(0,1)\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
are continuous and allowed to be singular at
t=0
or
t=1
.

2
Sufficient conditions for the existence of at least one positive solution

In this section, we establish existence of positive solution to the system of SBVPs (
2
). We say
(x,y)
is a positive solution to system of SBVPs (
2
) if
(x,y)\in(C[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
,
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
,
(x,y)
satisfies (
2
). Let
n_{0}>\max\{\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{1-\eta},\frac{2-\alpha}{1-\alpha\eta}\}

be a fixed positive integer. For each
x\in\mathcal{E}_{n}:=C[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
, we write

\|x\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}=\max\{|x(t)|:t\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]\}
,
where
n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,n_{0}+2,\cdots\}
. Clearly,

\mathcal{E}_{n}
with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}

is a Banach space. Define a cone
K_{n}
of
\mathcal{E}_{n}
as

\displaystyle K_{n}=\{x\in\mathcal{E}_{n}:x(t)\geq 0\text{ and }x^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ for }t\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]\}.

For any real constant
r>0
, define an open neighborhood of
0\in\mathcal{E}_{n}
of radius
r
by

\displaystyle\Omega_{r}=\{x\in\mathcal{E}_{n}:\|x\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}<r\}.

Lemma 2.1

For
z\in\mathcal{E}_{n}
, the linear BVP

\begin{split}-u^{\prime\prime}(t)&=z(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],\\
u(\frac{1}{n})&=0,\,u(1-\frac{1}{n})=\alpha u(\eta),\end{split}

(5)

has integral representation

u(t)=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)z(s)ds,

(6)

where the Green’s function
H_{n}:[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]\rightarrow[0,\infty)
is defined by

H_{n}(t,s)=\begin{cases}\frac{(t-\frac{1}{n})((1-\frac{1}{n}-s)-\alpha(\eta-s))}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}-(t-s),&\frac{1}{n}\leq s\leq t\leq 1-\frac{1}{n},\,s\leq\eta,\\
\frac{(t-\frac{1}{n})((1-\frac{1}{n}-s)-\alpha(\eta-s))}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta},&\frac{1}{n}\leq t\leq s\leq 1-\frac{1}{n},\,s\leq\eta,\\
\frac{(t-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta},&\frac{1}{n}\leq t\leq s\leq 1-\frac{1}{n},\,s\geq\eta,\\
\frac{(t-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}-(t-s),&\frac{1}{n}\leq s\leq t\leq 1-\frac{1}{n},\,s\geq\eta.\end{cases}

(7)

Lemma 2.2

The Green’s function
H_{n}
satisfies

(i)

H_{n}(t,s)\leq\mu_{n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s),\hskip 11.38092pt(t,s)\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
,

(ii)

H_{n}(t,s)\geq\nu_{n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s),\hskip 12.80365pt(t,s)\in[\eta,1-\frac{1}{n}]\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
,

where

\displaystyle\mu_{n}:=\frac{\max\{1,\alpha\}}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}>0,\,\nu_{n}:=\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\}\min\{\eta-\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}-\eta\}}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}>0.

Now we consider the modified system of non-singular BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=f(t,\max\{y(t)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=g(t,\max\{x(t)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],\\
x(\frac{1}{n})&=0,\,x(1-\frac{1}{n})=\alpha x(\eta),\\
y(\frac{1}{n})&=0,\,y(1-\frac{1}{n})=\alpha y(\eta),\end{split}

(8)

which in view of Lemma
2.1
, can be expressed as a system of integral equations

\begin{split}x(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\max\{y(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],\\
y(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)g(s,\max\{x(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}].\end{split}

(9)

Thus,
(x_{n},y_{n})\in\mathcal{E}_{n}\times\mathcal{E}_{n}
is a solution of (
8
) if and only if
(x_{n},y_{n})
is a solution of (
9
). Define maps
A_{n},B_{n},T_{n}:\mathcal{E}_{n}\rightarrow K_{n}
by

\begin{split}(A_{n}y)(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\max\{y(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],\\
(B_{n}x)(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)g(s,\max\{x(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],\\
(T_{n}x)(t)&=(A_{n}(B_{n}x))(t),\hskip 133.72786ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}].\end{split}

(10)

If
u_{n}\in K_{n}
is a fixed point of
T_{n}
; then the system of
BVPs (
8
) has a solution
(x_{n},y_{n})
given by

\displaystyle x_{n}(t)

\displaystyle=u_{n}(t),\hskip 32.72049ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}],

\displaystyle y_{n}(t)

\displaystyle=(B_{n}u_{n})(t),\hskip 14.22636ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}].

Assume that the following holds:

(\mathbf{A}_{1})

there exist
K,L\in C((0,1),(0,\infty))
and
F,G\in C((0,\infty),(0,\infty))
such that

\displaystyle f(t,u)\leq K(t)F(u),\hskip 11.38092ptg(t,u)\leq L(t)G(u),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\;u\in(0,\infty),

where

\displaystyle a:=\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)K(t)dt<+\infty,\hskip 11.38092ptb:=\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)L(t)dt<+\infty.

Lemma 2.3

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{1})
holds. Then the map
T_{n}:\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n}\rightarrow K_{n}
is completely continuous.

Proof

Clearly, for any
u\in K_{n}
, we have
(T_{n}u)(t)\geq 0

and
(T_{n}u)^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0
for
t\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
.
Consequently,
T_{n}u\in K_{n}
for all
u\in K_{n}
. Now, we show
that
T_{n}:\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n}\rightarrow K_{n}
is
uniformly bounded and equicontinuous. We introduce

\begin{split}d_{n}&=b\,\mu_{n}\max_{u\in[0,r]}G(u+\frac{1}{n}),\\
\omega_{n}&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}f(t,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)g(s,u(s)+\frac{1}{n})ds)dt.\end{split}

(11)

For any
u\in\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n}
, using (
10
),

(\mathbf{A}_{1})
and
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(T_{n}u)(t)=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)F(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\leq\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)K(s)F(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds.\end{split}

But, using
(\mathbf{A}_{1})
,
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
and
(
11
),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)g(s,u(s)+\frac{1}{n})ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)L(s)G(u(s)+\frac{1}{n})ds

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)L(s)G(u(s)+\frac{1}{n})ds

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{n}\max_{u\in[0,r]}G(u+\frac{1}{n})\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)L(s)ds

\displaystyle\leq b\mu_{n}\max_{u\in[0,r]}G(u+\frac{1}{n})=d_{n}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{n}\max_{u\in[0,d_{n}]}F(u+\frac{1}{n})\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)L(s)ds

\displaystyle\leq a\mu_{n}\max_{u\in[0,d_{n}]}F(u+\frac{1}{n}),

which implies that

\displaystyle\|T_{n}u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\leq a\mu_{n}\max_{u\in[0,d_{n}]}F(u+\frac{1}{n}),

that is,
T_{n}(\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n})
is uniformly
bounded. To show
T_{n}(\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n})
is
equicontinuous, let
t_{1},t_{2}\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
.
Since the Green’s function
H_{n}
is uniformly continuous on

[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
, therefore,
T_{n}(\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n})
is equicontinuous. By
Theorem
1.5
,
T_{n}(\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n})
is
relatively compact. Hence,
T_{n}
is a compact map. Now, we show that
T_{n}
is continuous. Let
u_{m},u\in\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n}
such that

\displaystyle\|u_{m}-u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\rightarrow 0\text{ as }m\rightarrow+\infty.

Using (
10
) and
(i)
of Lemma

2.2
, we have

\begin{split}&|(T_{n}u_{m})(t)-(T_{n}u)(t)|=\\
&\left|\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)\big(f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u_{m}(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\right.\\
&\left.-f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\big)ds\right|\\
&\leq\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)\\
&\left|f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u_{m}(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\right.\\
&\left.-f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\right|ds\end{split}

Consequently,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|T_{n}u_{m}-T_{n}u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\leq\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)\left|f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)\right.\\
&\left.g(\tau,u_{m}(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)-f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\right|ds.\end{split}

By the Lebesgue dominated convergent theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle\|T_{n}u_{m}-T_{n}u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\rightarrow 0\text{ as
}m\rightarrow+\infty,

that is,
T_{n}:\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n}\rightarrow K_{n}
is
a continuous. Hence,
T_{n}:\overline{\Omega}_{r}\cap K_{n}\rightarrow K_{n}
is completely continuous.

Assume that

(\mathbf{A}_{2})

there exist
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in(0,\infty)
with
\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\leq 1
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{u\rightarrow\infty}\frac{F(u)}{u^{\alpha_{1}}}\rightarrow 0,\hskip 11.38092pt\lim_{u\rightarrow\infty}\frac{G(u)}{u^{\alpha_{2}}}\rightarrow 0,

(\mathbf{A}_{3})

there exist
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in(0,\infty)
with

\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{u\rightarrow 0^{+}}\min_{t\in[\eta,1]}\frac{f(t,u)}{u^{\beta_{1}}}>0,\hskip 11.38092pt\liminf_{u\rightarrow 0^{+}}\min_{t\in[\eta,1]}\frac{g(t,u)}{u^{\beta_{2}}}>0.

Theorem 2.4

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{1})-(\mathbf{A}_{3})
hold. Then the system of SBVPs (
2
) has a positive solution.

Proof

By
(\mathbf{A}_{2})
, there exist real constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}>0
such that

2^{2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}<1,

(12)

and

F(u+\frac{1}{n})\leq c_{1}(u+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{1}}+c_{2},\,G(u+\frac{1}{n})\leq c_{3}(u+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4}\text{ for }u\geq 0.

(13)

In view of (
12
), we choose a real constant
R>0
such that

\small{R\geq\frac{a\mu_{n}c_{2}+2^{2\alpha_{1}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}}+2^{\alpha_{1}}a\mu_{n}n^{-\alpha_{1}}c_{1}+2^{2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}n^{-\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}c_{1}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}}{1-2^{2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}}.}

(14)

For any
u\in\partial\Omega_{R}\cap K_{n}
, using (
10
),

(\mathbf{A}_{1})
and (
13
), it follows that

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)F(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)(c_{1}(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}+c_{2})ds

\displaystyle=c_{1}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}ds

\displaystyle+c_{2}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)ds.

Again using
(\mathbf{A}_{1})
and (
13
), we obtain

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)L(\tau)G(u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}ds

\displaystyle+c_{2}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)ds

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)L(\tau)(c_{3}(u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4})d\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}ds

\displaystyle+c_{2}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)ds

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)ds(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)L(\tau)d\tau(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4}))^{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle+c_{2}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)ds.

Employing
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
and
(\mathbf{A}_{1})
, leads
to

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)K(s)ds(\frac{1}{n}+\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)

\displaystyle L(\tau)d\tau(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4}))^{\alpha_{1}}+c_{2}\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)K(s)ds

\displaystyle\leq a\mu_{n}c_{1}(\frac{1}{n}+b\,\mu_{n}(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4}))^{\alpha_{1}}+a\mu_{n}c_{2}.

But,

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{n}+b\,\mu_{n}(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4}))^{\alpha_{1}}\leq 2^{\alpha_{1}}(\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}}}+b^{\alpha_{1}}\,\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}}(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4})^{\alpha_{1}}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)\leq 2^{\alpha_{1}}a\mu_{n}c_{1}(\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}}}+b^{\alpha_{1}}\,\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}}(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4})^{\alpha_{1}})+a\mu_{n}c_{2}.

Also,

\displaystyle(c_{3}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{2}}+c_{4})^{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\alpha_{1}}(c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}(R+\frac{1}{n})^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}})

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\alpha_{1}}(2^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}(R^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}})+c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\alpha_{1}}a\mu_{n}c_{1}(\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}}}+2^{\alpha_{1}}b^{\alpha_{1}}\,\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}}(2^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}(R^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}})+c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}}))+a\mu_{n}c_{2}

\displaystyle=2^{\alpha_{1}}a\mu_{n}n^{-\alpha_{1}}c_{1}+2^{2\alpha_{1}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}(2^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}(R^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}})+c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}})+a\mu_{n}c_{2}

\displaystyle=2^{\alpha_{1}}a\mu_{n}n^{-\alpha_{1}}c_{1}+2^{2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}(R^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+\frac{1}{n^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}})+2^{2\alpha_{1}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}}+a\mu_{n}c_{2}

\displaystyle=2^{\alpha_{1}}a\mu_{n}n^{-\alpha_{1}}c_{1}+2^{2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}R^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle+2^{2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}n^{-\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}c_{1}c_{3}^{\alpha_{1}}+2^{2\alpha_{1}}ab^{\alpha_{1}}\mu_{n}^{\alpha_{1}+1}c_{1}c_{4}^{\alpha_{1}}+a\mu_{n}c_{2}.

Using (
14
), we obtain

\|T_{n}u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\leq\|u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\text{ for all
}u\in\partial\Omega_{R}\cap K_{n}.

(15)

Now, by
(\mathbf{A}_{3})
, there exist constants
c_{5},c_{6}>0

and
\rho\in(0,R)
such that

f(t,x)\geq c_{5}x^{\beta_{1}},\,g(t,x)\geq c_{6}x^{\beta_{2}}\text{ for
}x\in[0,\rho],\,t\in[\eta,1].

(16)

Choose

r_{n}=\min\big\{\rho,\nu_{n}^{\beta_{1}+1}n^{-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}c_{5}c_{6}^{\beta_{1}}(\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)ds)^{\beta_{1}+1}\big\}.

(17)

For any
u\in\partial\Omega_{r_{n}}\cap K_{n}
and

t\in[\eta,1-\frac{1}{n}]
, using (
10
) and (
16
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(T_{n}u)(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\geq c_{5}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)^{\beta_{1}}ds\\
&\geq c_{5}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)(\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)^{\beta_{1}}ds\\
&\geq c_{5}c_{6}^{\beta_{1}}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)(\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)(u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})^{\beta_{2}}d\tau)^{\beta_{1}}ds\\
&\geq n^{-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}c_{5}c_{6}^{\beta_{1}}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)(\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)d\tau)^{\beta_{1}}ds\\
&\geq n^{-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}c_{5}c_{6}^{\beta_{1}}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)(\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)d\tau)^{\beta_{1}}ds.\end{split}

Employing
(ii)
of Lemma
2.2
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}(T_{n}u)(t)&\geq\nu_{n}^{\beta_{1}+1}n^{-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}c_{5}c_{6}^{\beta_{1}}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)ds(\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)d\tau)^{\beta_{1}}\\
&=\nu_{n}^{\beta_{1}+1}n^{-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}c_{5}c_{6}^{\beta_{1}}(\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)ds)^{\beta_{1}+1}.\end{split}

Using (
17
), we obtain

\|T_{n}u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\geq\|u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\text{ for all }u\in\partial\Omega_{r_{n}}\cap K_{n}.

(18)

In view of
(
15
), (
18
) and by Theorem
1.11
,
T_{n}
has
a fixed point

u_{n}\in(\overline{\Omega}_{R}\setminus\Omega_{r_{n}})\cap K_{n}
.
Note that

r_{n}\leq\|u_{n}\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\leq R

(19)

and
r_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, we have
exhibited a uniform bound for each
u_{n}\in\mathcal{E}_{n}
, and

\{u_{m}\}_{m\geq n}
is uniformly bounded on
[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
.

Now, we show that
\{u_{m}\}_{m\geq n}
, is equicontinuous on

[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
. For

t\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
, consider the integral equation

\begin{split}u_{m}(t)=u_{m}(\frac{1}{n})+\frac{u_{m}(1-\frac{1}{n})-\alpha u_{m}(\eta)-(1-\alpha)u_{m}(\frac{1}{n})}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}(t-\frac{1}{n})+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)\tilde{f}(s)ds,\end{split}

where

\tilde{f}(t)=f(t,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)g(s,\frac{1}{n}+u_{m}(s))ds)
.

Which can also be written as

\displaystyle u_{m}(t)=u_{m}(\frac{1}{n})+\frac{u_{m}(1-\frac{1}{n})-\alpha u_{m}(\eta)-(1-\alpha)u_{m}(\frac{1}{n})}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}(t-\frac{1}{n})+\frac{t-\frac{1}{n}}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}

\displaystyle\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)\tilde{f}(s)ds-\frac{\alpha(t-\frac{1}{n})}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}\int_{1/n}^{\eta}(\eta-s)\tilde{f}(s)ds-\int_{1/n}^{t}(t-s)\tilde{f}(s)ds.

Differentiating with respect to
t
, we get

\displaystyle u_{m}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{m}(1-\frac{1}{n})-\alpha u_{m}(\eta)-(1-\alpha)u_{m}(\frac{1}{n})}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}+\frac{1}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}(s)ds-\frac{\alpha}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}\int_{1/n}^{\eta}(\eta-s)\tilde{f}(s)ds-\int_{1/n}^{t}\tilde{f}(s)ds,

which implies that

\displaystyle\|u_{m}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1+\alpha)R}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}+\frac{1}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)\tilde{f}(s)ds

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha}{1-\frac{2}{n}+\frac{\alpha}{n}-\alpha\eta}\int_{1/n}^{\eta}(\eta-s)\tilde{f}(s)ds+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}\tilde{f}(s)ds.

Hence,
\{u_{m}\}_{m\geq n}
is equicontinuous on

[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]
.

For
m\geq n
, we define

\displaystyle v_{m}(t)=\begin{cases}u_{m}(\frac{1}{n}),&0\leq t\leq\frac{1}{n},\\
u_{m}(t),&\frac{1}{n}\leq t\leq 1-\frac{1}{n},\\
\alpha u_{m}(\eta),&1-\frac{1}{n}\leq t\leq 1.\end{cases}

Since
v_{m}
is a constant extension of
u_{m}
to
[0,1]
, the
sequence
\{v_{m}\}
is uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on

[0,1]
. Thus, there exists a subsequence
\{v_{m_{k}}\}
of

\{v_{m}\}
converging uniformly to
v\in C[0,1]
. We introduce the notation

\displaystyle\begin{split}x_{m_{k}}(t)&=v_{m_{k}}(t),\,y_{m_{k}}(t)=\int_{1/m_{k}}^{1-1/m_{k}}H_{m_{k}}(t,s)g(s,v_{m_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{m_{k}})ds,\\
x(t)&=\lim_{m_{k}\rightarrow\infty}x_{m_{k}}(t),\,y(t)=\lim_{m_{k}\rightarrow\infty}y_{m_{k}}(t).\end{split}

For
t\in[0,1]
consider the integral equations

\displaystyle x_{m_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{1/m_{k}}^{1-1/m_{k}}H_{m_{k}}(t,s)f(t,y_{m_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{m_{k}})ds,

\displaystyle y_{m_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{1/m_{k}}^{1-1/m_{k}}H_{m_{k}}(t,s)g(t,x_{m_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{m_{k}})ds.

Letting
m_{k}\rightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle x(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f(t,y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)g(s,x(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

Moreover,

\displaystyle x(0)=0,\,x(1)=\alpha x(\eta),\,y(0)=0,\,y(1)=\alpha y(\eta).

Hence,
(x,y)
is a solution of the system of BVPs (
2
). Moreover, since the Green’s function
H
is positive on
(0,1)\times(0,1)
,
f(t,0)
and
g(t,0)
are not identically
0
, it follows that
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
.

Example 2.5

Let

\displaystyle f(t,y)=\frac{1}{t(1-t)}\left(\frac{1}{y}+3y^{1/3}\right),\hskip 11.38092ptg(t,x)=\frac{1}{t(1-t)}\left(\frac{1}{x}+4x\right)

and

\alpha=2,\,\eta=\frac{1}{3}
. Choose

\displaystyle K(t)=L(t)=\frac{1}{t(1-t)},\hskip 11.38092ptF(y)=\frac{1}{y}+3y^{1/3},\hskip 11.38092ptG(x)=\frac{1}{x}+4x,

and
\alpha_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha_{2}=2
,
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}=1
. Clearly,
(\mathbf{A}_{1})-(\mathbf{A}_{3})
are satisfied. Hence, by Theorem
2.4
, the system of SBVPs (
2
) has a positive solution.

Assume that

(\mathbf{A}_{4})

f(t,u)
,
G(u)
are non-increasing with respect
to
u
and for each fixed
n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,n_{0}+2,\cdots\}
,
there exists a constant
\rho_{n}>0
such that

\displaystyle f(t,\frac{1}{n}+b\,\mu_{n}G(\frac{1}{n}))\geq\rho_{n}(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)ds)^{-1},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}].

Theorem 2.6

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{1})
,
(\mathbf{A}_{2})
and

(\mathbf{A}_{4})
hold. Then the system of SBVPs (
2
) has a positive solution.

Proof

For any
u\in\partial\Omega_{\rho_{n}}\cap K_{n}
,
using (
10
),
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
and

(\mathbf{A}_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(T_{n}u)(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\geq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\geq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\mu_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)L(\tau)G(u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\geq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\mu_{n}G(\frac{1}{n})\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)L(\tau)d\tau)ds\\
&\geq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+b\,\mu_{n}\,G(\frac{1}{n}))ds.\end{split}

Now in view of
(\mathbf{A}_{4})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(T_{n}u)(t)&\geq\rho_{n}\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)ds(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)d\tau)^{-1}\\
&\geq\rho_{n}\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)ds(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)d\tau)^{-1}\\
&=\rho_{n},\end{split}

which implies that

\|T_{n}u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\geq\|u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\text{ for all }u\in\partial\Omega_{\rho_{n}}\cap K_{n}.

(20)

In view of
(\mathbf{A}_{2})
, we can choose
R>\rho_{n}
such that
(
15
) holds. Hence, in view of (
15
), (
20
)
and by Theorem
1.11
,
T_{n}
has a fixed point
u_{n}\in(\overline{\Omega}_{R}\setminus\Omega_{\rho_{n}})\cap K_{n}
. Now,
following the same procedure as done in Theorem
2.4
, the system of SBVPs (
2
) has a positive solution.

Example 2.7

Let

\displaystyle f(t,y)=\frac{e^{\frac{1}{y}}}{t(1-t)},\hskip 11.38092ptg(t,x)=\frac{e^{\frac{1}{x}}}{t(1-t)}

Assume that

(\mathbf{A}_{5})

F(u)
,
g(t,u)
are non-increasing with respect
to
u
and for each fixed
n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,n_{0}+2,\cdots\}
,

M>0
such that

\displaystyle a\,\mu_{n}F(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)g(s,M+\frac{1}{n})ds)\leq M.

Theorem 2.8

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{1})
,
(\mathbf{A}_{3})
and

(\mathbf{A}_{5})
holds. Then the system of SBVPs (
2
)
has a positive solution.

Proof

For any
u\in\partial\Omega_{M}\cap K_{n}
, using (
10
),

(\mathbf{A}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{A}_{5})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}(T_{n}u)(t)&=\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)f(s,\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)F(\frac{1}{n}+\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)F(\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,u(\tau)+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)F(\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,M+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds\\
&\leq\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)F(\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(s,\tau)g(\tau,M+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)ds.\end{split}

Employing
(ii)
of Lemma
2.2
and
(\mathbf{A}_{5})
,
leads to

\displaystyle(T_{n}u)(t)\leq F(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)g(\tau,M+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}H_{n}(t,s)K(s)ds.

Now, using
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
,

(\mathbf{A}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{A}_{5})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}(T_{n}u)(t)&\leq\mu_{n}F(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)g(\tau,M+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\\
&\int_{1/n}^{1-1/n}(s-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-s)K(s)ds\\
&\leq a\mu_{n}F(\nu_{n}\int_{\eta}^{1-1/n}(\tau-\frac{1}{n})(1-\frac{1}{n}-\tau)g(\tau,M+\frac{1}{n})d\tau)\\
&\leq M,\end{split}

which implies that

\|T_{n}u\|\leq\|u\|_{{}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}}\text{ for all }u\in\partial\Omega_{M}\cap K_{n}.

(21)

By

(\mathbf{A}_{3})
, we can choose
r_{n}\in(0,M)
such that
(
18
) holds. Hence, in view of (
21
), (
18
)
and by Theorem
1.11
,
T_{n}
has a fixed point

u_{n}\in(\overline{\Omega}_{M}\setminus\Omega_{r_{n}})\cap K_{n}
.
Consequently, the system of SBVPs (
2
) has a positive solution.

3
Sufficient conditions for the existence of at least one positive solution to a more general singular systems

In this section, we establish the existence of positive solution for the system of SBVPs (
3
). We say
(x,y)
is a positive solution of the system of SBVPs (
3
) if
(x,y)\in(C[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
,
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
,
(x,y)
satisfies (
3
). For
x\in C[0,1]
, we write
\|x\|=\max_{t\in[0,1]}|x(t)|
. For any real constant
r>0
, we define an open neighborhood of
0\in C[0,1]
as

\displaystyle\Omega_{r}=\{x\in C[0,1]:\|x\|<r\}.

Define a cone
K
of
C[0,1]
as

\displaystyle K=\{x\in C[0,1]:x(t)\geq\,t(1-t)\,\gamma\,\|x\|\text{ for }t\in[0,1]\},

where

\displaystyle 0<\gamma:=\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\}\min\{\eta,1-\eta\}}{\max\{1,\alpha\}}<1.

For each
(x,y)\in C[0,1]\times C[0,1]
, we write
\|(x,y)\|=\|x\|+\|y\|
. Clearly,
(C[0,1]\times C[0,1],\|\cdot\|)
is a Banach space and
K\times K
is a cone of
C[0,1]\times C[0,1]
.

For
n\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
, consider the following system of SBVPs

\begin{split}-x&{}^{\prime\prime}(t)=f(t,\max\{x(t)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\},\max\{y(t)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
-y&{}^{\prime\prime}(t)=g(t,\max\{x(t)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\},\max\{y(t)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
x&(0)=y(0)=0,\,x(1)=\alpha x(\eta),\,y(1)=\alpha y(\eta).\end{split}

(22)

The system of BVPs (
22
) can be expressed as an equivalent system of integral equations

\begin{split}&x(t)=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f(s,\max\{x(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\},\max\{y(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\\
&y(t)=\int_{0}^{10}H(t,s)g(s,\max\{x(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\},\max\{y(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\end{split}

(23)

where the Green’s function
H
is represented by (
1
). By a solution of the system of BVPs (
22
), we mean a solution of the corresponding system of integral equations (
23
). Define a map

T_{n}:C[0,1]\times C[0,1]\rightarrow K\times K
by

T_{n}(x,y)=(A_{n}(x,y),B_{n}(x,y)),

(24)

where the maps
A_{n},B_{n}:C[0,1]\times C[0,1]\rightarrow K
are defined by

\begin{split}&A_{n}(x,y)(t)=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f(s,\max\{x(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\},\max\{y(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds,\\
&B_{n}(x,y)(t)=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)g(s,\max\{x(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\},\max\{y(s)+\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n}\})ds.\end{split}

(25)

Clearly, if
(x_{n},y_{n})\in C[0,1]\times C[0,1]
is a fixed point of
T_{n}
, then
(x_{n},y_{n})
also a solution of the system of BVPs (
22
).

Assume that

(\mathbf{A}_{6})

for each
t\in(0,1)
,
f(t,x,y)
and
g(t,x,y)
are non-increasing with respect to
x
and
y
,
f(\cdot,1,1),g(\cdot,1,1)\in C((0,1),(0,\infty))
and

\displaystyle a:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)f(t,t(1-t),t(1-t))dt<+\infty,

\displaystyle b:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)g(t,t(1-t),t(1-t))dt<+\infty,

(\mathbf{A}_{7})

there exist real constants
\alpha_{i}
,
\beta_{i}
with
\alpha_{i}\leq 0\leq\beta_{i}
,
i=1,2
, such that for all
t\in(0,1)
,
x,y\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{\,\beta_{1}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\alpha_{1}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt\text{ if }0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\alpha_{1}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\,\beta_{1}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 10.52737pt\text{ if }c\geq 1,\\
c^{\,\beta_{2}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\alpha_{2}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt\text{ if }0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\alpha_{2}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\,\beta_{2}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 10.52737pt\text{ if }c\geq 1;\end{split}

(\mathbf{A}_{8})

there exist real constants
\gamma_{i}
,
\rho_{i}
with

\gamma_{i}\leq 0\leq\rho_{i}
,
i=1,2
, such that for all
t\in(0,1)
,
x,y\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{\,\rho_{1}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\gamma_{1}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt\text{ if }0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\gamma_{1}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\,\rho_{1}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 10.52737pt\text{ if }c\geq 1,\\
c^{\,\rho_{2}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\gamma_{2}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt\text{ if }0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\gamma_{2}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\,\rho_{2}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 10.52737pt\text{ if }c\geq 1.\end{split}

Lemma 3.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{6})-(\mathbf{A}_{8})
holds. Then the map
T_{n}:(\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K)\rightarrow K\times K
is completely continuous.

Proof

Clearly,
T_{n}(x,y)\in K\times K
for all
(x,y)\in K\times K
. Now, we show that the map
A_{n}:(\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K)\rightarrow K
is uniformly bounded and equicontinuous. For
(x,y)\in(\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K)
,
using (
25
),
(\mathbf{A}_{6})
,
(\mathbf{A}_{7})
and Lemma
1.19
,
A_{n}((\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K))
is uniformly bounded. Similarly, using (
25
),
(\mathbf{A}_{6})
,
(\mathbf{A}_{8})
and Lemma
1.19
, we can show that
B_{n}((\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K))
is also uniformly bounded. Thus,

T_{n}((\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K))
is
uniformly bounded. Since the Green’s function
H
is uniformly continuous on
[0,1]\times[0,1]
, therefore,
T_{n}((\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K))
is
equicontinuous. Thus by Theorem
1.5
, it follows that
T_{n}((\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K))
is
relatively compact. Hence,
T_{n}
is a compact map.

Now, we show that
T_{n}
is continuous. Let
(x_{m},y_{m}),(x,y)\in K\times K
such that

\displaystyle\|(x_{m},y_{m})-(x,y)\|\rightarrow 0\text{ as }m\rightarrow+\infty.

Using (
25
) and
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})(t)-A_{n}(x,y)(t)|=\left|\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)(f(s,x_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n},y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n},y(s)+\frac{1}{n}))ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)\left|f(s,x_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n},y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n},y(s)+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds\\
&\leq\mu\int_{0}^{1}s(1-s)\left|f(s,x_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n},y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n},y(s)+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds.\end{split}

Consequently,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|\leq&\mu\int_{0}^{1}s(1-s)\left|f(s,x_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n},y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n},y(s)+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds.\end{split}

By Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, it follows that

\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|\rightarrow 0\text{ as }m\rightarrow+\infty.

(26)

Similarly, by using (
25
) and
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
,
we have

\|B_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-B_{n}(x,y)\|\rightarrow 0\text{ as }m\rightarrow+\infty.

(27)

From (
26
), (
27
) and (
24
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|T_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-T_{n}(x,y)\|\rightarrow 0\text{ as }m\rightarrow+\infty,

that is,
T_{n}:(\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K)\rightarrow K\times K
is continuous. Hence,
T_{n}:(\overline{\Omega}_{r_{1}}\times\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2}})\cap(K\times K)\rightarrow K\times K
is completely continuous.

Theorem 3.2

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{6})-(\mathbf{A}_{8})
hold. Then the system of SBVPs (
3
) has a positive solution.

Proof

Choose a real constants
R_{1}>0
and
R_{2}>0
such that

\begin{split}R_{1}&\geq\max\left\{1,\left(\mu a\gamma^{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}c_{7}^{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}}R_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha_{1}}}\right\},\\
R_{2}&\geq\max\left\{1,\left(\mu b\gamma^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}c_{7}^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}-\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}}R_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{2}}}\right\}.\end{split}

(28)

where
c_{7}\in(0,1]
such that
c_{7}R_{1}\leq 1
and
c_{7}R_{2}\leq 1
. For any
(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{R_{1}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}})\cap(K\times K)
, using (
25
),
(\mathbf{A}_{6})
,
(\mathbf{A}_{7})
, (
28
) and
(i)
of Lemma
1.19
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n},y(s)+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\mu\int_{0}^{1}s(1-s)f(s,s(1-s)\gamma\|x\|,s(1-s)\gamma\|y\|)ds\\
&\leq R_{1}\end{split}

which implies that

\|A_{n}(x,y)\|\leq\|x\|\text{ for all }(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{R_{1}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}})\cap(K\times K).

(29)

Similarly, using (
25
),
(\mathbf{A}_{6})
,
(\mathbf{A}_{8})
,
(i)
of Lemma
2.2
, (
28
), we obtain

\|B_{n}(x,y)\|\leq\|y\|\text{ for all }(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{R_{1}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}})\cap(K\times K).

(30)

From (
29
), (
30
) and (
24
), it follows that

\|T_{n}(x,y)\|\leq\|(x,y)\|\text{ for all
}(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{R_{1}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}})\cap(K\times K).

(31)

Choose a real constants
r_{n}\in(0,R_{1})
and
s_{n}\in(0,R_{2})
such that

\begin{split}r_{n}&\leq c_{8}^{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}}n^{-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}}\nu\int_{\eta}^{1}s(1-s)f(s,1,1)ds\\
s_{n}&\leq c_{8}^{\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}-\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}}n^{-\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}}\nu\int_{\eta}^{1}s(1-s)g(s,1,1)ds\end{split}

(32)

where
c_{8}\in(0,1]
is such that
c_{8}(r_{n}+\frac{1}{n})\leq 1
and
c_{8}(s_{n}+\frac{1}{n})\leq 1
. For any

(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{r_{n}}\times\Omega_{s_{n}})\cap(K\times K)
, using
(
25
),
(\mathbf{A}_{7})
and Lemma
1.19
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f\left(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n},y(s)+\frac{1}{n}\right)ds\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f\left(s,c_{8}\frac{x(s)+\frac{1}{n}}{c_{8}},c_{8}\frac{y(s)+\frac{1}{n}}{c_{8}}\right)ds\\
&\geq c_{8}^{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}}n^{-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}}\nu\int_{\eta}^{1}s(1-s)f(s,1,1)ds\geq r_{n}\end{split}

Thus, in view of (
32
), it follows that

\|A_{n}(x,y)\|\geq\|x\|\text{ for all
}(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{r_{n}}\times\Omega_{s_{n}})\cap(K\times K).

(33)

Similarly, using (
25
),
(\mathbf{A}_{8})
and Lemma
1.19
, we get

\|B_{n}(x,y)\|\geq\|y\|\text{ for all
}(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{r_{n}}\times\Omega_{s_{n}})\cap(K\times K).

(34)

From (
33
) and (
34
), we obtain

\|T_{n}(x,y)\|\geq\|(x,y)\|\text{ for all
}(x,y)\in\partial(\Omega_{r_{n}}\times\Omega_{s_{n}})\cap(K\times K).

(35)

In view of (
31
), (
35
) and by Theorem
1.11
,

T_{n}
has a fixed point
(x_{n},y_{n})\in(\overline{\Omega_{R_{1}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}}}\setminus(\Omega_{r_{n}}\times\Omega_{s_{n}}))\cap(K\times K)
. Further

r_{n}\leq\|x_{n}\|\leq R_{1},\,\,s_{n}\leq\|y_{n}\|\leq R_{2},

(36)

where
r_{n},\,s_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Thus,
\{(x_{n},y_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
bounded uniformly on
[0,1]
. Moreover, since the Green’s function
H
is uniformly continuous on
[0,1]\times[0,1]
, therefore
\{(x_{n},y_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Thus, there exists a subsequence

\{(x_{n_{k}},y_{n_{k}})\}
of
\{(x_{n},y_{n})\}
converging
uniformly to
(x,y)\in C[0,1]\times C[0,1]
. Now, for
t\in[0,1]
consider the integral equations

\displaystyle x_{n_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f(t,x_{n_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}},y_{n_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})ds,

\displaystyle y_{n_{k}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)g(t,x_{n_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}},y_{n_{k}}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})ds,

as
n_{k}\rightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle x(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)f(t,x(s),y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,s)g(s,x(s),y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

Moreover,

\displaystyle x(0)=0,\,x(1)=\alpha x(\eta),\,y(0)=0,\,y(1)=\alpha y(\eta).

Hence,
(x,y)
is a solution of the system of BVPs (
3
). Moreover, since
f,g:(0,1)\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow(0,\infty)
and the Green’s function
H
is positive on
(0,1)\times(0,1)
, it follows that
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
.

Example 3.3

Let

\displaystyle\begin{split}f(t,x,y)&=\frac{1}{\sqrt[4]{t(1-t)\,x\,y}},\\
g(t,x,y)&=\frac{1}{\sqrt[4]{t(1-t)\,x\,y}}\end{split}

Clearly,
f
and
g
satisfy assumptions
(\mathbf{A}_{6})-(\mathbf{A}_{8})
. Hence, by Theorem
3.2
, the system of SBVPs (
3
) has a positive solution.

4
Singular systems of ODEs with four-point coupled BCs

In this section, we establish the existence of positive solutions for the system of SBVPs (
4
)
asif3
. By a positive solution to the system of SBVPs (
4
), we mean that
(x,y)\in(C[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
,
(x,y)
satisfies (
4
),
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
. For each
x\in C[0,1]
we write
\|x\|=\max_{t\in[0,1]}|x(t)|
. Let

\displaystyle P=\big\{x\in C[0,1]:\min_{t\in[\max\{\xi,\eta\},1]}x(t)\geq\gamma\|x\|\big\},

where

\displaystyle 0<\gamma:=\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\xi,\alpha\beta\xi,\beta\eta,\alpha\beta\eta\}\min\{\xi,\eta,1-\xi,1-\eta\}}{\max\{1,\alpha,\beta,\alpha\beta\xi,\alpha\beta\eta\}}<1.

Clearly,
(C[0,1],\|\cdot\|)
is a Banach space and
P
is a cone of
C[0,1]
. Similarly, for each
(x,y)\in C[0,1]\times C[0,1]
we write
\|(x,y)\|=\|x\|+\|y\|
. Clearly,
(C[0,1]\times C[0,1],\|\cdot\|)
is a Banach space and
P\times P
is a cone of
C[0,1]\times C[0,1]
. For any real constant
r>0
, define
\mathcal{O}_{r}=\left\{(x,y)\in C[0,1]\times C[0,1]:\|(x,y)\|<r\right\}
.

Lemma 4.1

Let
u,v\in C[0,1]
, then the system of BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=u(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=v(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
x(0)&=0,\,x(1)=\alpha y(\xi),\\
y(0)&=0,\,y(1)=\beta x(\eta),\end{split}

(37)

has integral representation

\begin{split}x(t)=&\int_{0}^{1}F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)u(s)ds+\int_{0}^{1}G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)v(s)ds,\\
y(t)=&\int_{0}^{1}F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)v(s)ds+\int_{0}^{1}G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)u(s)ds,\end{split}

(38)

where

F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)=\begin{cases}\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}-\frac{\alpha\beta\xi t(\eta-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}-(t-s),\hskip 11.38092pt&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\,s\leq\eta,\\
\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}-\frac{\alpha\beta\xi t(\eta-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta},\hskip 11.38092pt&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1,\,s\leq\eta,\\
\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}-(t-s),\hskip 11.38092pt&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\,s\geq\eta,\\
\frac{t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta},\hskip 11.38092pt&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1,\,s\geq\eta,\end{cases}

(39)

G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)=\begin{cases}\frac{\alpha\xi t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}-\frac{\alpha t(\xi-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta},\hskip 65.44142pt&0\leq s,t\leq 1,\,s\leq\xi,\\
\frac{\alpha\xi t(1-s)}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta},\hskip 11.38092pt&0\leq s,t\leq 1,\,s\geq\xi.\end{cases}

(40)

Lemma 4.2

The functions
F_{\xi\eta}
and
G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}
satisfies

(i)

F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)\leq\frac{\max\{1,\alpha\beta\xi\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092ptt,s\in[0,1]
,

(ii)

G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)\leq\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092ptt,s\in[0,1]
.

Remark 4.3

In view of Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max\{1,\alpha\beta\eta\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092ptt,s\in[0,1],

\displaystyle G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 29.87547ptt,s\in[0,1].

Lemma 4.4

The functions
F_{\xi\eta}
and
G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}
satisfies

(i)

F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)\geq\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\beta\xi\}\min\{\eta,1-\eta\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092pt(t,s)\in[\eta,1]\times[0,1]
,

(ii)

G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)\geq\frac{\alpha\xi\,\min\{\xi,1-\xi\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092pt(t,s)\in[\xi,1]\times[0,1]
.

Remark 4.5

In view of Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\beta\eta\}\min\{\xi,1-\xi\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 11.38092pt(t,s)\in[\xi,1]\times[0,1],

\displaystyle G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\beta\eta\,\min\{\eta,1-\eta\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}s(1-s),\hskip 54.06006pt(t,s)\in[\eta,1]\times[0,1].

Remark 4.6

From Lemma
4.2
and Remark
4.3
, for
t,s\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)

\displaystyle\leq\mu s(1-s),F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)\leq\mu s(1-s),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)

\displaystyle\leq\mu s(1-s),G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)\leq\mu s(1-s),

where

\mu=\frac{\max\{1,\alpha,\beta,\alpha\beta\xi,\alpha\beta\eta\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}
.
Similarly, from Lemma
4.4
and Remark
4.5
, for

(t,s)\in[\max\{\xi,\eta\},1]\times[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)

\displaystyle\geq\nu s(1-s),F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)\geq\nu s(1-s),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)

\displaystyle\geq\nu s(1-s),G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)\geq\nu s(1-s),

where

\nu=\frac{\min\{1,\alpha\xi,\alpha\beta\xi,\beta\eta,\alpha\beta\eta\}\min\{\xi,\eta,1-\xi,1-\eta\}}{1-\alpha\beta\xi\eta}
.

In view of Lemma
4.1
, the system of BVPs (
4
) can
be expressed as

\begin{split}x(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)f(s,x(s),y(s))ds+\int_{0}^{1}G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)g(s,x(s),y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
y(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)g(s,x(s),y(s))ds+\int_{0}^{1}G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)f(s,x(s),y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(41)

By a solution of the system of BVPs (
4
), we mean a solution of the corresponding system of integral equations (
41
). Define a map
T:P\times P\rightarrow P\times P
by

T(x,y)=(A(x,y),B(x,y)),

(42)

where the maps
A,B:P\times P\rightarrow P
are defined by

\begin{split}A(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}F_{\xi\eta}(t,s)f(s,x(s),y(s))ds+\int_{0}^{1}G_{\alpha\beta\xi\eta}(t,s)g(s,x(s),y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
B(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}F_{\eta\xi}(t,s)g(s,x(s),y(s))ds+\int_{0}^{1}G_{\beta\alpha\eta\xi}(t,s)f(s,x(s),y(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(43)

Clearly, if
(x,y)\in P\times P
is a fixed point of
T
, then

(x,y)
is a solution of the system of BVPs (
4
).

Assume that

(\mathbf{A}_{9})

f(\cdot,1,1),g(\cdot,1,1)\in C((0,1),(0,\infty))
and satisfy

\displaystyle a:=\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)f(t,1,1)dt<+\infty,\hskip 11.38092ptb:=\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)g(t,1,1)dt<+\infty,

(\mathbf{A}_{10})

there exist real constants
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}
with
0\leq\alpha_{i}\leq\beta_{i}<1
,
i=1,2
;

\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}<1
, such that for all
t\in(0,1)
,

x,y\in[0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{\,\beta_{1}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\alpha_{1}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\alpha_{1}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\,\beta_{1}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092ptc\geq 1,\\
c^{\,\beta_{2}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\alpha_{2}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\alpha_{2}}&f(t,x,y)\leq f(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\,\beta_{2}}f(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092ptc\geq 1.\end{split}

(\mathbf{A}_{11})

there exist real constants
\gamma_{i},\rho_{i}
with

0\leq\gamma_{i}\leq\rho_{i}<1
,
i=1,2
;
\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}<1
,
such that for all
t\in(0,1)
,
x,y\in[0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{\,\rho_{1}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\gamma_{1}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\gamma_{1}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,c\,x,y)\leq c^{\,\rho_{1}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092ptc\geq 1,\\
c^{\,\rho_{2}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\gamma_{2}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092pt0<c\leq 1,\\
c^{\gamma_{2}}&g(t,x,y)\leq g(t,x,c\,y)\leq c^{\,\rho_{2}}g(t,x,y),\hskip 11.38092ptc\geq 1.\end{split}

Lemma 4.7

Assume that
(\mathbf{A}_{9})-(\mathbf{A}_{11})
hold. Then the
map

is completely continuous.

Chapter 3
Singular Systems of Second-Order Two-Point Boundary Value Problems

The existence of positive solutions for a nonlinear second-order two-point BVPs has received much attention; see for example the case of regular nonlinearities,
lherbe1
;
lherbe2
;
hendersonwang
;
yli
, and the case of singular nonlinearities, see
rpagarwal
;
AR
;
chu
. However, these results are for the case when nonlinear functions are independent of the first derivative. The BVPs involving the first derivative with regular nonlinear functions can be seen in
guoge
;
johnny
;
gzhang
.

In
(
ao
, Section 2.4)
, Agarwal and O’Regan studied the existence of at least one positive solution for the following BVP with
a=1
and
b=0
,

\begin{split}-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)f(t,y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
a\,y(0)&-b\,y^{\prime}(0)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(44)

where
f:[0,1]\times[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
is continuous and is allowed to be singular at
y^{\prime}=0
;
q\in C(0,1)
and
q>0
on
(0,1)
. The existence of multiple positive solutions for second-order BVPs has also invited attention of many authors,

johnny
;
jiangxu
;
khan2
;
khan
;
zliu
;
thompson
;
liuqiu
;
zhang
;
lishen
;
zhangjiang
.
B. Yan et al.
YRA
have studied the existence of multiple positive solutions of the BVP (
44
) with
a=1
and
b=0
. Further, they generalized these results and established the existence of at least two positive solutions for BVP (
44
) with
a>0
and
b>0
,
YRA1
.

In Sections
5
,
6
,
7
and
8
, we study the existence and multiplicity of positive solutions to the following coupled systems of SBVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,x(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=y(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(45)

and

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,x(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
a_{1}&x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=0,\\
a_{2}&y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(46)

where the functions
f,g:[0,1]\times[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
are continuous and are allowed to be singular at
x^{\prime}=0
,
y^{\prime}=0
. Moreover,
p,q\in C(0,1)
and positive on
(0,1)
, and the real constants
a_{i}\ (i=1,2)>0
,
b_{i}\ (i=1,2)>0
. By singularity of
f
and
g
, we mean that the functions
f(t,x,y)
and
g(t,x,y)
are allowed to be unbounded at
y=0
.

Agarwal and O’Regan
(
ao
, Section 2.10)
have developed the
method of upper and lower solutions for the SBVP

\begin{split}-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)f(t,y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
y(0)&=y(1)=0,\end{split}

(47)

where
f:[0,1]\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

is continuous and singular at
y=0
and the function
q\in C(0,1)

is positive on
(0,1)
. Further, they have presented the method of
upper and lower solutions for more general problems in

oregan
.

In Section
10
, we study the existence of
C^{1}
-positive
solutions for the following system of SBVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&x(0)=x(1)=y(0)=y(1)=0,\end{split}

(48)

where

f_{1},f_{2}:[0,1]\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

are continuous. Moreover,
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
are allowed to change
sign and may be singular at
x=0
,
y=0
. Also,
p_{1},p_{2}\in C(0,1)
are positive on
(0,1)
.

Further in Section
9
, we study more general
coupled system of ODEs and prove the
existence of
C^{1}
-positive solution to the following system of
ODEs subject to two-point coupled BCs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 12.80365ptt\in(0,1),\\
&a_{1}y(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,y^{\prime}(1)=0,\\
&a_{2}x(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(49)

where the nonlinearities
f,g:[0,1]\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)

are continuous and are allowed to be singular at
x^{\prime}=0
,
y^{\prime}=0
.
Moreover,
p,q\in C(0,1)
,
p>0
and
q>0
on
(0,1)
.

5
Existence of
C^{1}
-positive solutions

In this section, we establish sufficient conditions for the existence of
C^{1}
-positive solutions to the system of SBVPs (
45
). By a
C^{1}
-positive solution to the system of SBVPs (
45
), we mean that
(x,y)\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
satisfying (
45
),
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
. For each
x\in C[0,1]\cap C^{1}(0,1]
, we write

\|x\|=\max_{t\in[0,1]}|x(t)|
and
\|x\|_{1}=\sup_{t\in(0,1]}t|x^{\prime}(t)|
. Moreover, for each
x\in\mathcal{E}:=\{x\in C[0,1]\cap C^{1}(0,1]:\|x\|_{1}<+\infty\}
, we write
\|x\|_{2}=\max\{\|x\|,\|x\|_{1}\}
. By Lemma
1.12
,
(\mathcal{E},\|\cdot\|_{2})
is a Banach space. Moreover, for each
x\in C^{1}[0,1]
, we write
\|x\|_{3}=\max\{\|x\|,\|x^{\prime}\|\}
. Clearly,
(C^{1}[0,1],\|\cdot\|_{3})
is a Banach space.

Assume that

(\mathbf{B}_{1})

p,q\in C(0,1)
,
p,q>0
on
(0,1)
,
\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt<+\infty
and
\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt<+\infty
;

(\mathbf{B}_{2})

f,g:[0,1]\times[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
are
continuous with
f(t,x,y)>0
and
g(t,x,y)>0
on

[0,1]\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)
;

(\mathbf{B}_{3})

f(t,x,y)\leq k_{1}(x)(u_{1}(y)+v_{1}(y))
and
g(t,x,y)\leq k_{2}(x)(u_{2}(y)+v_{2}(y))
, where
u_{i}(i=1,2)>0
are continuous
and nonincreasing on
(0,\infty)
,
k_{i}(i=1,2)\geq 0
,
v_{i}(i=1,2)\geq 0
are continuous and nondecreasing on
[0,\infty)
;

(\mathbf{B}_{4})

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds)}

\displaystyle>1,

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds)}

\displaystyle>1,

where
I(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{1}(\tau)+v_{1}(\tau)},\,J(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{2}(\tau)+v_{2}(\tau)}
, for

\mu\in(0,\infty)
;

(\mathbf{B}_{5})

I(\infty)=\infty
and
J(\infty)=\infty
;

(\mathbf{B}_{6})

for real constants
E>0
and
F>0
, there exist continuous functions
\varphi_{EF}
and
\psi_{EF}
defined on

[0,1]
and positive on
(0,1)
, and constants

0\leq\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1
such that

\displaystyle f(t,x,y)\geq\varphi_{EF}(t)x^{\delta_{1}},\,g(t,x,y)\geq\psi_{EF}(t)x^{\delta_{2}}\text{
on }[0,1]\times[0,E]\times[0,F];

(\mathbf{B}_{7})

\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{EF}(s)ds)dt<+\infty
and

\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{EF}(s)ds)dt<+\infty

for any real constant
C>0
.

Remark 5.1

Since
I
,
J
are continuous,
I(0)=0
,
I(\infty)=\infty
,

J(0)=0
,
J(\infty)=\infty
, and they are monotonically increasing.
Hence,
I
and
J
are invertible. Moreover,
I^{-1}
and
J^{-1}

are also monotonically increasing.

Theorem 5.2

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{7})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
45
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Proof

In view of
(\mathbf{B}_{4})
, we can choose real constants

M_{1}>0
and
M_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds)}>1,

\displaystyle\frac{M_{2}}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds)}>1.

From the continuity of
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
,
I
and
J
, choose

\varepsilon>0
small enough such that

\frac{M_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}>1,

(50)

\frac{M_{2}}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))}>1.

(51)

Choose real constants
L_{1}>0
and
L_{2}>0
such that

I(L_{1})>k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon),

(52)

J(L_{2})>k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon).

(53)

Choose
n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that

\frac{1}{n_{0}}<\varepsilon
. For each fixed

n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, define retractions

\theta_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,M_{i}]
and

\rho_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[\frac{1}{n},L_{i}]
by

\displaystyle\theta_{i}(x)=\max\{0,\min\{x,M_{i}\}\}\text{ and
}\rho_{i}(x)=\max\{\frac{1}{n},\min\{x,L_{i}\}\},\,i=1,2.

Consider the modified system of BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,\theta_{2}(y(t)),\rho_{1}(x^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,\theta_{1}(x(t)),\rho_{2}(y^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=y(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n}.\end{split}

(54)

Since

f(t,\theta_{2}(y(t)),\rho_{1}(x^{\prime}(t))),\,g(t,\theta_{1}(x(t)),\rho_{2}(y^{\prime}(t)))

are continuous and bounded on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, by Theorem

1.7
, it follows that the modified system of BVPs
(
54
) has a solution
(x_{n},y_{n})\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
.

Using (
54
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ and }y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ for
}t\in(0,1),

which on integration from
t
to
1
, using
the BCs (
54
), implies that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ and }y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{
for }t\in[0,1].

(55)

Integrating (
55
) from
0
to
t
, using the BCs (
54
), we have

x_{n}(t)\geq\frac{t}{n}\text{ and }y_{n}(t)\geq\frac{t}{n}\text{ for
}t\in[0,1].

(56)

From (
55
) and (
56
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|x_{n}\|=x_{n}(1)\text{ and }\|y_{n}\|=y_{n}(1).

Now, we show that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{1},\,\,y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{2},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(57)

First, we prove
x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{1}
for
t\in[0,1]
. Suppose

x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})\geq L_{1}
for some
t_{1}\in[0,1]
. Using
(
54
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p(t)k_{1}(\theta_{2}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{1}(\rho_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\rho_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))}\leq k_{1}(M_{2})p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating from
t_{1}
to
1
, using the BCs (
54
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho_{1}(z))+v_{1}(\rho_{1}(z))}\leq k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{t_{1}}^{1}p(t)dt,

which can also be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{L_{1}}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}+\int_{L_{1}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(L_{1})+v_{1}(L_{1})}\leq k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt.

Using the
increasing property of
I
, we obtain

\displaystyle I(L_{1})+\frac{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-L_{1}}{u_{1}(L_{1})+v_{1}(L_{1})}\leq k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon),

a
contradiction to (
52
). Hence,
x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{1}
for

t\in[0,1]
. Similarly, we can show that
y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{2}
for
t\in[0,1]
.

Now, we show that

x_{n}(t)<M_{1},\hskip 5.69046pty_{n}(t)<M_{2},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(58)

Suppose
x_{n}(t_{2})\geq M_{1}
for some
t_{2}\in[0,1]
. From
(
54
), (
57
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, it follows
that

\displaystyle-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p(t)k_{1}(\theta_{2}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq q(t)k_{2}(\theta_{1}(x_{n}(t)))(u_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(\theta_{2}(\|y_{n}\|))p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(M_{1})q(t),\hskip 36.98866ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating
from
t
to
1
, using the BCs (
54
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(\theta_{2}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{t}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{y_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{2}(z)+v_{2}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{t}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 36.98866ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

\displaystyle I(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))-I(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(\theta_{2}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle J(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))-J(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 36.98866ptt\in[0,1].

The increasing property of
I
and
J
leads to

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(\theta_{2}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

(59)

y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 36.98866ptt\in[0,1].

(60)

Integrating (
59
) from
0
to
t_{2}
and (
60
)
from
0
to
1
, using the BCs (
54
), we obtain

M_{1}\leq x_{n}(t_{2})\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(\theta_{2}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

(61)

\|y_{n}\|\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)).

(62)

Either we have
\|y_{n}\|<M_{2}
or
\|y_{n}\|\geq M_{2}
. If

\|y_{n}\|<M_{2}
, then from (
61
), we have

M_{1}\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(\|y_{n}\|)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)).

(63)

Now, by using (
62
) in (
63
) and the increasing
property of
k_{1}
and
I^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle M_{1}\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,

a contradiction to (
50
).

On the other hand, if
\|y_{n}\|\geq M_{2}
, then from
(
61
) and (
62
), we have

M_{1}\leq x_{n}(t_{2})\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(M_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

(64)

M_{2}\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)).

(65)

Using (
65
) in (
64
) and the increasing property
of
k_{1}
and
I^{-1}
, leads to

\displaystyle M_{1}\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,

a contradiction to (
50
). Hence,
x_{n}(t)<M_{1}
for

t\in[0,1]
. Similarly, we can show that
y_{n}(t)<M_{2}
for
t\in[0,1]
.

Thus, in view of (
54
)-(
58
),
(x_{n},y_{n})
is a
solution of the following coupled system of BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,x(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=y(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(66)

satisfy

\begin{split}\frac{t}{n}&\leq x_{n}(t)<M_{1},\,\frac{1}{n}\leq x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{1},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
\frac{t}{n}&\leq y_{n}(t)<M_{2},\,\frac{1}{n}\leq y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{2},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(67)

Now, in view of
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
, there exist continuous
functions
\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}
and
\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}
defined on

[0,1]
and positive on
(0,1)
, and real constants

0\leq\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1
such that

\begin{split}f(t,y_{n}(t),x_{n}^{\prime}(t))\geq\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(t)(y_{n}(t))^{\delta_{1}},\hskip 11.38092pt(t,y_{n}(t),x_{n}^{\prime}(t))\in[0,1]\times[0,M_{2}]\times[0,L_{1}],\\
g(t,x_{n}(t),y_{n}^{\prime}(t))\geq\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(t)(x_{n}(t))^{\delta_{2}},\hskip 11.38092pt(t,x_{n}(t),y_{n}^{\prime}(t))\in[0,1]\times[0,M_{1}]\times[0,L_{2}].\end{split}

(68)

We claim that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{2}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds,

(69)

y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{1}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds,

(70)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{1}=&\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}},\\
