C_{2}=&\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.\end{split}

To prove (
69
), consider the following relation

x_{n}(t)=\frac{t}{n}+\int_{0}^{t}sp(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds+\int_{t}^{1}tp(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds,

(71)

which implies that

\displaystyle x_{n}(1)\geq\int_{0}^{1}sp(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using (
68
) and Lemma
1.18
, we obtain

x_{n}(1)\geq(y_{n}(1))^{\delta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds.

(72)

Similarly, using (
68
) and Lemma
1.18
, we
obtain

\displaystyle y_{n}(1)\geq(x_{n}(1))^{\delta_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds,

which in view of (
72
) implies that

\displaystyle y_{n}(1)\geq(y_{n}(1))^{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds\right)^{\delta_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds.

Hence,

y_{n}(1)\geq C_{2}.

(73)

Now, from (
71
), it follows that

\displaystyle x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{1}p(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using (
68
), Lemma
1.18
and (
73
), we
obtain (
69
). Similarly, we can prove (
70
).

Now, using (
66
),
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, (
67
),
(
69
) and (
70
), we have

\begin{split}0\leq-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq k_{1}(M_{2})p(t)(u_{1}(C_{2}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds)+v_{1}(L_{1})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
0\leq-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq k_{2}(M_{1})q(t)(u_{2}(C_{1}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds)+v_{2}(L_{2})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).\end{split}

(74)

In view of (
67
), (
74
),
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{7})
, it follows that the sequences

\{(x_{n}^{(j)},y_{n}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are uniformly bounded and
equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Hence, by Theorem
1.5
, there
exist subsequences
\{(x_{n_{k}}^{(j)},y_{n_{k}}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)

of
\{(x_{n}^{(j)},y_{n}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
and
(x,y)\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
such that

(x_{n_{k}}^{(j)},y_{n_{k}}^{(j)})
converges uniformly to

(x^{(j)},y^{(j)})
on
[0,1]

(j=0,1)
. Also,

x(0)=y(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0
. Moreover, from (
69
) and
(
70
), with
n_{k}
in place of
n
and taking

\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow+\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{2}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{M_{2}L_{1}}(s)ds,

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{1}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{M_{1}L_{2}}(s)ds,

which shows that
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
,
x>0
and
y>0
on

(0,1]
. Further,
(x_{n_{k}},y_{n_{k}})
satisfy

\displaystyle x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p(s)f(s,y_{n_{k}}(s),x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}q(s)g(s,x_{n_{k}}(s),y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

Passing to the limit as
n_{k}\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p(s)f(s,y(s),x^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}q(s)g(s,x(s),y^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,x(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Hence,
(x,y)
is a
C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of SBVPs (
45
).

Example 5.3

Consider the following coupled system of SBVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-t)^{-\frac{2}{3}}(y(t))^{\frac{1}{3}}(x^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{1}},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=t^{-\frac{2}{3}}(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{3}}(x(t))^{\frac{2}{3}}(y^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{2}},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&x(0)=y(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(75)

where
0<\beta_{1}<1
and
0<\beta_{2}<\frac{1}{2}
.

Choose
p(t)=t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-t)^{-\frac{2}{3}}
,
q(t)=t^{-\frac{2}{3}}(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{3}}
,
k_{1}(x)=x^{\frac{1}{3}}
,
k_{2}(x)=x^{\frac{2}{3}}
,
u_{1}(x)=x^{-\beta_{1}}
,
u_{2}(x)=x^{-\beta_{2}}
and
v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)=0
.

Then,
I(z)=\frac{z^{\beta_{1}+1}}{\beta_{1}+1}
,
J(z)=\frac{z^{\beta_{2}+1}}{\beta_{2}+1}
,
I^{-1}(z)=(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}z^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}
and

J^{-1}(z)=(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}z^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}
.

Also,
\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt=\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt=\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{3}}
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds)}=

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{3}})^{\frac{3\beta_{2}+4}{3(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{3(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}c^{\frac{2}{9(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}}=\infty,

and

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds)}=

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{3}})^{\frac{3\beta_{1}+5}{3(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{2}{3(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}c^{\frac{2}{9(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}}=\infty.

Clearly,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{5})
are satisfied.
For
\delta_{1}=\frac{1}{3}
,
\delta_{2}=\frac{2}{3}
,

\varphi_{EF}(t)=F^{-\beta_{1}}
and
\psi_{EF}(t)=F^{-\beta_{2}}
,
then
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
holds. Further,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{EF}(s)ds)dt

\displaystyle=3^{-\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}F^{\beta_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-t)^{-\frac{\beta_{1}+2}{3}}dt

\displaystyle=3^{-\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}F^{\beta_{1}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})\Gamma(\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{3})}{\Gamma(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{3})},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{EF}(s)ds)dt

\displaystyle=(\frac{3}{2})^{-\beta_{2}}C^{-\beta_{2}}F^{\beta_{2}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}t^{-\frac{2}{3}}(1-t)^{-\frac{2\beta_{2}+1}{3}}dt

\displaystyle=(\frac{3}{2})^{-\beta_{2}}C^{-\beta_{2}}F^{\beta_{2}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})\Gamma(\frac{1-2\beta_{2}}{3})}{\Gamma(1-\frac{2\beta_{2}}{3})},

shows that
(\mathbf{B}_{7})
also holds. Since,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{7})
are satisfied. Therefore, by Theorem
5.2
, the system of BVPs (
75
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

6
Existence of at least two positive
solutions

In this section, we establish sufficient conditions for the
existence of at least two positive solutions of the system of SBVPs (
45
). By a positive solution
(x,y)
of the
system of BVPs (
45
), we mean that

(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
satisfies (
45
),
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1]
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on

[0,1)
. Define a cone
P
of
\mathcal{E}
by

\displaystyle P=\{x\in\mathcal{E}:x(t)\geq t\|x\|\text{ for all }t\in[0,1],x(1)\geq\|x\|_{1}\}.

For each

(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
we write

\|(x,y)\|_{4}=\|x\|_{2}+\|y\|_{2}
. Clearly,

(\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E},\|\cdot\|_{4})
is a Banach space and

P\times P
is a cone of
\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
. We define a
partial ordering in
\mathcal{E}
, by
x\leq y
if and only if

x(t)\leq y(t)
,
t\in[0,1]
. We define a partial ordering in

\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
, by
(x_{1},y_{1})\preceq(x_{2},y_{2})
if and only if
x_{1}\leq x_{2}
and
y_{1}\leq y_{2}
. For any real constant
r>0
, we define an open neighborhood
of
(0,0)\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{r}=\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}:\|(x,y)\|_{4}<r\}.

In view of
(\mathbf{B}_{4})
, there exist real constants

R_{1}>0
and
R_{2}>0
such that

\frac{R_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{1})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds)}>1,

(76)

\frac{R_{2}}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{2})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds)}>1.

(77)

From the continuity of
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
,
I
and
J
, we choose

\varepsilon>0
small enough such that

\frac{R_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}>1,

(78)

\frac{R_{2}}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))}>1.

(79)

Choose

n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that
\frac{1}{n_{0}}<\varepsilon
and
for each fixed
n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, consider the system
of non-singular BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,y(t)+\frac{t}{n},|x^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,x(t)+\frac{t}{n},|y^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x(0)&=x^{\prime}(1)=y(0)=y^{\prime}(1)=0.\end{split}

(80)

We write (
80
) as an equivalent system of integral equations

\begin{split}x(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
y(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)q(s)f(s,x(s)+\frac{s}{n},|y^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

(81)

where the Green’s function is defined as

\displaystyle G(t,s)=\begin{cases}s,\,&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\\
t,\,&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1.\end{cases}

By a solution of the system of BVPs (
80
), we mean a solution of the corresponding system of integral equations (
81
).

Define a map
T_{n}:\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}

by

T_{n}(x,y)=(A_{n}(x,y),B_{n}(x,y)),

(82)

where the maps

A_{n},B_{n}:\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
are
defined by

\begin{split}A_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
B_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)q(s)f(s,x(s)+\frac{s}{n},|y^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(83)

Clearly, if

(x_{n},y_{n})\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
is a fixed point of

T_{n}
; then
(x_{n},y_{n})
is a solution of the system of BVPs
(
80
).

Assume that

(\mathbf{B}_{8})

for any real constant
E>0
, there exist continuous functions
\varphi_{E}
and
\psi_{E}
defined on

[0,1]
and positive on
(0,1)
, and constants

0\leq\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1
such that

\displaystyle f(t,x,y)\geq\varphi_{E}(t)x^{\delta_{1}},\,g(t,x,y)\geq\psi_{E}(t)x^{\delta_{2}}\text{
on }[0,1]\times[0,E]\times[0,\infty);

(\mathbf{B}_{9})

for any real constant
C>0
,
\int_{0}^{1}p(t)v_{1}(\frac{C}{t})dt<+\infty
,
\int_{0}^{1}q(t)v_{2}(\frac{C}{t})dt<+\infty
,

\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{E}(s)ds)dt<+\infty
and

\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{E}(s)ds)dt<+\infty
.

Lemma 6.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{9})
hold. Then the map
T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)\rightarrow P\times P
is completely continuous.

Proof

Firstly, we show that
T_{n}(P\times P)\subseteq P\times P
. For
(x,y)\in P\times P
,
t\in[0,1]
, using (
83
) and
Lemma
1.15
, we obtain

\begin{split}&A_{n}(x,y)(t)=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\geq t\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G(\tau,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds=t\|A_{n}(x,y)\|\end{split}

(84)

and

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x,y)\|_{1}=\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(\tau)|=\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau\int_{\tau}^{1}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\max_{t\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\leq A_{n}(x,y)(1).\end{split}

(85)

From (
84
) and
(
85
),
A_{n}(x,y)\in P
for every
(x,y)\in P\times P
,
that is,
A_{n}(P\times P)\subseteq P
. Similarly, by using
(
83
) and Lemma
1.15
, we can show that

B_{n}(P\times P)\subseteq P
. Hence,
T_{n}(P\times P)\subseteq P\times P
.

Now, we show that
T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)\rightarrow P\times P
is uniformly bounded. For any

(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)
, using
(
83
),
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, Lemma
1.13
,

(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{9})
, we have

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x,y)\|=\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{\|x\|_{2}}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{r}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}((r+\frac{1}{n})\frac{1}{s}))ds<+\infty.\end{split}

(86)

Also, for
(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)
, using
(
83
), Lemma
1.15
and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x,y)\|_{1}=\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(\tau)|=\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau\int_{\tau}^{1}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\max_{t\in[0,1]}\int_{t}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds.\end{split}

Now, using Lemma
1.13
,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{9})
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x,y)\|_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{\|x\|_{2}}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{r}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}((r+\frac{1}{n})\frac{1}{s}))ds<+\infty.\end{split}

(87)

From (
86
) and (
87
), it follows that

A_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is uniformly
bounded under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{2}
. Similarly, by using
(
83
), Lemma
1.13
, Lemma
1.15
,

(\mathbf{B}_{1})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{9})
,
we can show that
B_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))

is uniformly bounded under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{2}
. Hence,

T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is uniformly
bounded.

Now, we show that
T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))

is equicontinuous. For

(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)
,

t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
, using (
83
),
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
and
Lemma
1.13
, we have

\begin{split}&|A_{n}(x,y)(t_{1})-A_{n}(x,y)(t_{2})|=\left|\int_{0}^{1}(G(t_{1},s)-G(t_{2},s))p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}|G(t_{1},s)-G(t_{2},s)|p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}|G(t_{1},s)-G(t_{2},s)|p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{0}^{1}|G(t_{1},s)-G(t_{2},s)|p(s)(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{\|x\|_{2}}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{0}^{1}|G(t_{1},s)-G(t_{2},s)|p(s)(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\frac{1}{s})ds,\end{split}

(88)

and

\begin{split}&|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(t_{1})-A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(t_{2})|=\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{s}{n})(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{\|x\|_{2}}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)\\
&(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{r}{s}+\frac{1}{n}))ds\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}((r+\frac{1}{n})\frac{1}{s}))ds.\end{split}

(89)

From (
88
), (
89
),
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{9})
, it follows that

A_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is equicontinuous
under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{3}
. But, the norm
\|\cdot\|_{3}
is
equivalent to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{2}
. Hence,

A_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is equicontinuous
under
\|\cdot\|_{2}
.

Similarly, using (
83
),
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
and Lemma

1.13
, we can show that

B_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is equicontinuous
under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{2}
. Consequently,

T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is
equicontinuous. Hence, by Theorem
1.5
,

T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P))
is relatively
compact which implies that
T_{n}
is a compact map.

Now, we show that
T_{n}
is continuous. Let

(x_{m},y_{m}),(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)

such that
\|(x_{m},y_{m})-(x,y)\|_{4}\rightarrow 0
as

m\rightarrow+\infty
. Using
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
and Lemma

1.13
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|f(t,y_{m}(t)+\frac{t}{n},|x_{m}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|\leq k_{1}(y_{m}(t)+\frac{t}{n})(u_{1}(|x_{m}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x_{m}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}))\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{\|x_{m}\|_{2}}{t}+\frac{1}{n}))\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(\frac{r}{t}+\frac{1}{n}))\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}((r+\frac{1}{n})\frac{1}{t})).\end{split}

Using (
83
) and Lemma
1.15
, we have

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|=\\
&\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)(f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\left|f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds\end{split}

(90)

and

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})^{\prime}-A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}\|_{1}=\\
&\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau\left|\int_{\tau}^{1}p(s)(f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\right|\\
&\leq\max_{t\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)\left|f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\left|f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds.\end{split}

(91)

From (
90
) and (
91
), using the Lebesgue dominated
convergence theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|\rightarrow 0,\hskip 5.69046pt\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})^{\prime}-A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}\|_{1}\rightarrow 0\text{
as }m\rightarrow+\infty.

Hence,

\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
as

m\rightarrow\infty
.

Similarly, we can show that

\|B_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-B_{n}(x,y)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0
as

m\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,

\|T_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-T_{n}(x,y)\|_{4}\rightarrow 0
as

m\rightarrow+\infty
, that is,

T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)\rightarrow P\times P
is continuous. Hence,

T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P\times P)\rightarrow P\times P
is completely continuous.

Assume that

(\mathbf{B}_{10})

there exist
h_{1},h_{2}\in C([0,\infty)\times(0,\infty),[0,\infty))
with
f(t,x,y)\geq h_{1}(x,y)
and
g(t,x,y)\geq h_{2}(x,y)
on
[0,1]\times[0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{x\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{h_{i}(x,y)}{x}

\displaystyle=+\infty,\text{ uniformly for }\,y\in(0,\infty),\,i=1,2.

Theorem 6.2

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{5})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{8})-(\mathbf{B}_{10})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
45
) has at least two positive solutions.

Proof

Let
R_{0}=R_{1}+R_{2}
and define

\mathcal{O}_{R_{0}}=\Omega_{R_{1}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}}
where

\displaystyle\Omega_{R_{1}}=\{x\in E:\|x\|_{2}<R_{1}\},\,\Omega_{R_{2}}=\{x\in E:\|x\|_{2}<R_{2}\}.

We claim that

(x,y)\neq\lambda T_{n}(x,y),\text{ for }\lambda\in(0,1],(x,y)\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R_{0}}\cap(P\times P).

(92)

Suppose there exist

(x_{0},y_{0})\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R}\cap(P\times P)
and

\lambda_{0}\in(0,1]
such that
(x_{0},y_{0})=\lambda_{0}T_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})
. Then,

\begin{split}-x_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=\lambda_{0}p(t)f(t,y_{0}(t)+\frac{t}{n},|x_{0}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=\lambda_{0}q(t)g(t,x_{0}(t)+\frac{t}{n},|y_{0}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
x_{0}(0)&=x_{0}^{\prime}(1)=y_{0}(0)=y_{0}^{\prime}(1)=0.\end{split}

(93)

From (
93
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
, we have
x_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
and
y_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
on
(0,1)
, integrating from
t
to
1
,
using the BCs (
93
), we obtain
x_{0}^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
and

y_{0}^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
for
t\in[0,1]
. From (
93
) and

(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p(t)k_{1}(y_{0}(t)+\frac{t}{n})(u_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq q(t)k_{2}(x_{0}(t)+\frac{t}{n})(u_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})}\leq p(t)k_{1}(y_{0}(t)+\frac{t}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})}\leq q(t)k_{2}(x_{0}(t)+\frac{t}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)q(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating from
t
to
1
, using the BCs (
93
), we obtain

\displaystyle I(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})-I(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{t}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle J(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})-J(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{t}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

\displaystyle x_{0}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{0}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which on integration from
0
to
1
, using the BCs (
93
)
and Lemma
1.14
, leads to

R_{1}\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

(94)

R_{2}\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)).

(95)

Now, using (
95
) in (
94
) together with increasing
property of
k_{1}
and
I^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{R_{1}}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,

a
contradiction to (
78
). Similarly, using (
94
) in
(
95
) together with increasing property of
k_{2}
and

J^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{R_{2}}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,

a
contradiction to (
79
). Hence, (
92
) is true and by
Lemma
1.8
, the fixed point index

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T_{n},\mathcal{O}_{R_{0}}\cap(P\times P),P\times P)=1.

(96)

Now, choose a
t_{0}\in(0,1)
and define

N_{1}=\left(t_{0}\min_{t\in[t_{0},1]}\int_{t_{0}}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)ds\right)^{-1}+1\text{
and
}N_{2}=\left(t_{0}\min_{t\in[t_{0},1]}\int_{t_{0}}^{1}G(t,s)q(s)ds\right)^{-1}+1.

(97)

By
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
, there exist real constants with

R_{1}^{*}>R_{1}
and
R_{2}^{*}>R_{2}
such that

\begin{split}h_{1}(x,y)&\geq N_{1}x,\text{ for }x\geq R_{1}^{*},y\in(0,\infty),\\
h_{2}(x,y)&\geq N_{2}x,\text{ for }x\geq R_{2}^{*},y\in(0,\infty).\end{split}

(98)

Let

R^{*}=\frac{R_{1}^{*}+R_{2}^{*}}{t_{0}}
and define

\mathcal{O}_{R^{*}}=\Omega_{R_{1}^{*}}\times\Omega_{R_{2}^{*}}
,
where

\displaystyle\Omega_{R_{1}^{*}}=\{x\in E:\|x\|_{2}<\frac{R_{1}^{*}}{t_{0}}\},\,\Omega_{R_{2}^{*}}=\{x\in E:\|x\|_{2}<\frac{R_{2}^{*}}{t_{0}}\}.

We show that

T_{n}(x,y)\npreceq(x,y),\text{ for }(x,y)\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R^{*}}\cap(P\times P).

(99)

Suppose
T_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})\preceq(x_{0},y_{0})
for some

(x_{0},y_{0})\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R^{*}}\cap(P\times P)
. Then,

x_{0}(t)\geq A_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})(t)\text{ and }y_{0}(t)\geq B_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})(t)\text{ for }t\in[0,1].

(100)

By Lemma
1.14
, we have

\displaystyle x_{0}(t)\geq t\|x_{0}\|\geq t_{0}\|x_{0}\|_{2}=t_{0}\frac{R_{1}^{*}}{t_{0}}=R_{1}^{*}\text{ for
}t\in[t_{0},1].

Similarly,
y_{0}(t)\geq R_{2}^{*}
for

t\in[t_{0},1]
. Hence,

|x_{0}(t)|+\frac{t}{n}\geq R_{1}^{*}\text{
and }|y_{0}(t)|+\frac{t}{n}\geq R_{2}^{*}\text{ for
}t\in[t_{0},1].

Now, using (
100
), (
98
) and

(\mathbf{B}_{10})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&x_{0}(t)\geq A_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})(t)\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y_{0}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{0}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\geq\int_{t_{0}}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)h_{1}(y_{0}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{0}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\geq\int_{t_{0}}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)N_{1}(y_{0}(s)+\frac{s}{n})ds\\
&\geq\int_{t_{0}}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)dsN_{1}R_{2}^{*}\\
&\geq\min_{t\in[t_{0},1]}\int_{t_{0}}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)dsN_{1}R_{2}^{*}\\
&>\frac{R_{2}^{*}}{t_{0}},\end{split}

which implies that

\|x_{0}\|_{2}=\|x_{0}\|>\frac{R_{2}^{*}}{t_{0}}
. Similarly, using
(
98
), (
100
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
, we have

\|y_{0}\|_{2}>\frac{R_{1}^{*}}{t_{0}}
. Consequently, it follows
that,
\|(x_{0},y_{0})\|_{4}=\|x_{0}\|_{2}+\|y_{0}\|_{2}>R^{*}
, a
contradiction. Hence, (
99
) is true and by Lemma

1.10
, the fixed point index

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T_{n},\mathcal{O}_{R^{*}}\cap(P\times P),P\times P)=0.

(101)

From (
96
) and (
101
), it follows that

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T_{n},(\mathcal{O}_{R^{*}}\setminus\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{R})\cap(P\times P),P\times P)=-1.

(102)

Thus, in view of (
96
) and (
102
), there exist

(x_{n,1},y_{n,1})\in\mathcal{O}_{R}\cap(P\times P)
and

(x_{n,2},y_{n,2})\in(\mathcal{O}_{R^{*}}\setminus\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{R})\cap(P\times P)
such that
(x_{n,j},y_{n,j})=T_{n}(x_{n,j},y_{n,j}),\,(j=1,2)

which implies that

\begin{split}x_{n,j}(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(t,y_{n,j}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{n,j}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
y_{n,j}(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)q(s)g(s,x_{n,j}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|y_{n,j}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\,j=1,2.\end{split}

(103)

Using
(\mathbf{B}_{8})
there exist continuous functions

\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}
and
\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}
defined
on
[0,1]
and positive on
(0,1)
and real constants

0\leq\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1
such that

\begin{split}f(t,x,y)&\geq\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(t)x^{\delta_{1}},\hskip 11.38092pt(t,x,y)\in[0,1]\times[0,R_{2}+\varepsilon]\times[0,\infty),\\
g(t,x,y)&\geq\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(t)x^{\delta_{2}},\hskip 11.38092pt(t,x,y)\in[0,1]\times[0,R_{1}+\varepsilon]\times[0,\infty).\end{split}

(104)

By the definition of
P
, we have
x_{n,1}(t)\geq t\|x_{n,1}\|
and

y_{n,1}(t)\geq t\|y_{n,1}\|
for
t\in[0,1]
. We show that

x_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{4}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

(105)

y_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{3}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

(106)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{3}&=\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}},\\
C_{4}&=\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.\end{split}

In order to prove (
105
), using (
103
) and
(
104
), we consider

\displaystyle\begin{split}x_{n,1}(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y_{n,1}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{n,1}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\geq\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)(y_{n,1}(s)+\frac{s}{n})^{\delta_{1}}ds\\
&\geq\|y_{n,1}\|^{\delta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,\end{split}

which shows that

\|x_{n,1}\|\geq\|y_{n,1}\|^{\delta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds.

(107)

Similarly, from (
103
) and (
104
), we have

\|y_{n,1}\|\geq\|x_{n,1}\|^{\delta_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds.

(108)

Using (
108
) in (
107
), we have

\displaystyle\|y_{n,1}\|\geq\left(\|y_{n,1}\|^{\delta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\delta_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,

which implies that

\|y_{n,1}\|\geq\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}+1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}+1}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}=C_{4}.

(109)

Using (
104
) and (
109
) in the following relation

\displaystyle x_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{1}p(s)f(s,y_{n,1}(s)+\frac{s}{n},|x_{n,1}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,

we obtain (
105
). Similarly, we can prove (
106
).

Now, differentiating (
103
), using
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,
(
105
), (
106
) and Lemma
1.13
, we have

\begin{split}0\leq-x_{n,1}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq p(t)k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)(u_{1}(C_{4}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds)+v_{1}(\frac{R_{1}+1}{t})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
0\leq-y_{n,1}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq q(t)k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)(u_{2}(C_{3}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds)+v_{2}(\frac{R_{2}+1}{t})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).\end{split}

(110)

Integration from
t
to
1
, using the BCs (
80
), leads to

\displaystyle x_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{t}^{1}p(s)(u_{1}(C_{4}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{s}^{1}\tau^{\delta_{1}}p(\tau)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(\tau)d\tau)+v_{1}(\frac{R_{1}+1}{s}))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{t}^{1}q(s)(u_{2}(C_{3}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{s}^{1}\tau^{\delta_{2}}q(\tau)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(\tau)d\tau)+v_{2}(\frac{R_{2}+1}{s}))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

\begin{split}x_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)&\leq k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)(u_{1}(C_{4}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{s}^{1}\tau^{\delta_{1}}p(\tau)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(\tau)d\tau)+v_{1}(\frac{R_{1}+1}{s}))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
y_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)&\leq k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)(u_{2}(C_{3}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{s}^{1}\tau^{\delta_{2}}q(\tau)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(\tau)d\tau)+v_{2}(\frac{R_{2}+1}{s}))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(111)

In view of (
105
), (
106
), (
111
),
(
110
),
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{9})
, the
sequences
\{(x_{n,1}^{(j)},y_{n,1}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are
uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Thus, by Theorem

1.5
, there exist subsequences

\{(x_{n_{k},1}^{(j)},y_{n_{k},1}^{(j)})\}\,(j=0,1)
of

\{(x_{n,1}^{(j)},y_{n,1}^{(j)})\}
and functions
(x_{0,1},y_{0,1})\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
such that
(x_{n_{k},1}^{(j)},y_{n_{k},1}^{(j)})

converges uniformly to
(x_{0,1}^{(j)},y_{0,1}^{(j)})
on
[0,1]
. Also,
x_{0,1}(0)=y_{0,1}(0)=x_{0,1}^{\prime}(1)=y_{0,1}^{\prime}(1)=0
. Moreover, from
(
105
) and (
106
), with
n_{k}
in place of
n
and taking
\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow+\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle x_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\geq C_{4}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,

\displaystyle y_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\geq C_{3}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,

which implies that
x_{0,1}^{\prime}>0
and
y_{0,1}^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
,

x_{0,1}>0
and
y_{0,1}>0
on
(0,1]
. Further,

\begin{split}\left|f(t,y_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{t}{n},x_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})\right|&\leq k_{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon)(u_{1}(C_{4}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{R_{2}+\varepsilon}(s)ds)+v_{1}(\frac{R_{1}+1}{t})),\\
\left|g(t,x_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{t}{n},y_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})\right|&\leq k_{2}(R_{1}+\varepsilon)(u_{2}(C_{3}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}(s)ds)+v_{2}(\frac{R_{2}+1}{t})),\end{split}

(112)

\begin{split}\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow\infty}f(t,y_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{t}{n_{k}},x_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})&=f(t,y_{0,1}(t),x_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1],\\
\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow\infty}g(t,x_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{t}{n_{k}},y_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})&=g(t,x_{0,1}(t),y_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1].\end{split}

(113)

Moreover,
(x_{n_{k},1},y_{n_{k},1})

satisfies

\displaystyle x_{n_{k},1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y_{n_{k},1}(s)+\frac{s}{n_{k}},x_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{n_{k},1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)q(s)g(s,x_{n_{k},1}(s)+\frac{s}{n_{k}},y_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which in view of (
112
),
(\mathbf{B}_{9})
, (
113
), the
Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and taking

\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow+\infty}
, leads to

\displaystyle x_{0,1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)p(s)f(s,y_{0,1}(s),x_{0,1}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{0,1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G(t,s)q(s)g(s,x_{0,1}(s),y_{0,1}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that
(x_{0,1},y_{0,1})\in C^{2}(0,1)\times C^{2}(0,1)
and

\displaystyle-x_{0,1}^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,y_{0,1}(t),x_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y_{0,1}^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,x_{0,1}(t),y_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Moreover, by (
76
) and (
77
), we have

\|x_{0,1}\|_{2}<R_{1}
and
\|y_{0,1}\|_{2}<R_{2}
, that is,

\|(x_{0,1},y_{0,1})\|_{3}<R_{0}
. By a similar proof the sequence

\{(x_{n,2},y_{n,2})\}
has a convergent subsequence

\{(x_{n_{k},2},y_{n_{k},2})\}
converging uniformly to

(x_{0,2},y_{0,2})\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}
on
[0,1]
.
Moreover,
(x_{0,2},y_{0,2})
is a solution to the system (
45
) with
x_{0,2}>0
and
y_{0,2}>0
on

(0,1]
,
x^{\prime}_{0,2}>0
and
y^{\prime}_{0,2}>0
on
[0,1)
,

R_{0}<\|(x_{0,2},y_{0,2})\|_{4}<R^{*}
.

Example 6.3

Consider the following coupled system of SBVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=\mu_{1}(1+(y(t))^{\delta_{1}}+(y(t))^{\eta_{1}})(1+(x^{\prime}(t))^{\alpha_{1}}+(x^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{1}}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=\mu_{2}(1+(x(t))^{\delta_{2}}+(x(t))^{\eta_{2}})(1+(y^{\prime}(t))^{\alpha_{2}}+(y^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{2}}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&x(0)=y(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(114)

where
0\leq\delta_{i}<1
,
\eta_{i}>1
,
0<\alpha_{i}<1
,

0<\beta_{i}<1
, and
\mu_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
.

Choose
p(t)=\mu_{1}
,
q(t)=\mu_{2}
,
k_{i}(x)=1+x^{\delta_{i}}+x^{\eta_{i}}
,
u_{i}(x)=x^{-\beta_{i}}
and
v_{i}(x)=1+x^{\alpha_{i}}
,
i=1,2
. Also,
\varphi_{E}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\psi_{E}(t)=\mu_{2}
and
h_{i}(x,y)=\mu_{i}(1+x^{\eta_{i}})
,
i=1,2
.

Assume that
\mu_{1}
is arbitrary real constant and

\displaystyle\mu_{2}<\min\{\inf_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{J(c)}{k_{2}(I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(c)))},\,\inf_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{J((\mu_{1}^{-1}I(c))^{\delta_{1}^{-1}})}{k_{2}(c)},\,\inf_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{J((\mu_{1}^{-1}I(c))^{\eta_{1}^{-1}})}{k_{2}(c)}\}.

Then,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{I^{-1}(k_{1}(J^{-1}(k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds)}\\
&=\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c))))}\\
&\geq\frac{c}{I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c))))},\hskip 130.88284ptc\in(0,\infty)\\
&=\frac{c}{I^{-1}(\mu_{1}(1+(J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c)))^{\delta_{1}}+(J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c)))^{\eta_{1}}))},\hskip 28.45274ptc\in(0,\infty)\\
&>1,\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{J^{-1}(k_{2}(I^{-1}(k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds))\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds)}\\
&=\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(c))))}\\
&=\frac{c}{J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(c))))},\hskip 39.83368ptc\in(0,\infty)\\
&>1.\end{split}

Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}p(t)v_{1}(\frac{C}{t})dt

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}(1+\frac{C^{\alpha_{1}}}{1-\alpha_{1}})<+\infty,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{E}(s)ds)dt

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{1}^{1-2\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}(\delta_{1}+1)^{\beta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-\beta_{1}}dt

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}^{1-2\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}(\delta_{1}+1)^{\beta_{1}}(1-\beta_{1})^{-1}<+\infty,\text{
etc.}

Also,

\displaystyle\lim_{x\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{h_{i}(x,y)}{x}=\lim_{x\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{\mu_{i}(1+x^{\eta_{i}})}{x}=+\infty,\,i=1,2.

Clearly,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{5})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{8})-(\mathbf{B}_{10})
are satisfied. Hence, by
Theorem
6.2
, the system of BVPs (
114
) has at least
two positive solutions.

7
Existence of
C^{1}
-positive solutions with more general BCs

In this section, we study the system of BVPs (
46
) and establish sufficient conditions for the existence
of
C^{1}
-positive solutions.
By a
C^{1}
-positive solution to the system of BVPs (
46
), we mean
(x,y)\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
satisfying (
46
),
x>0
and
y>0
on
[0,1]
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
.

Assume that

(\mathbf{B}_{11})

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)}&>1,\\
\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}&>1,\end{split}

where
I(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{1}(\tau)+v_{1}(\tau)},\,J(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{2}(\tau)+v_{2}(\tau)}
, for

\mu\in(0,\infty)
;

(\mathbf{B}_{12})

\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{EF}(s)ds)dt<+\infty
and

\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{EF}(s)ds)dt<+\infty

for any real constant
C>0
.

Theorem 7.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{5})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{12})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
46
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Proof

In view of
(\mathbf{B}_{11})
, we can choose real constants

M_{3}>0
and
M_{4}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{3}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)}>1,

\displaystyle\frac{M_{4}}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}>1.

From the continuity of
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
,
I
and
J
, we choose

\varepsilon>0
small enough such that

\frac{M_{3}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon))}>1,

(115)

\frac{M_{4}}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt+J(\varepsilon))}>1.

(116)

Choose real constants
L_{3}>0
and
L_{4}>0
such that

I(L_{3})>k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon),

(117)

J(L_{4})>k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt+J(\varepsilon).

(118)

Choose
n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that

\frac{1}{n_{0}}<\varepsilon
. For each fixed

n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, define retractions

\theta_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,M_{i}]
and

\rho_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[\frac{1}{n},L_{i}]
by

\displaystyle\theta_{i}(x)=\max\{0,\min\{x,M_{i}\}\}\text{ and }\rho_{i}(x)=\max\{\frac{1}{n},\min\{x,L_{i}\}\},i=3,4.

Consider the modified system of BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,\theta_{4}(y(t)),\rho_{3}(x^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,\theta_{3}(x(t)),\rho_{4}(y^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
a_{1}x(&0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n},\\
a_{2}y(&0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,y^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n}.\end{split}

(119)

Since
f(t,\theta_{4}(y(t)),\rho_{3}(x^{\prime}(t)))
,

g(t,\theta_{3}(x(t)),\rho_{4}(y^{\prime}(t)))
are continuous and bounded
on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, by Theorem
1.7
, it follows that
the modified system of BVPs (
119
) has a solution

(x_{n},y_{n})\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
.

Using (
119
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ and }y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ for }t\in(0,1),

which on integration from
t
to
1
, and using the BCs
(
119
), yields

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ and }y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ for }t\in[0,1].

(120)

Integrating (
120
) from
0
to
t
, using the BCs
(
119
) and (
120
), we have

x_{n}(t)\geq(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}\text{ and }y_{n}(t)\geq(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}\text{ for }t\in[0,1].

(121)

From (
120
) and (
121
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|x_{n}\|=x_{n}(1)\text{ and }\|y_{n}\|=y_{n}(1).

Now, we show that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{3},\,y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{4},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(122)

First, we prove
x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{3}
for
t\in[0,1]
. Suppose

x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})\geq L_{3}
for some
t_{1}\in[0,1]
. Using
(
119
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p(t)k_{1}(\theta_{4}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{1}(\rho_{3}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho_{3}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\rho_{3}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho_{3}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))}\leq k_{1}(M_{4})p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating from
t_{1}
to
1
, using the BCs (
119
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho_{3}(z))+v_{1}(\rho_{3}(z))}\leq k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{t_{1}}^{1}p(t)dt,

which can also be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{L_{3}}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho_{3}(z))+v_{1}(\rho_{3}(z))}+\int_{L_{3}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho_{3}(z))+v_{1}(\rho_{3}(z))}\leq k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt.

Using the
increasing property of
I
, we obtain

\displaystyle I(L_{3})+\frac{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-L_{3}}{u_{1}(L_{3})+v_{1}(L_{3})}\leq k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon),

a contradiction to (
117
). Hence,
x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{3}
for

t\in[0,1]
. Similarly, we can show that
y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{4}
for
t\in[0,1]
.

Now, we show that

x_{n}(t)<M_{3},\,y_{n}(t)<M_{4},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(123)

Suppose
x_{n}(t_{2})\geq M_{3}
for some
t_{2}\in[0,1]
. From
(
119
), (
122
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, it follows
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq p(t)k_{1}(\theta_{4}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq q(t)k_{2}(\theta_{3}(x_{n}(t)))(u_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\end{split}

which implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))}&\leq k_{1}(\theta_{4}(\|y_{n}\|))p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
\frac{-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))}&\leq k_{2}(M_{3})q(t),\hskip 36.98866ptt\in(0,1).\end{split}

Integrating from
t
to
1
, using the BCs (
119
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}&\leq k_{1}(\theta_{4}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{t}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{y_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{2}(z)+v_{2}(z)}&\leq k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{t}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 36.98866ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

which implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}I(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))-I(\frac{1}{n})&\leq k_{1}(\theta_{4}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
J(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))-J(\frac{1}{n})&\leq k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 36.98866ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

The increasing property of
I
and
J
leads to

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(\theta_{4}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

(124)

y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 39.83368ptt\in[0,1].

(125)

Integrating (
124
) from
0
to
t_{2}
and (
125
)
from
0
to
1
, using the BCs (
119
), (
124
) and
(
125
), we obtain

M_{3}\leq x_{n}(t_{2})\leq(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(\theta_{4}(\|y_{n}\|))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

(126)

\|y_{n}\|\leq(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)).

(127)

Either we have
\|y_{n}\|<M_{4}
or
\|y_{n}\|\geq M_{4}
. If

\|y_{n}\|<M_{4}
, then from (
126
), we have

M_{3}\leq(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(\|y_{n}\|)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

(128)

Now, by using (
127
) in (
128
) and the increasing
property of
k_{1}
and
I^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle M_{3}\leq(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{3}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,

a
contradiction to (
115
).

On the other hand, if
\|y_{n}\|\geq M_{4}
, then from
(
126
) and (
127
), we have

M_{3}\leq(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(M_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

(129)

M_{4}\leq(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)).

(130)

Using (
130
) in (
129
) and the increasing property
of
k_{1}
and
I^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle M_{3}\leq(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{3}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(M_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,

a contradiction to (
115
). Hence,
x_{n}(t)<M_{3}
for

t\in[0,1]
. Similarly, we can show that
y_{n}(t)<M_{4}
for
t\in[0,1]
.

Thus, in view of (
119
)-(
123
),
(x_{n},y_{n})
is a
solution of the following coupled system of BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,x(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
a_{1}x(&0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n},\\
a_{2}y(&0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,y^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(131)

satisfy

\begin{split}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}&\leq x_{n}(t)<M_{3},\,\frac{1}{n}\leq x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{3},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}&\leq y_{n}(t)<M_{4},\,\frac{1}{n}\leq y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{4},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(132)

Now, in view of
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
, there exist continuous
functions
\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}
and
\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}
defined on

[0,1]
and positive on
(0,1)
, and real constants

0\leq\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1
such that

\begin{split}f(t,y_{n}(t),x_{n}^{\prime}(t))&\geq\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(t)(y_{n}(t))^{\delta_{1}},\hskip 5.69046pt(t,y_{n}(t),x_{n}^{\prime}(t))\in[0,1]\times[0,M_{4}]\times[0,L_{3}],\\
g(t,x_{n}(t),y_{n}^{\prime}(t))&\geq\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(t)(x_{n}(t))^{\delta_{2}},\hskip 5.69046pt(t,x_{n}(t),y_{n}^{\prime}(t))\in[0,1]\times[0,M_{3}]\times[0,L_{4}].\end{split}

(133)

We claim that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{6}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(s)ds,

(134)

y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{5}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(s)ds,

(135)

where

\displaystyle C_{5}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}p(t)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(t)dt\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}q(t)\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(t)dt\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}},

\displaystyle C_{6}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}p(t)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(t)dt\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}q(t)\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(t)dt\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.

To prove (
134
), consider the following relation

\begin{split}x_{n}(t)=&(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}(a_{1}s+b_{1})p(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds\\
&+\frac{1}{a_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}(a_{1}t+b_{1})p(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

(136)

which implies that

\displaystyle x_{n}(0)=\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\frac{1}{n}+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using (
133
) and (
132
), we obtain

\begin{split}x_{n}(0)\geq&\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(s)(y_{n}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds\geq(y_{n}(0))^{\delta_{1}}\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(s)ds.\end{split}

(137)

Similarly, using (
133
) and (
132
), we obtain

\displaystyle y_{n}(0)\geq(x_{n}(0))^{\delta_{2}}\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(s)ds,

which in view of (
137
) implies that

\displaystyle y_{n}(0)\geq(y_{n}(0))^{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(s)ds\right)^{\delta_{2}}\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(s)ds.

Hence,

y_{n}(0)\geq C_{6}.

(138)

Now, from (
136
), it follows that

\displaystyle x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{1}p(s)f(s,y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds,

and using (
133
) and (
138
), we obtain
(
134
). Similarly, we can prove (
135
).

Now, using (
131
),
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, (
132
),
(
134
) and (
135
), we have

\begin{split}0\leq-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq k_{1}(M_{4})p(t)(u_{1}(C_{6}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(s)ds)+v_{1}(L_{3})),\hskip 8.5359ptt\in(0,1),\\
0\leq-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq k_{2}(M_{3})q(t)(u_{2}(C_{5}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{3}}(s)ds)+v_{2}(L_{4})),\hskip 18.49411ptt\in(0,1).\end{split}

(139)

In view of (
132
), (
139
),
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{12})
, it follows that the sequences

\{(x_{n}^{(j)},y_{n}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are uniformly bounded and
equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Hence, by Theorem (
1.5
), there
exist subsequences
\{(x_{n_{k}}^{(j)},y_{n_{k}}^{(j)})\}\ \ (j=0,1)
of
\{(x_{n}^{(j)},y_{n}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
and
(x,y)\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
such that

(x_{n_{k}}^{(j)},y_{n_{k}}^{(j)})
converges uniformly to

(x^{(j)},y^{(j)})
on
[0,1]

(j=0,1)
. Also,

a_{1}x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0
. Moreover,
from (
134
) and (
135
), with
n_{k}
in place of
n

and taking
\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow+\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)\geq

\displaystyle C_{6}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{4}L_{3}}(s)ds,

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(t)\geq

\displaystyle C_{5}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{3}L_{4}}(s)ds,

which shows that
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
,
x>0
and
y>0
on

[0,1]
. Further,
(x_{n_{k}},y_{n_{k}})
satisfy

\displaystyle x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p(s)f(s,y_{n_{k}}(s),x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}q(s)f(s,x_{n_{k}}(s),y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

Passing to the limit as
n_{k}\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p(s)f(s,y(s),x^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}q(s)f(s,x(s),y^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle p(t)f(t,y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(t)=

\displaystyle q(t)f(t,x(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Hence,
(x,y)
is a
C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of SBVPs (
46
).

Example 7.2

Consider the following coupled system of singular BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=(1-t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}(y(t))^{\frac{1}{4}}(x^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{1}},\hskip 13.37282ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{4}}(x(t))^{\frac{3}{4}}(y^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{2}},\hskip 13.37282ptt\in(0,1),\\
&x(0)-x^{\prime}(0)=y(0)-y^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(140)

where
0<\beta_{1}<1
and
0<\beta_{2}<1
.

Choose
p(t)=(1-t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}
,
q(t)=(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{4}}
,

k_{1}(x)=x^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
k_{2}(x)=x^{\frac{3}{4}}
,

u_{1}(x)=x^{-\beta_{1}}
,
u_{2}(x)=x^{-\beta_{2}}
and

v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)=0
. Also
\delta_{1}=\frac{1}{4}
,
\delta_{2}=\frac{3}{4}
,

\varphi_{EF}(t)=F^{-\beta_{1}}
and
\psi_{EF}(t)=F^{-\beta_{2}}
. Then,

I(z)=\frac{z^{\beta_{1}+1}}{\beta_{1}+1}
,

J(z)=\frac{z^{\beta_{2}+1}}{\beta_{2}+1}
,

I^{-1}(z)=(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}z^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}

and

J^{-1}(z)=(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}z^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}
. Then,
\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt=4
and
\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt=\frac{4}{3}
.

Clearly,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{5})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
are satisfied.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)}=\\
&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{2^{1+\frac{9}{4(\beta_{1}+1)}}(\frac{4}{3})^{\frac{1}{4(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{4(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}c^{\frac{3}{16(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}}=\infty,\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{x\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}=\\
&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{2^{1+\frac{3}{4(\beta_{2}+1)}(1+\frac{2}{\beta_{1}+1})}(\frac{4}{3})^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{3}{4(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}c^{\frac{3}{16(\beta_{1}+1)(\beta_{2}+1)}}}=\infty,\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{EF}(s)ds)dt=4^{1-\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}F^{\beta_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-\frac{\beta_{1}+3}{4}}dt=\frac{4^{1-\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}F^{\beta_{1}^{2}}}{1-\beta_{1}},\\
&\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{EF}(s)ds)dt=(\frac{4}{3})^{-\beta_{2}}C^{-\beta_{2}}F^{\beta_{2}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-\frac{3\beta_{2}+1}{4}}dt\\
&\hskip 157.91287pt=\frac{(4/3)^{1-\beta_{2}}C^{-\beta_{2}}F^{\beta_{2}^{2}}}{1-\beta_{2}},\end{split}

which shows that
(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{12})
also holds.

Since,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{5})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{12})
are satisfied. Therefore, by Theorem
7.1
, the system of BVPs (
140
) has at least one
C^{1}
-positive solution.

8
Existence of at least two positive solutions with more general BCs

In this section, we establish at lest two
C^{1}
-positive solutions
to the system of SBVPs (
46
). For each
(x,y)\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
, we write
\|(x,y)\|_{5}=\|x\|_{3}+\|y\|_{3}
. Clearly,
(C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1],\|\cdot\|_{5})
is a Banach space. We define a partial ordering in
C^{1}[0,1]
, by
x\leq y
if and only if
x(t)\leq y(t)
,
t\in[0,1]
. We define a partial ordering in
C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
, by
(x_{1},y_{1})\preceq(x_{2},y_{2})
if and only if
x_{1}\leq x_{2}
and
y_{1}\leq y_{2}
. Let

\displaystyle P_{i}=\{x\in C^{1}[0,1]:x(t)\geq\gamma_{i}\|x\|\text{ for all }t\in[0,1],x(0)\geq\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}\|x^{\prime}\|\},

where
\gamma_{i}=\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}+b_{i}}
,
i=1,2
. Clearly,
P_{i}

(i=1,2)
are cones of
C^{1}[0,1]
and
P_{1}\times P_{2}
is a cone of
C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
. For any real constant
r>0
, we define an open neighborhood of
(0,0)\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{r}=\{(x,y)\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]:\|(x,y)\|_{5}<r\}.

In view of
(\mathbf{B}_{11})
, there exist real constants

R_{3}>0
and
R_{4}>0
such that

\frac{R_{3}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{3})\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)}>1,

(141)

\frac{R_{4}}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{4})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}>1.

(142)

From the continuity of
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
,
I
and
J
, we choose

\varepsilon>0
small enough such that

\frac{R_{3}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt+J(\varepsilon))+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon))}>1,

(143)

\frac{R_{4}}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon))+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt+J(\varepsilon))}>1.

(144)

Choose
n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that

\max\{\frac{1}{n_{0}}(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),\frac{1}{n_{0}}(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\}<\varepsilon

and for each fixed
n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, consider the
system of non-singular BVPs

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p(t)f(t,y(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y^{\prime\prime}(t)&=q(t)g(t,x(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),|y^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
a_{1}x(0)&-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0.\end{split}

(145)

We write (
145
) as an equivalent system of integral equations

\begin{split}x(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
y(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(t,s)q(s)f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),|y^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

(146)

where

\displaystyle G_{i}(t,s)=\frac{1}{a_{i}}\begin{cases}b_{i}+a_{i}s,\,&0\leq s\leq t\leq 1,\\
b_{i}+a_{i}t,\,&0\leq t\leq s\leq 1,\,i=1,2.\end{cases}

By a solution of the system of BVPs (
145
), we mean a
solution of the corresponding system of integral equations
(
146
).

Define a map
T_{n}:C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]\rightarrow C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
by

T_{n}(x,y)=(A_{n}(x,y),B_{n}(x,y)),

(147)

where the maps
A_{n},B_{n}:C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]\rightarrow C^{1}[0,1]
are defined by

\begin{split}A_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
B_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(t,s)q(s)f(s,x(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),|y^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(148)

Clearly, if
(x_{n},y_{n})\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
is a fixed point of
T_{n}
; then
(x_{n},y_{n})
is a solution of the system of BVPs (
145
).

Lemma 8.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
hold. Then the
map
T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})\rightarrow P_{1}\times P_{2}
is completely continuous.

Proof

Firstly, we show that
T_{n}(P_{1}\times P_{2})\subseteq P_{1}\times P_{2}
. For
(x,y)\in P_{1}\times P_{2}
,
t\in[0,1]
, using
(
148
) and Lemma
1.17
, we obtain

\begin{split}A_{n}(x,y)(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\geq\gamma_{1}\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(\tau,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&=\gamma_{1}\|A_{n}(x,y)\|\end{split}

(149)

and

\begin{split}A_{n}(x,y)(0)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(0,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&=\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{\tau}^{1}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&=\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(\tau)|\\
&=\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}\|.\end{split}

(150)

From (
149
) and (
150
),
A_{n}(x,y)\in P_{1}
for
every
(x,y)\in P_{1}\times P_{2}
, that is,
A_{n}(P_{1}\times P_{2})\subseteq P_{1}
. Similarly, by using (
148
) and Lemma

1.17
, we can show that
B_{n}(P_{1}\times P_{2})\subseteq P_{2}
. Hence,
T_{n}(P_{1}\times P_{2})\subseteq P_{1}\times P_{2}
.

Now, we show that
T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})\rightarrow P_{1}\times P_{2}
is uniformly bounded. For any

(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})
, using
(
148
), Lemma
1.17
,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x,y)\|=\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\right|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}(a_{1}s+b_{1})p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq\frac{1}{a_{1}}k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}))\int_{0}^{1}(a_{1}s+b_{1})p(s)ds<+\infty,\end{split}

(151)

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}\|=\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(\tau)|\\
&=\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{\tau}^{1}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}))\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds<+\infty.\end{split}

(152)

From (
151
) and (
152
), it follows that

A_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is
uniformly bounded under
\|\cdot\|_{3}
. Similarly, by using
(
148
), Lemma
1.17
,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we can show that

B_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is
uniformly bounded under
\|\cdot\|_{3}
. Hence,

T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is
uniformly bounded.

Now, we show that
T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is equicontinuous. For any

(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})
and

t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
, using (
148
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,
we have

\begin{split}&|A_{n}(x,y)(t_{1})-A_{n}(x,y)(t_{2})|\\
&=\left|\int_{0}^{1}(G_{1}(t_{1},s)-G_{1}(t_{2},s))p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}|G_{1}(t_{1},s)-G_{1}(t_{2},s)|p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}|G_{1}(t_{1},s)-G_{1}(t_{2},s)|p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}))\int_{0}^{1}|G(t_{1},s)-G(t_{2},s)|p(s)ds,\end{split}

(153)

\begin{split}&|A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(t_{1})-A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}(t_{2})|=\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)k_{1}(y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\\
&\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}))\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p(s)ds.\end{split}

(154)

From (
153
), (
154
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
, it
follows that
A_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is equicontinuous under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{3}
.
Similarly, using (
148
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we can show
that
B_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is
equicontinuous under
\|\cdot\|_{3}
. Consequently,

T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is
equicontinuous. Hence, by Theorem
1.5
,

T_{n}(\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}))
is
relatively compact which implies that
T_{n}
is a compact map. Further, we show that
T_{n}
is continuous. Let
(x_{m},y_{m}),(x,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})
such that

\displaystyle\|(x_{m},y_{m})-(x,y)\|_{5}\rightarrow 0\text{ as
}m\rightarrow+\infty.

Using
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|f(t,y_{m}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{m}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|\leq k_{1}(y_{m}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))\\
&(u_{1}(|x_{m}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(|x_{m}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}))\leq k_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n}(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(r+\frac{1}{n})).\end{split}

Using (
148
) and Lemma
1.17
, we have

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|\\
&=\max_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)(f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\right|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}(a_{1}s+b_{1})p(s)\left|f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds\end{split}

(155)

and

\begin{split}&\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})^{\prime}-A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}\|\\
&=\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|\int_{\tau}^{1}p(s)(f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n}))ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\left|f(s,y_{m}(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{m}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})-f(s,y(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})\right|ds.\end{split}

(156)

From (
155
) and (
156
), using Lebesgue dominated
convergence theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|\rightarrow 0,\hskip 5.69046pt\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})^{\prime}-A_{n}(x,y)^{\prime}\|\rightarrow 0,\text{
as }m\rightarrow+\infty.

Hence,

\|A_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-A_{n}(x,y)\|_{3}\rightarrow 0
as

m\rightarrow\infty
. Similarly, we can show that
\|B_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-B_{n}(x,y)\|_{3}\rightarrow 0
as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,
\|T_{n}(x_{m},y_{m})-T_{n}(x,y)\|_{5}\rightarrow 0
as

m\rightarrow+\infty
, that is,
T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})\rightarrow P_{1}\times P_{2}
is continuous. Hence,
T_{n}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{r}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})\rightarrow P_{1}\times P_{2}
is completely continuous.

Assume that

(\mathbf{B}_{13})

for any real constant
C>0
,
\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{E}(s)ds)dt<+\infty

and

\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{E}(s)ds)dt<+\infty
.

Theorem 8.2

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{5})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{8})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{13})
hold. Then the system of SBVPs (
46
) has at least two
C^{1}
-positive solutions.

Proof

Let
R_{5}=R_{3}+R_{4}
and define

\mathcal{O}_{R_{5}}=\Omega_{R_{3}}\times\Omega_{R_{4}}
where

\displaystyle\Omega_{R_{3}}=\{x\in E:\|x\|_{3}<R_{3}\},\,\Omega_{R_{4}}=\{x\in E:\|x\|_{3}<R_{4}\}.

We claim that

(x,y)\neq\lambda T_{n}(x,y),\text{ for }\lambda\in(0,1],(x,y)\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R_{5}}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}).

(157)

Suppose there exist

(x_{0},y_{0})\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R_{5}}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})

and
\lambda_{0}\in(0,1]
such that

\displaystyle(x_{0},y_{0})=\lambda_{0}T_{n}(x_{0},y_{0}).

Then,

\begin{split}-x_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=\lambda_{0}p(t)f(t,y_{0}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{0}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-y_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=\lambda_{0}q(t)g(t,x_{0}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),|y_{0}^{\prime}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
a_{1}x(0)&-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0.\end{split}

(158)

From (
158
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
, we have
x_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
and
y_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
on
(0,1)
, integrating from
t
to
1
,
using the BCs (
158
), we obtain
x_{0}^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
and

y_{0}^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
for
t\in[0,1]
. From (

) and

(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p(t)k_{1}(y_{0}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))(u_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq q(t)k_{2}(x_{0}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}))(u_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{1}(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})}\leq p(t)k_{1}(y_{0}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}))

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{0}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})+v_{2}(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})}\leq q(t)k_{2}(x_{0}(t)+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}))

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)q(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating from
t
to
1
, using the BCs (

), we obtain

\displaystyle I(x_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})-I(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)\int_{t}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle J(y_{0}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n})-J(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)\int_{t}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

x_{0}^{\prime}(t)\leq I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

(159)

y_{0}^{\prime}(t)\leq J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(160)

Integrating from
0
to
t
, using the BCs (

), leads to

\displaystyle x_{0}(t)\leq\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}x_{0}^{\prime}(0)+

\displaystyle I^{-1}(k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{0}(t)\leq\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}y_{0}^{\prime}(0)+

\displaystyle J^{-1}(k_{2}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(161)

)-(

(165)

Now, choose a

and define

}N_{4}=\frac{1+\gamma_{1}^{-1}\varrho_{1}^{-1}}{\max_{t\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(t,s)q(s)ds}.

(166)

By
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
, there exist real constants with

\begin{split}h_{1}(x,y)&\geq N_{3}x,\text{ for }x\geq R_{3}^{*},y\in(0,\infty),\\

(167)

Let

and define

We show that

T_{n}(x,y)\npreceq(x,y),\text{ for }(x,y)\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R^{**}}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}).

(168)

Suppose
T_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})\preceq(x_{0},y_{0})
for some

(x_{0},y_{0})\in\partial\mathcal{O}_{R^{**}}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2})
. Then,

x_{0}(t)\geq A_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})(t)\text{ and }y_{0}(t)\geq B_{n}(x_{0},y_{0})(t)\text{ for }t\in[0,1].

(169)

By Lemma
1.16
, we have

\displaystyle x_{0}(t)\geq\gamma_{1}\varrho_{1}\|x_{0}\|_{3}=R_{3}^{*},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

Similarly,
y_{0}(t)\geq R_{4}^{*}
for

. Hence,

\displaystyle|x_{0}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\geq R_{3}^{*}\text{ and
}|y_{0}(t)|+\frac{1}{n}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\geq R_{4}^{*}\text{
for }t\in[0,1].

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|x_{0}\|\geq x_{0}(t)\\

&\geq\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)h_{1}(y_{0}(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),|x_{0}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds\\

&\geq N_{3}R_{4}^{*}\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

in view of (

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|x_{0}\|&\geq N_{3}R_{4}^{*}\max_{t\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)ds>\frac{R_{4}^{*}}{\gamma_{2}\varrho_{2}}.\end{split}

.

),
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
,
(

\|(x_{0},y_{0})\|_{5}=\|x_{0}\|_{3}+\|y_{0}\|_{3}>R^{**}
, a

) is true and by Lemma

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T_{n},\mathcal{O}_{R^{**}}\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}),P_{1}\times P_{2})=0.

) and (

), it follows that

\operatorname{ind_{{}_{FP}}}(T_{n},(\mathcal{O}_{R^{**}}\setminus\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{R})\cap(P_{1}\times P_{2}),P_{1}\times P_{2})=-1.

(171)

) and (

), there exist

y_{n,j}(t)&=\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(t,s)q(s)g(s,x_{n,j}(s)+\frac{1}{n}(s+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),|y_{n,j}^{\prime}(s)|+\frac{1}{n})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\,j=1,2.\end{split}

(172)

Using
(\mathbf{B}_{8})
there exist continuous functions

By the Lemma

, we have

&\left(\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(\tau,s)q(s)\psi_{R_{3}+\varepsilon}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.\end{split}

Similarly, from (

) in (

Now, differentiating (

(179)

which on integration from

\begin{split}x_{n,1}^{\prime}(t)&\leq k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}p(s)(u_{1}(C_{8}^{\delta_{1}}\gamma_{2}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{s}^{1}p(\tau)\varphi_{R_{4}+\varepsilon}(\tau)d\tau)+v_{1}(R_{3}+\varepsilon))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\

(180)

In view of (

), (

), (

),
(

(\mathbf{B}_{1})

, the

. Thus, by Theorem

1.5
, there exist subsequences

\{(x_{n_{k},1}^{(j)},y_{n_{k},1}^{(j)})\}

of

\{(x_{n,1}^{(j)},y_{n,1}^{(j)})\}
