(x_{0,1},y_{0,1})\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
such that
(x_{n_{k},1}^{(j)},y_{n_{k},1}^{(j)})
converges uniformly to

(x_{0,1}^{(j)},y_{0,1}^{(j)})
on
[0,1]
. Also,

a_{1}x_{0,1}(0)-b_{1}x_{0,1}^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}y_{0,1}(0)-b_{2}y_{0,1}^{\prime}(0)=x_{0,1}^{\prime}(1)=y_{0,1}^{\prime}(1)=0
.
Moreover, from (
174
) and (
175
), with
n_{k}
in
place of
n
and taking
\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow+\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle x_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\geq C_{8}^{\delta_{1}}\gamma_{2}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{4}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,

\displaystyle y_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\geq C_{7}^{\delta_{2}}\gamma_{1}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{R_{3}+\varepsilon}(s)ds,

which implies that
x_{0,1}^{\prime}>0
and
y_{0,1}^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
,

x_{0,1}>0
and
y_{0,1}>0
on
[0,1]
. Further,

\begin{split}&\left|f(t,y_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),x_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})\right|\\
&\leq p(t)k_{1}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)(u_{1}(C_{8}^{\delta_{1}}\gamma_{2}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{R_{4}+\varepsilon}(s)ds)+v_{1}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)),\\
&\left|g(t,x_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),y_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})\right|\\
&\leq q(t)k_{2}(R_{3}+\varepsilon)(u_{2}(C_{7}^{\delta_{2}}\gamma_{1}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{R_{3}+\varepsilon}(s)ds)+v_{2}(R_{4}+\varepsilon)),\end{split}

(181)

\begin{split}\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow\infty}f(t,y_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),x_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})&=f(t,y_{0,1}(t),x_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow\infty}g(t,x_{n_{k},1}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),y_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(t)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})&=g(t,x_{0,1}(t),y_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).\end{split}

(182)

Moreover,
(x_{n_{k},1},y_{n_{k},1})
satisfies

\displaystyle x_{n_{k},1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)f(s,y_{n_{k},1}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}}(s+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}),x_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{n_{k},1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(t,s)q(s)g(s,x_{n_{k},1}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}}(s+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}),y_{n_{k},1}^{\prime}(s)+\frac{1}{n_{k}})ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

in view of (
181
),
(\mathbf{B}_{13})
, (
182
), the
Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and taking

\lim_{n_{k}\rightarrow+\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle x_{0,1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G_{1}(t,s)p(s)f(s,y_{0,1}(s),x_{0,1}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{0,1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}G_{2}(t,s)q(s)g(s,x_{0,1}(s),y_{0,1}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that
(x_{0,1},y_{0,1})\in C^{2}(0,1)\times C^{2}(0,1)
and

\displaystyle-x_{0,1}^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,y_{0,1}(t),x_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y_{0,1}^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,x_{0,1}(t),y_{0,1}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Moreover, by (
141
) and (
142
), we have

\|x_{0,1}\|_{3}<R_{3}
and
\|y_{0,1}\|_{3}<R_{4}
, that is,

\|(x_{0,1},y_{0,1})\|_{5}<R_{5}
. By a similar proof the sequence

\{(x_{n,2},y_{n,2})\}
has a convergent subsequence

\{(x_{n_{k},2},y_{n_{k},2})\}
converging uniformly to

(x_{0,2},y_{0,2})\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
on
[0,1]
.
Moreover,
(x_{0,2},y_{0,2})
is a solution to the system of SBVPs (
46
) with
x_{0,2}>0
and
y_{0,2}>0
on

[0,1]
,
x^{\prime}_{0,2}>0
and
y^{\prime}_{0,2}>0
on
[0,1)
,

R_{5}<\|(x_{0,2},y_{0,2})\|_{5}<R^{**}
.

Example 8.3

Consider the following coupled system of SBVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=\mu_{1}(1+(y(t))^{\delta_{1}}+(y(t))^{\eta_{1}})(1+(x^{\prime}(t))^{\alpha_{1}}+(x^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{1}}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=\mu_{2}(1+(x(t))^{\delta_{2}}+(x(t))^{\eta_{2}})(1+(y^{\prime}(t))^{\alpha_{2}}+(y^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{2}}),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&x(0)-x^{\prime}(0)=y(0)-y^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(183)

where
0\leq\delta_{i}<1
,
\eta_{i}>1
,
0<\alpha_{i}<1
,

0<\beta_{i}<1
, and
\mu_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
.

Choose
p(t)=\mu_{1}
,
q(t)=\mu_{2}
,

k_{i}(x)=1+x^{\delta_{i}}+x^{\eta_{i}}
,
u_{i}(x)=x^{-\beta_{i}}

and
v_{i}(x)=1+x^{\alpha_{i}}
,
i=1,2
. Assume that
\mu_{1}
is
arbitrary and

\small{\begin{split}\mu_{2}<\min\{\inf_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{J(2^{-1}(\mu_{1}^{-1}I(\frac{c}{2}))^{\delta_{1}^{-1}})}{k_{2}(c)},\,\inf_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{J(2^{-1}(\mu_{1}^{-1}I(\frac{c}{2}))^{\eta_{1}^{-1}})}{k_{2}(c)},\,\inf_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{J(\frac{c}{2})}{k_{2}(2I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(c)))}\}.\end{split}}

We choose
\varphi_{E}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\psi_{E}(t)=\mu_{2}
and

h_{i}(x,y)=\mu_{i}(1+x^{\eta_{i}})
,
i=1,2
. Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{x\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{h_{i}(x,y)}{x}=\lim_{x\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{\mu_{i}(1+x^{\eta_{i}})}{x}=+\infty,\,i=1,2.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}((1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)}\\
&=\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{2I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(2J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c))))}\\
&\geq\frac{c}{2I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(2J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c))))},\hskip 136.5733ptc\in(0,\infty)\\
&=\frac{c}{2I^{-1}(\mu_{1}(1+(2J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c)))^{\delta_{1}}+(2J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(c)))^{\eta_{1}}))},\hskip 28.45274ptc\in(0,\infty)\\
&>1,\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(k_{2}((1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt))\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}\\
&=\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{2J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(2I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(c))))}\\
&=\frac{c}{2J^{-1}(\mu_{2}k_{2}(2I^{-1}(\mu_{1}k_{1}(c))))},\hskip 11.38092ptc\in(0,\infty)\\
&>1.\end{split}

Further,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{E}(s)ds)dt=\mu_{1}^{1-2\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-\beta_{1}}dt=\frac{\mu_{1}^{1-2\beta_{1}}C^{-\beta_{1}}}{1-\beta_{1}},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{E}(s)ds)dt=\mu_{2}^{1-2\beta_{2}}C^{-\beta_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-\beta_{2}}dt=\frac{\mu_{1}^{1-2\beta_{2}}C^{-\beta_{2}}}{1-\beta_{2}}.

Clearly,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{5})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{8})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{13})
are satisfied. Hence,
by Theorem
8.2
, the system of BVPs (
183
) has at
least two
C^{1}
-positive solutions.

9
System of ODEs with two-point coupled BCs

In this section, we establish existence of at least one

C^{1}
-positive solution for the system of BVPs (
49
). By a

C^{1}
-positive solution to the system of BVPs (
49
), we
mean that
(x,y)\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
,
(x,y)
satisfies (
49
),
x>0

and
y>0
on
[0,1]
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
.

Assume that

(\mathbf{B}_{14})

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(h_{1}(c)k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)+(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(h_{2}(c)k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}>1,

where
I(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{1}(\tau)+v_{1}(\tau)}
,

J(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{2}(\tau)+v_{2}(\tau)}
, for

\mu>0
;

(\mathbf{B}_{16})

for real constants
M>0
and
L>0
there exist continuous functions
\varphi_{ML}
and
\psi_{ML}
defined on

[0,1]
and positive on
(0,1)
, and constants

0\leq\gamma_{1},\delta_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta_{2}<1
satisfying

(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})\neq\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
, such that

f(t,x,y,z)\geq\varphi_{ML}(t)x^{\gamma_{1}}y^{\delta_{1}}
and

g(t,x,y,z)\geq\psi_{ML}(t)x^{\gamma_{2}}y^{\delta_{2}}
on

[0,1]\times[0,M]\times[0,M]\times[0,L]
;

(\mathbf{B}_{17})

\int_{0}^{1}p(t)u_{1}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{1}}p(s)\varphi_{ML}(s)ds)dt<+\infty
and

\int_{0}^{1}q(t)u_{2}(C\int_{t}^{1}s^{\delta_{2}}q(s)\psi_{ML}(s)ds)dt<+\infty

for any real constant
C>0
.

Theorem 9.1

Under the hypothesis
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{17})
, the
system of BVPs (
49
) has at least one
C^{1}
-positive
solution.

Proof

In view of
(\mathbf{B}_{14})
, we can choose real constant

M_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{5}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds)+(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds)}>1.

From the continuity of
I
and
J
, we choose
\varepsilon>0
small
enough such that

\small{\frac{M_{5}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))+(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))}>1.}

(184)

Choose a real constant
L_{5}>0
such that

L_{5}>\max\{I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon)),J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt+J(\varepsilon))\}

(185)

Choose
n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that

\frac{1}{n_{0}}<\varepsilon
. For each

n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, define retractions

\theta_{5}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,M_{5}]
and

\rho_{5}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[\frac{1}{n},L_{5}]
by

\displaystyle\theta_{5}(x)=\max\{0,\min\{x,M_{5}\}\}\text{ and
}\rho_{5}(x)=\max\{\frac{1}{n},\min\{x,L_{5}\}\}.

Consider the modified system of BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,\theta_{5}(x(t)),\theta_{5}(y(t)),\rho_{5}(x^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,\theta_{5}(x(t)),\theta_{5}(y(t)),\rho_{5}(y^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&a_{1}y(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,y^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n}\\
&a_{2}x(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n}.\end{split}

(186)

Since

f(t,\theta_{5}(x(t)),\theta_{5}(y(t)),\rho_{5}(x^{\prime}(t))),\,g(t,\theta_{5}(x(t)),\theta_{5}(y(t)),\rho_{5}(y^{\prime}(t)))

are continuous and bounded on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, by Theorem

1.7
, it follows that the modified system of BVPs
(
186
) has a solution
(x_{n},y_{n})\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
.

Using (
186
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ and }y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0\text{ for
}t\in(0,1),

which on integration from
t
to
1
, using
the BCs (
186
), implies that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ and }y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{
for }t\in[0,1].

(187)

Integrating (
187
) from
0
to

t
, using the BCs (
186
) and (
187
), we have

x_{n}(t)\geq(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}\text{ and
}y_{n}(t)\geq(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}\text{ for
}t\in[0,1].

(188)

From (
187
) and (
188
), it
follows that

\|x_{n}\|=x_{n}(1)\text{ and }\|y_{n}\|=y_{n}(1).

(189)

Now, we show that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5},\,\,y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(190)

First, we prove
x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5}
for
t\in[0,1]
. Suppose

x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})\geq L_{5}
for some
t_{1}\in[0,1]
. Using
(
186
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p(t)h_{1}(\theta_{5}(x_{n}(t)))k_{1}(\theta_{5}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{1}(\rho_{5}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho_{5}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\rho_{5}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho_{5}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t)))}\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating from
t_{1}
to
1
, using the BCs (
186
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho_{5}(z))+v_{1}(\rho_{5}(z))}\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{t_{1}}^{1}p(t)dt,

which can also be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{L_{5}}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}+\int_{L_{5}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(L_{5})+v_{1}(L_{5})}\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt.

Using
the increasing property of
I
, we obtain

\displaystyle I(L_{5})+\frac{x_{n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-L_{5}}{u_{1}(L_{5})+v_{1}(L_{5})}\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon),

and using the increasing property of
I^{-1}
, leads to

\displaystyle L_{5}\leq I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt+I(\varepsilon)).

Which is a contradiction to (
185
). Hence,
x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5}

for
t\in[0,1]
. Similarly, we can show that
y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5}
for
t\in[0,1]
.

Now, we show that

\|x_{n}\|+\|y_{n}\|<M_{5}.

(191)

Suppose
\|x_{n}\|+\|y_{n}\|\geq M_{5}
. From (
186
),
(
187
), (
190
) and
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, it follows
that

\displaystyle-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p(t)h_{1}(\theta_{5}(x_{n}(t)))k_{1}(\theta_{5}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq q(t)h_{2}(\theta_{5}(x_{n}(t)))k_{2}(\theta_{5}(y_{n}(t)))(u_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})p(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})q(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Integrating from
t
to
1
, using the BCs (
186
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{t}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{y_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{2}(z)+v_{2}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{t}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which can also be written as

\displaystyle I(x_{n}^{\prime}(t))-I(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle J(y_{n}^{\prime}(t))-J(\frac{1}{n})

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

The increasing property of
I
and
J
leads to

\begin{split}x_{n}^{\prime}(t)&\leq I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
y_{n}^{\prime}(t)&\leq J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 9.95863ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(192)

Integrating from
0
to
t
, using the BCs (
186
) and
(
192
), we obtain

\begin{split}&x_{n}(t)\leq I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))\\
&+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
&y_{n}(t)\leq\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))\\
&+J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),\hskip 22.76228ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(193)

From (
193
) and (
189
), it follows that

\displaystyle M_{5}\leq\|x_{n}\|+\|y_{n}\|\leq(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))

\displaystyle+(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon)),

which implies that

\small{\begin{split}\frac{M_{5}}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}p(s)ds+I(\varepsilon))+(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})\int_{0}^{1}q(s)ds+J(\varepsilon))}\leq 1,\end{split}}

a contradiction to (
184
). Hence,

\|x_{n}\|+\|y_{n}\|<M_{5}
.

Thus, in view of (
186
)-(
191
),
(x_{n},y_{n})
is a
solution of the following coupled system of BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&a_{1}y(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n},\\
&a_{2}x(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(1)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(194)

satisfying

\begin{split}(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}&\leq x_{n}(t)<M_{5},\,\frac{1}{n}\leq x_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}&\leq y_{n}(t)<M_{5},\,\frac{1}{n}\leq y_{n}^{\prime}(t)<L_{5},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

(195)

We
claim that

x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{9}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{10}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds,

(196)

y_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{9}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{10}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds,

(197)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{9}=&\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1-\gamma_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}},\\
C_{10}=&\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1-\gamma_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.\end{split}

To prove (
196
), consider the following relation

\begin{split}&x_{n}(t)=(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}+\int_{0}^{t}sp(s)f(s,x_{n}(s),y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds\\
&+\int_{t}^{1}tp(s)f(s,x_{n}(s),y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)g(s,x_{n}(s),y_{n}(s),y_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\end{split}

(198)

which implies that

\displaystyle x_{n}(0)=\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\frac{1}{n}+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)g(s,x_{n}(s),y_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using
(\mathbf{B}_{16})
and (
195
), we obtain

\small{\begin{split}x_{n}(0)\geq\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)(x_{n}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(y_{n}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds\geq(x_{n}(0))^{\gamma_{1}}(y_{n}(0))^{\delta_{1}}\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds,\end{split}}

which implies that

x_{n}(0)\geq(y_{n}(0))^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\gamma_{1}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{1}}}.

(199)

Similarly, using
(\mathbf{B}_{16})
and (
195
), we obtain

y_{n}(0)\geq(x_{n}(0))^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\gamma_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{2}}}.

(200)

Now, using (
200
) in (
199
), we have

\displaystyle x_{n}(0)\geq

\displaystyle(x_{n}(0))^{\frac{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})}}\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{1}}}.

Hence,

x_{n}(0)\geq C_{9}.

(201)

Similarly, using (
199
) in (
200
), we obtain

y_{n}(0)\geq C_{10}.

(202)

Now, from (
198
), it follows that

\displaystyle x_{n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{1}p(s)f(s,x_{n}(s),y_{n}(s),x_{n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

and using
(\mathbf{B}_{16})
, (
195
), (
201
) and
(
202
), we obtain (
196
). Similarly, we can prove (
197
).

Now, using (
194
),
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
, (
195
),
(
196
) and (
197
), we have

\begin{split}0\leq-x_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq h_{1}(M_{5})k_{1}(M_{5})p(t)(u_{1}(C_{9}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{10}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds)+v_{1}(L_{5})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
0\leq-y_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)&\leq h_{2}(M_{5})k_{2}(M_{5})q(t)(u_{2}(C_{9}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{10}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds)+v_{2}(L_{5})),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).\end{split}

(203)

In view of (
195
), (
203
),
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{17})
, it follows that the sequences

\{(x_{n}^{(j)},y_{n}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are uniformly bounded and
equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Hence, by Theorem (
1.5
), there
exist subsequences
\{(x_{n_{k}}^{(j)},y_{n_{k}}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)

of
\{(x_{n}^{(j)},y_{n}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
and
(x,y)\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
such that

(x_{n_{k}}^{(j)},y_{n_{k}}^{(j)})
converges uniformly to

(x^{(j)},y^{(j)})
on
[0,1]

(j=0,1)
. Also,

a_{2}x(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=a_{1}y(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0
. Moreover,
from (
196
) and (
197
), with
n_{k}
in place of

and taking

, we have

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{9}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{10}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p(s)\varphi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds,

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{9}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{10}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}q(s)\psi_{M_{5}L_{5}}(s)ds,

which shows that
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
,
x>0
and
y>0
on

[0,1]
. Further,
(x_{n_{k}},y_{n_{k}})
satisfy

\displaystyle x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p(s)f(s,x_{n_{k}}(s),y_{n_{k}}(s),x_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}q(s)g(s,x_{n_{k}}(s),y_{n_{k}}(s),y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

Passing to the limit as
n_{k}\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p(s)f(s,x(s),y(s),x^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}q(s)g(s,x(s),y(s),y^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],

which implies that

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p(t)f(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(t)=q(t)g(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Hence,
(x,y)
is a
C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs
(
49
).

Example 9.2

Consider the following coupled system of SBVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=\nu^{\beta_{1}+1}(x(t))^{\gamma_{1}}(y(t))^{\delta_{1}}(x^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{1}},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=\nu^{\beta_{2}+1}(x(t))^{\gamma_{2}}(y(t))^{\delta_{2}}(y^{\prime}(t))^{-\beta_{2}},\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),\\
&x(0)-y^{\prime}(0)=y(0)-x^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(1)=y^{\prime}(1)=0,\end{split}

(204)

where
0\leq\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1

satisfying
(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})\neq\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
,

0<\beta_{1}<1
,
0<\beta_{2}<1
and
\nu>0
such that

\displaystyle\nu<\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}\frac{c}{2\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\beta_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{i}+1}}c^{\frac{{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}}{\beta_{i}+1}}}.

Choose
p(t)=q(t)=1
,
h_{1}(x)=\nu^{\beta_{1}+1}x^{\gamma_{1}}
,

h_{2}(x)=\nu^{\beta_{2}+1}x^{\gamma_{2}}
,

k_{1}(x)=x^{\delta_{1}}
,
k_{2}(x)=x^{\delta_{2}}
,

u_{1}(x)=x^{-\beta_{1}}
,
u_{2}(x)=x^{-\beta_{2}}
and

v_{1}(x)=v_{2}(x)=0
,
\varphi_{ML}(t)=L^{-\beta_{1}}
,

\psi_{ML}(t)=L^{-\beta_{2}}
.

Then,

I(\nu)=\frac{\nu^{\beta_{1}+1}}{\beta_{1}+1}
,

J(\nu)=\frac{\nu^{\beta_{2}+1}}{\beta_{2}+1}
,

I^{-1}(\nu)=(\beta_{1}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}\nu^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}+1}}

and

J^{-1}(\nu)=(\beta_{2}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}z^{\frac{1}{\beta_{2}+1}}
. Also,

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})I^{-1}(h_{1}(c)k_{1}(c)\int_{0}^{1}p(t)dt)+(1+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})J^{-1}(h_{2}(c)k_{2}(c)\int_{0}^{1}q(t)dt)}=

\displaystyle\sup_{c\in(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{2\nu\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\beta_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\beta_{i}+1}}c^{\frac{{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}}{\beta_{i}+1}}}>1.

Clearly,
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{17})
are satisfied.
Hence, by Theorem
9.1
, the system of BVPs (
204
) has
at least one
C^{1}
-positive solution.

10
Existence of at least one
C^{1}
-positive
solution via lower and upper solutions

In this section, we establish existence of at least one

C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs (
48
)
asif1
. By a

C^{1}
-positive solution to the system of BVPs (
48
), we
means
(x,y)\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
satisfying (
48
),
x>0
and
y>0
on
(0,1)
.

Let
\{\rho_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a nonincreasing sequence of
real constants such that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{n}=0
.
Assume that the following holds:

(\mathbf{B}_{18})

p_{i}\in C(0,1)
,
p_{i}>0
on
(0,1)
and
\int_{0}^{1}p_{i}(t)dt<+\infty
,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{B}_{19})

f_{i}:[0,1]\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
are
continuous,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{B}_{20})

there exist
(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
and

n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that
\beta_{1}(t)\geq\rho_{n_{0}}
,

\beta_{2}(t)\geq\rho_{n_{0}}
for
t\in[0,1]
and

\displaystyle-\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\geq p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,\beta_{1}(t),\beta_{2}(t),\beta_{1}^{\prime}(t)),\,\,\,\,t\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-\beta_{2}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\geq p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,\beta_{1}(t),\beta_{2}(t),\beta_{2}^{\prime}(t)),\,\,\,\,t\in(0,1);

(\mathbf{B}_{21})

there exist
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))\times(C^{1}[0,1]\cap C^{2}(0,1))
with

\alpha_{1}(0)=\alpha_{1}(1)=\alpha_{2}(0)=\alpha_{2}(1)=0
,

\alpha_{1}>0
and
\alpha_{2}>0
on
(0,1)
such that for

(t,x,y)\in(0,1)\times\{x\in(0,\infty):x<\alpha_{1}(t)\}\times\{y\in(0,\infty):y\leq\beta_{2}(t)\}
,

\displaystyle-\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle<p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x,y,\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(t)),

for

(t,x,y)\in(0,1)\times\{x\in(0,\infty):x\leq\beta_{1}(t)\}\times\{y\in(0,\infty):y<\alpha_{2}(t)\}
,

\displaystyle-\alpha_{2}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle<p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x,y,\alpha_{2}^{\prime}(t));

(\mathbf{B}_{22})

for each
n\in\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
,
0\leq t\leq 1
,
\rho_{n}\leq x\leq\beta_{1}(t)
,
\rho_{n}\leq y\leq\beta_{2}(t)
, we have
f_{1}(t,\rho_{n},y,0)\geq 0
and

f_{2}(t,x,\rho_{n},0)\geq 0
;

(\mathbf{B}_{23})

|f_{i}(t,x,y,z)|\leq(h_{i}(x)+k_{i}(x))(u_{i}(y)+v_{i}(y))\psi_{i}(|z|)
, where
h_{i},u_{i}>0
are continuous and nonincreasing
on
(0,\infty)
,
k_{i},v_{i}\geq 0
,
\psi_{i}>0
are continuous on

[0,\infty)
with
\frac{k_{i}}{h_{i}},\frac{v_{i}}{u_{i}}

nondecreasing on
(0,\infty)
,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{B}_{24})

\int_{0}^{1}p_{i}(t)h_{i}(\alpha_{1}(t))u_{i}(\alpha_{2}(t))dt<+\infty
,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{B}_{25})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{du}{\psi_{i}(u)}>\left[1+\frac{k_{i}(b_{1})}{h_{i}(b_{1})}\right]\left[1+\frac{v_{i}(b_{2})}{u_{i}(b_{2})}\right]\int_{0}^{1}p_{i}(t)h_{i}(\alpha_{1}(t))u_{i}(\alpha_{2}(t))dt,

where
b_{i}=\max\{\beta_{i}(t):t\in[0,1]\}
,
i=1,2
.

Theorem 10.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{B}_{18})-(\mathbf{B}_{25})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
