) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Chapter 4
Singular Systems of BVPs on Infinite Intervals

Recently, the theory on existence of solutions to nonlinear BVPs on unbounded
domain has attracted the attention of many authors, see for example

agarwalinfinite
;
eloe
;
kw1
;
liange
;
liu1
;
rma
;
yl
and the
references therein. For BVPs defined on half-line, an excellent
resource is produced by Agarwal and O’Regan
agarwalinfinite

that have been received considerable attentions.

Agarwal and O’Regan
(
ao
, Section 2.15)
studied the existence of positive
solutions to the following BVP

\begin{split}-x^{\prime\prime}(t)&=\phi(t)f(t,x(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,\infty),\\
x(0)&=0,\,\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0,\end{split}

(205)

where
f(t,x)
is singular at
x=0
. Further, in

(
agarwalinfinite
, Section 1.11)
they establish the existence
results for (
205
) when
f
includes first derivative also. However in

wei
;
cheng
, it was assumed that the nonlinearities are
positive which leads to concave solutions.

In Sections
11
and
12
, we
study the existence of
C^{1}
-positive solutions to the following
coupled systems of SBVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+},\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+},\\
&x(0)=y(0)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0\end{split}

(206)

and

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+},\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+},\\
&a_{1}x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0,\\
&a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\prime}(t)=0,\end{split}

(207)

where the functions
f_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
are continuous and allowed to change sign.
Further, the nonlinearities
f_{i}

(i=1,2)
are allowed to be
singular at
x^{\prime}=0
and
y^{\prime}=0
. Also,
p_{i}\in C(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})
,

p_{i}(i=1,2)>0
on
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
and the constants

a_{i},b_{i}(i=1,2)>0
; here
\mathbb{R}=(-\infty,\infty)
,

\mathbb{R}_{0}=\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
\mathbb{R}^{+}=[0,\infty)
,

\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}=\mathbb{R}^{+}\setminus\{0\}
.

11
Systems of BVPs on infinite intervals

In this section, we establish the existence of
C^{1}
-positive
solutions for the system of BVPs (
206
). We say,

(x,y)\in(C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\cap C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}))\times(C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\cap C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}))
is a

C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs (
206
), if
(x,y)
satisfies (
206
),

x>0
and
y>0
on
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on

\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Assume that

(\mathbf{C}_{1})

p_{i}\in C(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})
,
p_{i}>0
on
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
,
\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{i}(t)dt<+\infty
,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{C}_{2})

f_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is continuous,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{C}_{3})

|f_{i}(t,x,y,z)|\leq h_{i}(|x|)k_{i}(|y|)(u_{i}(|z|)+v_{i}(|z|))
, where

u_{i}>0
is continuous
and nonincreasing on
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
,
h_{i},\,k_{i},\,v_{i}\geq 0
are continuous and nondecreasing on

\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{C}_{4})

there exist a constant
M>0
such that

\frac{M}{\omega(M)}>1
, where

\omega(M)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)
,

\displaystyle\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}[J_{i}^{-1}\big(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds+J_{i}(\varepsilon)\big)]dt

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}J_{i}^{-1}\big(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds+J_{i}(\varepsilon)\big),

\displaystyle J_{i}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{d\tau}{u_{i}(\tau)+v_{i}(\tau)},\text{
for }\mu>0,\,i=1,2;

(\mathbf{C}_{5})

J_{1}(\infty)=\infty
and
J_{2}(\infty)=\infty
;

(\mathbf{C}_{6})

f_{i}
is positive on
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times(0,M]^{3}
,

i=1,2
;

(\mathbf{C}_{7})

there exist continuous functions
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
defined on

\mathbb{R}^{+}
and positive on
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
, and constants

0\leq\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\delta_{1},\delta_{2}<1
satisfying

(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})\neq\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
, such that

f_{1}(t,x,y,z)\geq\varphi_{M}(t)x^{\gamma_{1}}y^{\delta_{1}}
and

f_{2}(t,x,y,z)\geq\psi_{M}(t)x^{\gamma_{2}}y^{\delta_{2}}
on

\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[0,M]^{3}
.

11.1
Existence of positive solutions on finite intervals

Choose

m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, where
N_{0}:=\{0,1,\cdots\}
, and
consider the following system of BVPs on finite interval

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
&x(0)=y(0)=x^{\prime}(m)=y^{\prime}(m)=0.\end{split}

(208)

First, we show
that system of BVPs (
208
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution. We
say,
(x,y)\in(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))\times(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))
, a
C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs
(
208
), if
(x,y)
satisfies (
208
),
x>0
and
y>0
on

(0,m]
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,m)
.

Theorem 11.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{7})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
208
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Proof

In view of
(\mathbf{C}_{4})
, we choose
\varepsilon>0
small
enough such that

\frac{M}{\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)}>1.

(209)

Choose
n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that

\frac{1}{n_{0}}<\varepsilon
. For each
n\in N:=\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, define retractions

\theta:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,M]
and
\rho:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[\frac{1}{n},M]

as

\displaystyle\theta(x)=\max\{0,\min\{x,M\}\}\text{ and
}\rho(x)=\max\{\frac{1}{n},\min\{x,M\}\}.

Consider the
modified system of BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}^{*}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}^{*}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
&x(0)=y(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(m)=y^{\prime}(m)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(210)

where

f_{1}^{*}(t,x,y,x^{\prime})=f_{1}(t,\theta(x),\theta(y),\rho(x^{\prime}))
and

f_{2}^{*}(t,x,y,y^{\prime})=f_{2}(t,\theta(x),\theta(y),\rho(y^{\prime}))
.
Clearly,
f_{i}^{*}\,(i=1,2)
are continuous and bounded on

[0,m]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Hence, by Theorem
1.7
, the modified
system of BVPs (
210
) has a solution

(x_{m,n},y_{m,n})\in(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))\times(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))
.

Using (
210
),
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{C}_{6})
,
we obtain

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0\text{ and }y_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0\text{ on }\in(0,m).

Integrating from
t
to
m
and using the BCs (
210
), we
obtain

x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ and
}y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ for }t\in[0,m].

(211)

Integrating (
211
) from
0
to
t
, using the BCs
(
210
), we have

x_{m,n}(t)\geq\frac{t}{n}\text{ and }y_{m,n}(t)\geq\frac{t}{n}\text{ for }t\in[0,m].

(212)

From (
211
) and (
212
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}=x_{m,n}(m)\text{ and
}\|y_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}=y_{m,n}(m),\text{ where
}\|u\|_{{}_{7,m}}=\max_{t\in[0,m]}|u(t)|.

Now, we show that the following hold

\|x_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M\text{ and }\|y_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M.

(213)

Suppose
x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})\geq M
for some
t_{1}\in[0,m]
. Using
(
210
) and
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p_{1}(t)h_{1}(\theta(x_{m,n}(t)))k_{1}(\theta(y_{m,n}(t)))(u_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))),\hskip 2.84544ptt\in(0,m),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).

Integrating from
t_{1}
to
m
, using the BCs (
210
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho(z))+v_{1}(\rho(z))}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t_{1}}^{m}p_{1}(t)dt,

which can also be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{M}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}+\int_{M}^{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(M)+v_{1}(M)}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(t)dt.

Using
the increasing property of
J_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{1}(M)+\frac{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-M}{u_{1}(M)+v_{1}(M)}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(t)dt+J_{1}(\varepsilon),

and the
increasing property of
J_{1}^{-1}
yields

\displaystyle M\leq J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(t)dt+J_{1}(\varepsilon))\leq\omega_{\varepsilon}(M),

a contradiction to (
209
). Hence,
\|x_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Similarly, we can show that
\|y_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Now, we show that

\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M\text{ and }\|y_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M.

(214)

Suppose
\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}\geq M
. From (
210
),
(
211
), (
213
) and
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, it follows that

\displaystyle-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p_{1}(t)h_{1}(\theta(x_{m,n}(t)))k_{1}(\theta(y_{m,n}(t)))(u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).

Integrating from
t
to
m
, using the BCs (
210
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

which can also be written as

\displaystyle J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))-J_{1}(\frac{1}{n})\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].

The increasing property of
J_{1}
and
J_{1}^{-1}
, leads to

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\leq J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].

Now, integrating from
0
to
m
, using the BCs (
210
), we
obtain

\displaystyle M\leq\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}\leq\int_{0}^{m}[J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon))]dt,

which implies that

\displaystyle M\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}[J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon))]dt\leq\omega_{\varepsilon}(M),

a contradiction to (
209
). Therefore,

\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Similarly, we can show that
\|y_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Hence, in view of (
210
)-(
214
),
(x_{m,n},y_{m,n})
is
a solution of the following coupled system of BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
&x(0)=y(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(m)=y^{\prime}(m)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(215)

satisfying

\begin{split}\frac{t}{n}&\leq x_{m,n}(t)<M,\,\frac{1}{n}\leq x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],\\
\frac{t}{n}&\leq y_{m,n}(t)<M,\,\frac{1}{n}\leq y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].\end{split}

(216)

Now, we show that

\{x_{m,n}^{\prime}\}_{n\in N}\text{ and }\{y_{m,n}^{\prime}\}_{n\in N}\text{ are
equicontinuous on }[0,m].

(217)

From (
215
), (
216
) and
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, it
follows that

\displaystyle-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq

\displaystyle p_{1}(t)h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)(u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

\displaystyle-y_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq

\displaystyle p_{2}(t)h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)(u_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)p_{2}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).

Thus for
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,m]
, we have

\begin{split}|J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{1}(t)dt\right|,\\
|J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{2}(t)dt\right|.\end{split}

(218)

In view of (
218
),
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
, uniform continuity
of
J_{i}^{-1}
over
[0,J_{i}(M)]

(i=1,2)
and

\displaystyle|x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

\displaystyle|y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

we obtain (
217
).

From (
216
) and (
217
), it follows that the sequences

\{(x_{m,n}^{(j)},y_{m,n}^{(j)})\}_{n\in N}

(j=0,1)
are uniformly
bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,m]
. Hence, by Theorem
(
1.5
), there exist subsequence
N_{*}
of
N
and

(x_{m},y_{m})\in C^{1}[0,m]\times C^{1}[0,m]
such that for each

j=0,1
, the sequences
(x_{m,n}^{(j)},y_{m,n}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(x_{m}^{(j)},y_{m}^{(j)})
on
[0,m]
as

n\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{*}
. From the BCs (
215
), we
have
x_{m}(0)=y_{m}(0)=x_{m}^{\prime}(m)=y_{m}^{\prime}(m)=0
. Next, we show that

x_{m}>0
and
y_{m}>0
on
(0,m]
,
x_{m}^{\prime}>0
and
y_{m}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,m)
.

We claim that

x_{m,n}(t)\geq C_{11}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{12}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t,1\}}\tau^{1+\gamma_{1}+\delta_{1}}p_{1}(\tau)\varphi_{M}(\tau)d\tau\equiv\Phi_{M}(t),\hskip 5.69046ptt\in[0,m],

(219)

y_{m,n}(t)\geq C_{11}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{12}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t,1\}}\tau^{1+\gamma_{2}+\delta_{2}}p_{2}(\tau)\psi_{M}(\tau)d\tau\equiv\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 5.69046ptt\in[0,m],

(220)

x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 56.9055ptt\in[0,m],

(221)

y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{m}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{2}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{2}}ds,\hskip 56.9055ptt\in[0,m],

(222)

where

\small{\begin{split}&C_{11}=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tau^{1+\gamma_{1}+\delta_{1}}p_{1}(\tau)\varphi_{M}(\tau)d\tau\right)^{\frac{1-\gamma_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tau^{1+\gamma_{2}+\delta_{2}}p_{2}(\tau)\psi_{M}(\tau)d\tau\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}},\\
&C_{12}=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tau^{1+\gamma_{1}+\delta_{1}}p_{1}(\tau)\varphi_{M}(\tau)d\tau\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tau^{1+\gamma_{2}+\delta_{2}}p_{2}(\tau)\psi_{M}(\tau)d\tau\right)^{\frac{1-\gamma_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.\end{split}}

First we prove (
219
). Let
z(t)=x_{m,n}(t)-tx_{m,n}(1)

for
t\in[0,1]
. Then,
z(0)=z(1)=0
,
z^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq 0
for
t\in[0,1]
.
So,
z(t)\geq 0
for
t\in[0,1]
, that is

x_{m,n}(t)\geq tx_{m,n}(1),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(223)

Similarly,

y_{m,n}(t)\geq ty_{m,n}(1),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].

(224)

Now, consider the following relation

\begin{split}x_{m,n}(t)&=\frac{t}{n}+\int_{0}^{t}sp_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds\\
&+\int_{t}^{m}tp_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].\end{split}

(225)

In view of
(\mathbf{C}_{7})
, using (
223
) and
(
224
), for
t\in[0,m]
, we have

\begin{split}x_{m,n}(t)&\geq\int_{0}^{t}sp_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds\\
&\geq\int_{0}^{\min\{t,1\}}sp_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds\\
&\geq\int_{0}^{\min\{t,1\}}sp_{1}(s)(x_{m,n}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(y_{m,n}(s))^{\delta_{1}}\varphi_{M}(s)ds\\
&\geq(x_{m,n}(1))^{\gamma_{1}}(y_{m,n}(1))^{\delta_{1}}\int_{0}^{\min\{t,1\}}s^{1+\gamma_{1}+\delta_{1}}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\end{split}

(226)

which implies that

x_{m,n}(1)\geq(y_{m,n}(1))^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\gamma_{1}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{1+\gamma_{1}+\delta_{1}}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{1}}}.

(227)

Similarly,

y_{m,n}(1)\geq(x_{m,n}(1))^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\gamma_{2}}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{1+\gamma_{2}+\delta_{2}}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{2}}}.

(228)

Now, using (
228
) in (
227
), we have

\displaystyle(x_{m,n}(1))^{1-\frac{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{1+\gamma_{1}+\delta_{1}}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\left(\int_{0}^{1}s^{1+\gamma_{2}+\delta_{2}}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})}}.

Hence,

x_{m,n}(1)\geq C_{11}.

(229)

Similarly, using (
227
) in (
228
), we obtain

y_{m,n}(1)\geq C_{12}.

(230)

Thus, from (
226
), using (
229
) and (
230
), we
get (
219
).

Similarly, we can prove (
220
).

Now, we prove (
221
). From (
225
), it follows that

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using
(\mathbf{C}_{7})
, (
219
) and (
220
), we
obtain (
221
).

Similarly, we can prove (
222
).

From (
219
)-(
222
), passing to the limit

n\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{*}
, we obtain

\begin{split}x_{m}(t)&\geq\Phi_{M}(t),\,\,y_{m}(t)\geq\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 85.35826ptt\in[0,m],\\
x_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],\\
y_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{m}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{2}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{2}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].\end{split}

(231)

Consequently,
x_{m}>0
and
y_{m}>0
on
(0,m]
,
x_{m}^{\prime}>0
and
y_{m}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,m)
.

Moreover,
(x_{m,n},y_{m,n})
satisfy

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{m,n}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

\displaystyle y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{m,n}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].

Letting
n\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{*}
, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{m}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m}(s),y_{m}(s),x_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

\displaystyle y_{m}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,x_{m}(s),y_{m}(s),y_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

which imply that

\begin{split}-x_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x_{m}(t),y_{m}(t),x_{m}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-y_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x_{m}(t),y_{m}(t),y_{m}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).\end{split}

(232)

Hence,
(x_{m},y_{m})
is a
C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs (
208
).

11.2
Existence of positive solutions on an infinite interval

Theorem 11.2

Assume that
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{7})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
206
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Proof

By Theorem
11.1
, for each
m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, the
system of BVPs (
208
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution

(x_{m},y_{m})
defined on
[0,m]
. By applying diagonalization
argument we will show that the system of BVPs (
206
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution. For this purpose we
define a continuous extension
(\overline{x}_{m},\overline{y}_{m})

of
(x_{m},y_{m})
by

\overline{x}_{m}(t)=\begin{cases}x_{m}(t),\,&t\in[0,m],\\
x_{m}(m),\,&t\in[m,\infty),\end{cases}\hskip 14.22636pt\overline{y}_{m}(t)=\begin{cases}y_{m}(t),\,&t\in[0,m],\\
y_{m}(m),\,&t\in[m,\infty).\end{cases}

(233)

Clearly,
\overline{x}_{m},\overline{y}_{m}\in C^{1}[0,\infty)
and
satisfy

\begin{split}0&\leq\overline{x}_{m}(t)<M,\,0\leq\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,\infty),\\
0&\leq\overline{y}_{m}(t)<M,\,0\leq\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,\infty).\end{split}

(234)

We claim that

\{\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}}\text{ and
}\{\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}}\text{ are
equicontinuous on }[0,1].

(235)

Using (
232
), (
233
), (
234
) and

(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p_{1}(t)h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)(u_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p_{2}(t)h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)(u_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle\frac{-\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)p_{2}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Hence, for
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
, we have

\begin{split}|J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{1}(t)dt\right|,\\
|J_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{2}(t)dt\right|.\end{split}

(236)

In view of (
236
),
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
, uniform continuity
of
J_{i}^{-1}
over
[0,J_{i}(L)]

(i=1,2)
and

\displaystyle|\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})-\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

\displaystyle|\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})-\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

we establish (
235
).

From (
234
) and (
235
), it follows that the sequences

\{(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are
uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Hence, by Theorem

1.5
, there exist subsequence
N_{1}
of

N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
and
(u_{1},v_{1})\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
such that for each
j=0,1
, the sequence

(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(u_{1}^{(j)},v_{1}^{(j)})
on
[0,1]
as

m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{1}
. Also from BCs (
208
), we
have
u_{1}(0)=v_{1}(0)=0
.

Moreover, from (
231
) and (
233
), for each
m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{x}_{m}(t)&\geq\Phi_{M}(t),\,\,\overline{y}_{m}(t)\geq\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 81.09052ptt\in[0,1],\\
\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{2}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{2}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1].\end{split}

Passing to the limit
m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{1}(t)&\geq\Phi_{M}(t),\,\,v_{1}(t)\geq\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 85.35826ptt\in[0,1],\\
u_{1}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
v_{1}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{2}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{2}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

which shows that
u_{1}>0
and
v_{1}>0
on
(0,1]
,
u_{1}^{\prime}>0
and
v_{1}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,1)
.

By the same process as above, we can show that

\{\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{1}\setminus\{1\}}\text{ and
}\{\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{1}\setminus\{1\}}\text{ are
equicontinuous families on }[0,2].

(237)

Further, in view of (
234
) and (
237
), it follows
that the sequences

\{(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are
uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,2]
. Hence, by Theorem

1.5
, there exist subsequence
N_{2}
of

N_{1}\setminus\{1\}
and
(u_{2},v_{2})\in C^{1}[0,2]\times C^{1}[0,2]
such that for each
j=0,1
, the sequence

(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(u_{2}^{(j)},v_{2}^{(j)})
on
[0,2]
as

m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{2}
. Also from BCs (
208
),

u_{2}(0)=v_{2}(0)=0
. Moreover, in view of (
231
) and
(
233
), for each
m\in N_{1}\setminus\{1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{x}_{m}(t)&\geq\Phi_{M}(t),\,\,\overline{y}_{m}(t)\geq\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 81.09052ptt\in[0,2],\\
\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{2}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2],\\
\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{2}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{2}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{2}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2].\end{split}

Now, the
\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}
through
N_{2}
leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{2}(t)&\geq\Phi_{M}(t),\,\,v_{2}(t)\geq\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 85.35826ptt\in[0,2],\\
u_{2}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{2}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2],\\
v_{2}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{2}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{2}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{2}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2],\end{split}

which shows that
u_{2}>0
and
v_{2}>0
on
(0,2]
,
u_{2}^{\prime}>0
and
v_{2}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,2)
. Note that,
u_{2}=u_{1}
and
v_{2}=v_{1}
on
[0,1]
as

N_{2}\subseteq N_{1}
.

In general, for each
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, there exists a
subsequence
N_{k}
of
N_{k-1}\setminus\{k-1\}
and

(u_{k},v_{k})\in C^{1}[0,k]\times C^{1}[0,k]
such that

(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(u_{k}^{(j)},v_{k}^{(j)})

(j=0,1)
on
[0,k]
, as

m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{k}
. Also,
u_{k}(0)=v_{k}(0)=0
,

u_{k}=u_{k-1}
and
v_{k}=v_{k-1}
on
[0,k-1]
as
N_{k}\subseteq N_{k-1}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{k}(t)&\geq\Phi_{M}(t),\,\,v_{k}(t)\geq\Psi_{M}(t),\hskip 85.35826ptt\in[0,k],\\
u_{k}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{k}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,k],\\
v_{k}^{\prime}(t)&\geq\int_{t}^{k}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)(\Phi_{M}(s))^{\gamma_{1}}(\Psi_{M}(s))^{\delta_{1}}ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,k],\end{split}

which shows that
u_{k}>0
and
v_{k}>0
on
(0,k]
,
u_{k}^{\prime}>0
and
v_{k}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,k)
.

Define functions
x,y:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
as:

For fixed
\tau\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
and
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
with

\tau\leq k
,
x(\tau)=u_{k}(\tau)
and
y(\tau)=v_{k}(\tau)
. Then,

x
and
y
are well defined as,
x(t)=u_{k}(t)>0
and

y(t)=v_{k}(t)>0
for
t\in(0,k]
. We can do this for each

\tau\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
. Thus,
(x,y)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\times C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
with
x>0
and
y>0
on
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
,
x^{\prime}>0
and

y^{\prime}>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Now, we show that
(x,y)
is a solution of system of BVPs (
206
). Choose a fixed
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
such that
k\geq\tau
. Then,

(\overline{x}_{m}(\tau),\overline{y}_{m}(\tau))
where
m\in N_{k}
, satisfy

\displaystyle\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,\overline{x}_{m}(s),\overline{y}_{m}(s),\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds,

\displaystyle\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,\overline{x}_{m}(s),\overline{y}_{m}(s),\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Passing to the limit
m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle u_{k}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=u_{k}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,u_{k}(s),v_{k}(s),u_{k}^{\prime}(s))ds,

\displaystyle v_{k}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=v_{k}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,u_{k}(s),v_{k}(s),v_{k}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Hence,

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x(s),y(s),x^{\prime}(s))ds,

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,x(s),y(s),y^{\prime}(s))ds,

which implies that

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=p_{1}(\tau)f_{1}(\tau,x(\tau),y(\tau),x^{\prime}(\tau)),

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=p_{2}(\tau)f_{2}(\tau,x(\tau),y(\tau),y^{\prime}(\tau)).

We can do this for each
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+},

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}.

Thus,
(x,y)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})\times C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})
,

x(0)=y(0)=0
.

It remains to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\prime}(t)=0.

First, we show that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0
. Suppose

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=\varepsilon_{0}
, for some

\varepsilon_{0}>0
. Then,
x^{\prime}(t)\geq\varepsilon_{0}
for all

t\in[0,\infty)
. Choose
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, then for
m\in N_{k}
, in view of (
233
), we have

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)=u_{k}^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}x_{m}^{\prime}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,k],

which leads to

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(k)=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}x_{m}^{\prime}(k).

Thus for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
m^{*}\in N_{k}
such
that
|x_{m}^{\prime}(k)-x^{\prime}(k)|<\varepsilon
for all
m\geq m^{*}
. Without
loss of generality assume that
m^{*}=k
, then

|x_{k}^{\prime}(k)-x^{\prime}(k)|<\varepsilon
, that is,
|x^{\prime}(k)|<\varepsilon
.
Which is a contradiction whenever
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
.
Hence,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0
. Similarly, we can prove

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\prime}(t)=0
. Thus,
(x,y)
is a

C^{1}
-positive solution of system of BVPs (
206
).

Example 11.3

Let

\displaystyle f_{i}(t,x,y,z)=\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}e^{-t}(M+1-x)(M+1-y)|x|^{\gamma_{i}}|y|^{\delta_{i}}|z|^{-\alpha_{i}},\,i=1,2,

where
\nu>0
,
M>0
,
\alpha_{i}>0
,

0\leq\gamma_{i},\delta_{i}<1
,
i=1,2
.

Assume that
(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})\neq\delta_{1}\delta_{2}

and

\displaystyle\nu<\frac{M}{\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\alpha_{i}+2)(\alpha_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}(2M+1)^{\frac{2}{\alpha_{i}+1}}M^{\frac{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}{\alpha_{i}+1}}}.

Taking
p_{i}(t)=e^{-t}
,

h_{i}(x)=\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}(M+1+x)x^{\gamma_{i}}
,

k_{i}(y)=(M+1+y)y^{\delta_{i}}
,
u_{i}(z)=z^{-\alpha_{i}}
and

v_{i}(z)=0
,
i=1,2
. Choose

\varphi_{M}(t)=\nu^{\alpha_{1}+1}M^{-\alpha_{1}}e^{-t}
and

\psi_{M}(t)=\nu^{\alpha_{2}+1}M^{-\alpha_{2}}e^{-t}
. Then,

J_{i}(\mu)=\frac{\mu^{\alpha_{i}+1}}{\alpha_{i}+1}
and

J_{i}^{-1}(\mu)=(\alpha_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}\mu^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}
,

i=1,2
.

Also,

\small{\begin{split}\frac{M}{\omega(M)}&=\frac{M}{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{i}^{-1}\big(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds\big)dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}J_{i}^{-1}\big(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds\big)}\\
&=\frac{M}{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{i}^{-1}\big(\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}(2M+1)^{2}M^{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}e^{-t}\big)dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}J_{i}^{-1}\big(\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}(2M+1)^{2}M^{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}\big)}\\
&=\frac{M}{\nu\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\alpha_{i}+2)(\alpha_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}(2M+1)^{\frac{2}{\alpha_{i}+1}}M^{\frac{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}{\alpha_{i}+1}}}>1.\end{split}}

Clearly,
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{7})
are satisfied.
Hence, by Theorem
11.2
, the system of BVPs (
206
) has at least one
C^{1}
-positive solution.

12
Systems of BVPs on infinite intervals with more general
BCs

We say,
(x,y)\in(C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\cap C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}))\times(C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\cap C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}))
is a

C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs (
207
), if
(x,y)
satisfies (
207
),

x>0
,
y>0
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Assume that

(\mathbf{C}_{8})

there exist a constant
M>0
such that

\frac{M}{\omega(M)}>1
, where

\omega(M)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)
,

\displaystyle\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}[J_{i}^{-1}\big(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds+J_{i}(\varepsilon)\big)]dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}\Big)J_{i}^{-1}\big(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds+J_{i}(\varepsilon)\big),

\displaystyle J_{i}(\mu)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\mu}\frac{dz}{u_{i}(z)+v_{i}(z)},\text{ for
}\mu>0,\,i=1,2.

12.1
Existence of positive solutions on finite intervals

Choose

m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, where
N_{0}:=\{0,1,\cdots\}
, and
consider the system of BVPs on finite interval

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
&a_{1}x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=x^{\prime}(m)=0,\\
&a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=y^{\prime}(m)=0.\end{split}

(238)

First we
show that the system of BVPs (
238
) has a
C^{1}
-positive
solution. We say,
(x,y)\in(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))\times(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))
, a
C^{1}
-positive
solution of the system of BVPs (
238
), if
(x,y)
satisfies
(
238
),
x>0
and
y>0
on
[0,m]
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on

[0,m)
.

Theorem 12.1

Assume that
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{3})
and
(\mathbf{C}_{5})-(\mathbf{C}_{8})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
238
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Proof

In view of
(\mathbf{C}_{8})
, we choose
\varepsilon>0
small
enough such that

\frac{M}{\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)}>1.

(239)

Choose
n_{0}\in\{1,2,\cdots\}
such that

\frac{1}{n_{0}}<\varepsilon
. For each
n\in N:=\{n_{0},n_{0}+1,\cdots\}
, define retractions

\theta:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,M]
and
\rho:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[\frac{1}{n},M]

as

\displaystyle\theta(x)=\max\{0,\min\{x,M\}\}\text{ and
}\rho(x)=\max\{\frac{1}{n},\min\{x,M\}\}.

Consider the
modified system of BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}^{*}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}^{*}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
&a_{1}x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(m)=\frac{1}{n},\\
&a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,y^{\prime}(m)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(240)

where

f_{1}^{*}(t,x,y,x^{\prime})=f_{1}(t,\theta(x),\theta(y),\rho(x^{\prime}))
and

f_{2}^{*}(t,x,y,y^{\prime})=f_{2}(t,\theta(x),\theta(y),\rho(y^{\prime}))
.
Clearly,
f_{i}^{*}\,(i=1,2)
are continuous and bounded on

[0,m]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Hence, by Theorem
1.7
, the modified
system of BVPs (
240
) has a solution

(x_{m,n},y_{m,n})\in(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))\times(C^{1}[0,m]\cap C^{2}(0,m))
.

Using (
240
),
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{C}_{6})
,
we obtain

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0\text{ and }y_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0\text{ on }\in(0,m).

Integrating from
t
to
m
and using the BCs (
240
), we
obtain

x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ and
}y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\frac{1}{n}\text{ for }t\in[0,m].

(241)

Integrating (
241
) from
0
to
t
, using the BCs (
240
)
and (
241
), we have

x_{m,n}(t)\geq(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}\text{ and
}y_{m,n}(t)\geq(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}\text{ for
}t\in[0,m].

(242)

From (
241
) and (
242
), it follows that

\displaystyle\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}=x_{m,n}(m)\text{ and
}\|y_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}=y_{m,n}(m).

Now, we show that the following hold

\|x_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M\text{ and }\|y_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M.

(243)

Suppose
x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})\geq M
for some
t_{1}\in[0,m]
. Using
(
240
) and
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, we have

\displaystyle-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p_{1}(t)h_{1}(\theta(x_{m,n}(t)))k_{1}(\theta(y_{m,n}(t)))(u_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))),\hskip 2.84544ptt\in(0,m),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))+v_{1}(\rho(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)))}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).

Integrating from
t_{1}
to
m
, using the BCs (
240
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(\rho(z))+v_{1}(\rho(z))}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t_{1}}^{m}p_{1}(t)dt,

which can also be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{M}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}+\int_{M}^{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(M)+v_{1}(M)}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(t)dt.

Using
the increasing property of
J_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{1}(M)+\frac{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-M}{u_{1}(M)+v_{1}(M)}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(t)dt+J_{1}(\varepsilon),

and the
increasing property of
J_{1}^{-1}
yields

\displaystyle M\leq J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(t)dt+J_{1}(\varepsilon))\leq\omega_{\varepsilon}(M)

a contradiction to (
239
). Hence,
\|x_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Similarly, we can show that
\|y_{m,n}^{\prime}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Now, we show that

\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M\text{ and }\|y_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M.

(244)

Suppose
\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}\geq M
. From (
240
),
(
241
), (
243
) and
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, it follows that

\displaystyle-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq p_{1}(t)h_{1}(\theta(x_{m,n}(t)))k_{1}(\theta(y_{m,n}(t)))(u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).

Integrating from
t
to
m
, using the BCs (
240
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)}\frac{dz}{u_{1}(z)+v_{1}(z)}\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

which can also be written as

\displaystyle J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))-J_{1}(\frac{1}{n})\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].

The increasing property of
J_{1}
and
J_{1}^{-1}
, leads to

x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\leq J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

(245)

Now, integrating from
0
to

m
, using the BCs (
240
) and (
245
), we obtain

\displaystyle M\leq\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}\leq\int_{0}^{m}

\displaystyle[J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon))]dt

\displaystyle+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}

\displaystyle J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon)),

which implies that

\displaystyle M\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}[J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon))]dt

\displaystyle+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}J_{1}^{-1}(h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{1}(s)ds+J_{1}(\varepsilon))\leq\omega_{\varepsilon}(M),

a contradiction to (
239
). Therefore,

\|x_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Similarly, we can show that
\|y_{m,n}\|_{{}_{7,m}}<M
.

Hence, in view of (
240
)-(
244
),
(x_{m,n},y_{m,n})
is
a solution of the following coupled system of BVPs

\begin{split}-&x^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-&y^{\prime\prime}(t)=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
&a_{1}x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=0,\,x^{\prime}(m)=\frac{1}{n},\\
&a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0,\,y^{\prime}(m)=\frac{1}{n},\end{split}

(246)

satisfying

\begin{split}(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}&\leq x_{m,n}(t)<M,\,\frac{1}{n}\leq x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],\\
(t+\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}})\frac{1}{n}&\leq y_{m,n}(t)<M,\,\frac{1}{n}\leq y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].\end{split}

(247)

Now, we show that

\{x_{m,n}^{\prime}\}_{n\in N}\text{ and }\{y_{m,n}^{\prime}\}_{n\in N}\text{ are
equicontinuous on }[0,m].

(248)

From (
246
), (
247
) and
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, it
follows that

\displaystyle-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq

\displaystyle p_{1}(t)h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)(u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

\displaystyle-y_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq

\displaystyle p_{2}(t)h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)(u_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-x_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{m,n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)p_{2}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).

Thus for
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,m]
, we have

\begin{split}|J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{1}(t)dt\right|,\\
|J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{2}(t)dt\right|.\end{split}

(249)

In view of (
249
),
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
, uniform continuity
of
J_{i}^{-1}
over
[0,J_{i}(M)]

(i=1,2)
and

\displaystyle|x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

\displaystyle|y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})-y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

we obtain (
248
).

From (
247
) and (
248
), it follows that the sequences

\{(x_{m,n}^{(j)},y_{m,n}^{(j)})\}_{n\in N}

(j=0,1)
are uniformly
bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,m]
. Hence, by Theorem
(
1.5
), there exist subsequence
N_{*}
of
N
and

(x_{m},y_{m})\in C^{1}[0,m]\times C^{1}[0,m]
such that for each

j=0,1
the sequence
(x_{m,n}^{(j)},y_{m,n}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(x_{m}^{(j)},y_{m}^{(j)})
on
[0,m]
as

n\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{*}
. From the BCs (
246
), we
have

a_{1}x_{m}(0)-b_{1}x_{m}^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}y_{m}(0)-b_{2}y_{m}^{\prime}(0)=x_{m}^{\prime}(m)=y_{m}^{\prime}(m)=0
.
Next, we show that
x_{m}>0
and
y_{m}>0
on
[0,m]
,
x_{m}^{\prime}>0

and
y_{m}^{\prime}>0
on
[0,m)
.

We claim that

x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

(250)

y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{m}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

(251)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{13}=\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1-\gamma_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}},\\
&C_{14}=\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1-\gamma_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})-\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}}.\end{split}

To prove (
250
), consider the following relation

\begin{split}x_{m,n}(t)&=(t+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}(a_{1}s+b_{1})p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{1}{a_{1}}\int_{t}^{m}(a_{1}t+b_{1})p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],\end{split}

(252)

which implies that

\displaystyle x_{m,n}(0)=\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\frac{1}{n}+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{m}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using
(\mathbf{C}_{7})
and (
247
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}x_{m,n}(0)\geq&(x_{m,n}(0))^{\gamma_{1}}(y_{m,n}(0))^{\delta_{1}}\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{m}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\\
\geq&(x_{m,n}(0))^{\gamma_{1}}(y_{m,n}(0))^{\delta_{1}}\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\end{split}

which implies that

x_{m,n}(0)\geq(y_{m,n}(0))^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{1-\gamma_{1}}}\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{1}}}.

(253)

Similarly,

y_{m,n}(0)\geq(x_{m,n}(0))^{\frac{\delta_{2}}{1-\gamma_{2}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{2}}}.

(254)

Now, using (
254
) in (
253
), we have

\displaystyle(x_{m,n}(0))^{1-\frac{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})}}\geq\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{1}}}\left(\frac{b_{2}}{a_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds\right)^{\frac{\delta_{1}}{(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})}}.

Hence,

x_{m,n}(0)\geq C_{13}.

(255)

Similarly, using (
253
) in (
254
), we obtain

y_{m,n}(0)\geq C_{14}.

(256)

Now, from (
252
), it follows that

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)\geq\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Using
(\mathbf{C}_{6})
, (
247
), (
255
) and
(
256
), we obtain (
250
).

Similarly, we can prove (
251
).

From (
250
) and (
251
), passing to the limit

n\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{*}
, we obtain

\begin{split}x_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{m}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],\\
y_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{m}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].\end{split}

(257)

Consequently,
x_{m}^{\prime}>0,\,y_{m}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,m)
and
x_{m}>0,\,y_{m}>0
on
[0,m]
.

Moreover,
x_{m,n},y_{m,n}
satisfy

\displaystyle x_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{m,n}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),x_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

\displaystyle y_{m,n}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{m,n}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,x_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}(s),y_{m,n}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m].

Letting
n\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{*}
, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{m}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=x_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x_{m}(s),y_{m}(s),x_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

\displaystyle y_{m}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=y_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,x_{m}(s),y_{m}(s),y_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,m],

which imply that

\begin{split}-x_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x_{m}(t),y_{m}(t),x_{m}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m),\\
-y_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)&=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x_{m}(t),y_{m}(t),y_{m}^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,m).\end{split}

(258)

Hence,
(x_{m},y_{m})
is a
C^{1}
-positive solution of (
8.1
).

12.2
Existence of positive solutions on an infinite interval

Theorem 12.2

Assume that
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{3})
and
(\mathbf{C}_{5})-(\mathbf{C}_{8})
hold. Then the system of BVPs (
207
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Proof

By Theorem
12.1
, for each
m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, the
system of BVPs (
238
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution

(x_{m},y_{m})
defined on
[0,m]
. By applying diagonalization
argument we will show that the system of BVPs (
207
) has a
C^{1}
-positive solution. For this we define a
continuous extension
(\overline{x}_{m},\overline{y}_{m})
of

(x_{m},y_{m})
by

\overline{x}_{m}(t)=\begin{cases}x_{m}(t),\,&t\in[0,m],\\
x_{m}(m),\,&t\in[m,\infty),\end{cases}\hskip 14.22636pt\overline{y}_{m}(t)=\begin{cases}y_{m}(t),\,&t\in[0,m],\\
y_{m}(m),\,&t\in[m,\infty).\end{cases}

(259)

Clearly,
\overline{x}_{m},\overline{y}_{m}\in C^{1}[0,\infty)
and
satisfy,

\begin{split}0&\leq\overline{x}_{m}(t)<M,\,0\leq\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,\infty),\\
0&\leq\overline{y}_{m}(t)<M,\,0\leq\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t)<M,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,\infty).\end{split}

(260)

We claim that

\{\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}}\text{ and
}\{\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}}\text{ are
equicontinuous on }[0,1].

(261)

Using (
258
), (
259
), (
260
) and

(\mathbf{C}_{3})
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p_{1}(t)h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)(u_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle-\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq p_{2}(t)h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)(u_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{-\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)p_{1}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1),

\displaystyle\frac{-\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{u_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))+v_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)p_{2}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in(0,1).

Hence, for
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
, we have

\begin{split}|J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{1}(M)k_{1}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{1}(t)dt\right|,\\
|J_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1}))-J_{2}(\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2}))|&\leq h_{2}(M)k_{2}(M)\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}p_{2}(t)dt\right|.\end{split}

(262)

In view of (
262
),
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
, uniform continuity
of
J_{i}^{-1}
over
[0,J_{i}(L)]

(i=1,2)
, and

\displaystyle|\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})-\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{1}^{-1}(J_{1}(\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

\displaystyle|\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})-\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m}^{\prime}(t_{1})))-J_{2}^{-1}(J_{2}(y_{m}^{\prime}(t_{2})))|,

we establish (
261
).

From (
260
) and (
261
), it follows that the sequences

\{(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})\}\,(j=0,1)
are
uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,1]
. Hence, by Theorem
(
1.5
), there exist subsequence
N_{1}
of

N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
and
(u_{1},v_{1})\in C^{1}[0,1]\times C^{1}[0,1]
such that for each
j=0,1
, the sequence

(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(u_{1}^{(j)},v_{1}^{(j)})
on
[0,1]
as

m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{1}
. Also from BCs (
238
), we
have
a_{1}u_{1}(0)-b_{1}u_{1}^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}v_{1}(0)-b_{2}v_{1}^{\prime}(0)=0
.

Moreover, from (
257
) and (
259
), for each
m\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

as limit
m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{1}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{1}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\\
v_{1}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{1}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,1],\end{split}

which shows that
u_{1}^{\prime}>0
and
v_{1}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,1)
,
u_{1}>0
and
v_{1}>0
on
[0,1]
.

By the same process as above, we can show that

\{\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{1}\setminus\{1\}}\text{ and
}\{\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}_{m\in N_{1}\setminus\{1\}}\text{ are
equicontinuous families on }[0,2].

(263)

Now, in view of (
260
) and (
263
), it follows that
the sequences
\{(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})\}

(j=0,1)
are uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on
[0,2]
.
Hence, by Theorem (
1.5
), there exist subsequence
N_{2}
of

N_{1}\setminus\{1\}
and
(u_{2},v_{2})\in C^{1}[0,2]\times C^{1}[0,2]
such that for each
j=0,1
, the sequence

(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(u_{2}^{(j)},v_{2}^{(j)})
on
[0,2]
as

m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{2}
. Also,

a_{1}u_{2}(0)-b_{1}u_{2}^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}v_{2}(0)-b_{2}v_{2}^{\prime}(0)=0
.
Moreover, in view of (
257
) and (
259
), for each
m\in N_{1}\setminus\{1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{2}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2],\\
\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{2}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2].\end{split}

Now, the
\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}
through
N_{2}
leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{2}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{2}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2],\\
v_{1}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{2}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,2],\end{split}

which shows that
u_{2}^{\prime}>0
and
v_{2}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,2)
,
u_{2}>0
and
v_{2}>0
on
[0,2]
. Note that,

u_{2}=u_{1}
and
v_{2}=v_{1}
on
[0,1]
as
N_{2}\subseteq N_{1}
.

In general, for each
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, there exists a
subsequence
N_{k}
of
N_{k-1}\setminus\{k-1\}
and

(u_{k},v_{k})\in C^{1}[0,k]\times C^{1}[0,k]
such that

(\overline{x}_{m}^{(j)},\overline{y}_{m}^{(j)})
converges
uniformly to
(u_{k}^{(j)},v_{k}^{(j)})

(j=0,1)
on
[0,k]
, as

m\rightarrow\infty
through
N_{k}
. Also,

a_{1}u_{k}(0)-b_{1}u_{k}^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}v_{k}(0)-b_{2}v_{k}^{\prime}(0)=0
,

u_{k}=u_{k-1}
and
v_{k}=v_{k-1}
on
[0,k-1]
as
N_{k}\subseteq N_{k-1}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{k}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{1}}C_{14}^{\delta_{1}}\int_{t}^{k}p_{1}(s)\varphi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,k],\\
v_{k}^{\prime}(t)&\geq C_{13}^{\gamma_{2}}C_{14}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{t}^{k}p_{2}(s)\psi_{M}(s)ds,\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,k],\end{split}

which shows that
u_{k}^{\prime}>0
and
v_{k}^{\prime}>0
on

[0,k)
,
u_{k}>0
and
v_{k}>0
on
[0,k]
.

Define functions
x,y:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
as:

For fixed
\tau\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
and
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
with

\tau\leq k
,
x(\tau)=u_{k}(\tau)
and
y(\tau)=v_{k}(\tau)
. Then,

x
and
y
are well defined as,
x(t)=u_{k}(t)>0
and

y(t)=v_{k}(t)>0
for
t\in[0,k]
. We can do this for each

\tau\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
. Thus,
(x,y)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\times C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
with
x>0
,
y>0
,
x^{\prime}>0
and
y^{\prime}>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Now, we show that
(x,y)
is a solution of system of BVPs (
207
). Choose a fixed
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
such that
k\geq\tau
. Then,

(\overline{x}_{m}(\tau),\overline{y}_{m}(\tau))
where
m\in N_{k}
, satisfy

\displaystyle\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,\overline{x}_{m}(s),\overline{y}_{m}(s),\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds,

\displaystyle\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,\overline{x}_{m}(s),\overline{y}_{m}(s),\overline{y}_{m}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Passing to the limit
m\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle u_{k}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=u_{k}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,u_{k}(s),v_{k}(s),u_{k}^{\prime}(s))ds,

\displaystyle v_{k}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=v_{k}^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,u_{k}(s),v_{k}(s),v_{k}^{\prime}(s))ds.

Hence,

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{1}(s)f_{1}(s,x(s),y(s),x^{\prime}(s))ds,

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}(0)-\int_{0}^{\tau}p_{2}(s)f_{2}(s,x(s),y(s),y^{\prime}(s))ds,

which implies that

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=p_{1}(\tau)f_{1}(\tau,x(\tau),y(\tau),x^{\prime}(\tau)),

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle=p_{2}(\tau)f_{2}(\tau,x(\tau),y(\tau),y^{\prime}(\tau)).

We can do this for each
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle-x^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=p_{1}(t)f_{1}(t,x(t),y(t),x^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+},

\displaystyle-y^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=p_{2}(t)f_{2}(t,x(t),y(t),y^{\prime}(t)),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}.

Thus,
(x,y)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})\times C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})
,

a_{1}x(0)-b_{1}x^{\prime}(0)=a_{2}y(0)-b_{2}y^{\prime}(0)=0
.

It remains to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\prime}(t)=0.

First, we show that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0
. Suppose

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=\varepsilon_{0}
, for some

\varepsilon_{0}>0
. Then,
x^{\prime}(t)\geq\varepsilon_{0}
for all

t\in[0,\infty)
. Choose
k\in N_{0}\setminus\{0\}
, then for
m\in N_{k}
, in view of (
259
), we have

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(t)=u_{k}^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\overline{x}_{m}^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}x_{m}^{\prime}(t),\hskip 11.38092ptt\in[0,k],

which leads to

\displaystyle x^{\prime}(k)=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}x_{m}^{\prime}(k).

Thus for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
m^{*}\in N_{k}
such
that
|x_{m}^{\prime}(k)-x^{\prime}(k)|<\varepsilon
for all
m\geq m^{*}
. Without
loss of generality assume that
m^{*}=k
, then

|x_{k}^{\prime}(k)-x^{\prime}(k)|<\varepsilon
, that is,
|x^{\prime}(k)|<\varepsilon
.
Which is a contradiction whenever
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
.
Hence,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}x^{\prime}(t)=0
. Similarly, we can prove

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\prime}(t)=0
. Thus,
(x,y)
is a

C^{1}
-positive solution of the system of BVPs (
207
).

Example 12.3

Let

\displaystyle f_{i}(t,x,y,z)=\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}e^{-t}(M+1-x)(M+1-y)|x|^{\gamma_{i}}|y|^{\delta_{i}}|z|^{-\alpha_{i}},\,i=1,2,

where
\nu>0
,
M>0
,
\alpha_{i}>0
,

0\leq\gamma_{i},\delta_{i}<1
,
i=1,2
.

Assume that
(1-\gamma_{1})(1-\gamma_{2})\neq\delta_{1}\delta_{2}

and

\displaystyle\nu<\frac{M}{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big(\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}+\alpha_{i}+2\Big)(\alpha_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}(2M+1)^{\frac{2}{\alpha_{i}+1}}M^{\frac{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}{\alpha_{i}+1}}}.

Taking
p_{i}(t)=e^{-t}
,

h_{i}(x)=\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}(M+1+x)x^{\gamma_{i}}
,

k_{i}(y)=(M+1+y)y^{\delta_{i}}
,
u_{i}(z)=z^{-\alpha_{i}}
and

v_{i}(z)=0
,
i=1,2
. Choose

\varphi_{M}(t)=\nu^{\alpha_{1}+1}M^{-\alpha_{1}}e^{-t}
and

\psi_{M}(t)=\nu^{\alpha_{2}+1}M^{-\alpha_{2}}e^{-t}
. Then,

J_{i}(\mu)=\frac{\mu^{\alpha_{i}+1}}{\alpha_{i}+1}
and

J_{i}^{-1}(\mu)=(\alpha_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}\mu^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}
,

i=1,2
.

Also,

\small{\begin{split}&\frac{M}{\omega(M)}=\frac{M}{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{i}^{-1}(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{t}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds)dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}(1+\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}})J_{i}^{-1}(h_{i}(M)k_{i}(M)\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{i}(s)ds)}\\
&=\frac{M}{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{i}^{-1}(\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}(2M+1)^{2}M^{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}e^{-t})dt+\sum_{i=1}^{2}(1+\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}})J_{i}^{-1}(\nu^{\alpha_{i}+1}(2M+1)^{2}M^{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}})}\\
&=\frac{M}{\nu\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}+\alpha_{i}+2)(\alpha_{i}+1)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}}(2M+1)^{\frac{2}{\alpha_{i}+1}}M^{\frac{\gamma_{i}+\delta_{i}}{\alpha_{i}+1}}}>1.\end{split}}

Clearly,
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{3})
and

(\mathbf{C}_{5})-(\mathbf{C}_{8})
are satisfied. Hence, by
Theorem
12.2
, the system of BVPs (
207
)
has at least one
C^{1}
-positive solution.

Chapter 5
Concluding Remarks

In Chapter
2
, Section
2
, we have established four different results (Theorem
2.4
, Theorem
2.6
, Theorem
2.8
and Theorem
2.10
) for the existence of at least one positive solutions to the system of SBVPs (
2
) under
the new assumption on the nonlinearities
f
and
g
. In
Theorem
2.4
, we provide the existence of at least one
positive solution for the system of SBVPs (
2
) under the assumptions
(\mathbf{A}_{1})-(\mathbf{A}_{3})
, where

(\mathbf{A}_{1})
is about integrability of nonlinearities while

(\mathbf{A}_{2})
and
(\mathbf{A}_{3})
are natural assumptions
satisfied by a class of singular nonlinearities. Our next result, Theorem
2.6
, is obtained by replacing
(\mathbf{A}_{3})
with
(\mathbf{A}_{4})
in Theorem
2.4
. Theorem
2.8
is obtained by replacing
(\mathbf{A}_{2})
with
(\mathbf{A}_{5})
in Theorem

2.4
. Moreover, Theorem
2.10
can be obtained
either by replacing
(\mathbf{A}_{2})
with
(\mathbf{A}_{5})
in
Theorem
2.6
or by replacing
(\mathbf{A}_{3})
with

(\mathbf{A}_{4})
in Theorem
2.8
. Further in Section
3
, Theorem
3.2
, the existence of positive solutions to SBVPs (
3
) is provided under the assumption
(\mathbf{A}_{6})-(\mathbf{A}_{8})
, where the assumption
(\mathbf{A}_{6})
is integrability condition on nonlinearities while
(\mathbf{A}_{7})
and
(\mathbf{A}_{8})
are sublinear conditions. In Section
4
, we discuss the four-point coupled system of SBVPs (
4
). In Theorem
4.8
, by employing
the Guo-Krasnosel’skii fixed point theorem for a completely
continuous map on a positive cone, it is shown that the system
(
4
) has a positive solution under the assumptions

(\mathbf{A}_{9})-(\mathbf{A}_{11})
, where
(\mathbf{A}_{9})

is integrability condition while
(\mathbf{A}_{10})
and

(\mathbf{A}_{11})
are sublinear conditions on nonlinearities
f
and
g
.

In Chapter
3
, Section
5
, we establish the
existence results for a coupled system of SBVPs (
45
). In Theorem
5.2
, we prove the existence of at
least one
C^{1}
-positive solution for the system of SBVPs
(
45
) under the assumptions
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{7})
. The assumptions
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{7})
are some integrability
conditions,
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
is necessary because, otherwise,
positive solution
(x,y)
will not satisfy the condition
x^{\prime}>0
and

y^{\prime}>0
on
[0,1)
, and therefore,
(x,y)\notin C^{2}(0,1)\cap C^{2}(0,1)
,
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
is a natural assumption when

f(t,x,y)
and
g(t,x,y)
have singularity at
y=0
,

(\mathbf{B}_{4})
is required to bound the solution, whereas

(\mathbf{B}_{5})
is necessary for invertibility of the maps
I

and
J
, and the solution is positive due to
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
.
By replacing the assumptions
(\mathbf{B}_{6})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{7})
of Theorem
5.2
with

(\mathbf{B}_{8})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{9})
, and including
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
, we obtained the existence of at least two positive solutions for the system of SBVPs (
45
), that is, Theorem

6.2
of Section
6
. The assumption
(\mathbf{B}_{10})
is required for the existence of at least two
solutions. By replacing the assumption
(\mathbf{B}_{4})
and

(\mathbf{B}_{7})
of Theorem
5.2
with

(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{12})
we get our next
result, that is, Theorem
7.1
of Section
7
,
which provide existence of at least one
C^{1}
-positive solutions
to the SBVPs (
46
). Theorem
8.2
of
Section
8
is obtained by replacing the assumptions
(\mathbf{B}_{4})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{9})
of Theorem

6.2
with
(\mathbf{B}_{11})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{13})
,
which is a criteria for the existence of at least two

C^{1}
-positive solutions for the system of SBVPs (
46
). Moreover in Section
9
, Theorem

9.1
, we studied the existence of
C^{1}
-positive solutions
to the system of SBVPs (
49
) under the assumptions
(\mathbf{B}_{1})-(\mathbf{B}_{3})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{5})
,

(\mathbf{B}_{14})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{16})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{17})
.
The assumption
(\mathbf{B}_{14})
is a replacement of

(\mathbf{B}_{11})
in the case of two-point coupled BCs
(
49
),
(\mathbf{B}_{16})
is a generalization of

(\mathbf{B}_{6})
in case system of SBVPs (
49
) while

(\mathbf{B}_{17})
is just similar to
(\mathbf{B}_{7})
. In Section
10
, we develop the notion of upper and lower solutions for the system of SBVPs (
48
). Theorem
10.1
guarantees the existence of
C^{1}
-positive solutions for the system (
48
) under the assumptions
(\mathbf{B}_{18})-(\mathbf{B}_{25})
, where
(\mathbf{B}_{18})
is equivalent to
(\mathbf{B}_{1})
,
(\mathbf{B}_{19})
is just a continuity condition on nonlinearities
f_{i}\ (i=1,2)
,
(\mathbf{B}_{20})
and
(\mathbf{B}_{21})
defines upper and
lower solutions,
(\mathbf{B}_{22})
is a condition about the
concavity of solutions,
(\mathbf{B}_{23})
is a natural assumption
when the functions
f
,
g
are singular with respect to
x=0
and

y=0
,
(\mathbf{B}_{24})
is about integrability condition and

(\mathbf{B}_{25})
is required to bound the derivative of solution.

In Chapter
4
, Section
11
, we establish the existence of

C^{1}
-positive solutions to the coupled system of SBVPs (
206
). Theorem
11.2
offer
C^{1}
-positive solutions to the system of SBVPs (
206
) under the assumption
(\mathbf{C}_{1})-(\mathbf{C}_{7})
, where
(\mathbf{C}_{1})
is some integrability condition,

(\mathbf{C}_{2})
and
(\mathbf{C}_{6})
are weaker than
(\mathbf{B}_{2})
,
(\mathbf{C}_{3})
is more general than
(\mathbf{B}_{3})
when nonlinearities are sign-changing,
(\mathbf{C}_{4})
is required to bound the solution which is much simpler than
(\mathbf{B}_{4})
,
(\mathbf{C}_{5})
is just

(\mathbf{B}_{5})
, and
(\mathbf{C}_{7})
is required to prove that the solution is positive. By replacing the assumption
(\mathbf{C}_{4})
in
Theorem
11.2
with
(\mathbf{C}_{8})
we obtain Theorem

12.2
of Section
12
, which is a criteria for
the existence of at least one
C^{1}
-positive solutions to the
system of SBVPs (
207
).
